````math
    \Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}=\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2},e_{j}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2},e_{\tau(g_{1})}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,5},e_{\tau(y_{0})}\rangle+\frac{5}{2}\mu+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0});
    ``` |  |
2.  2.
    else if $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X}\setminus\{x_{1}\})`$,

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}=\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2},e_{\tau(g_{1})}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,5},e_{\tau(y_{0})}\rangle+\frac{5}{2}\mu+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0});
    ``` |  |
3.  3.
    otherwise,
    $`0\leq\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\leq\frac{5}{2}\mu+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})`$.

###### Claim A.2.

If
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at iteration $`t`$, then for an input $`\mathbf{Z}^{1,0}`$,

1.  1.
    if $`j=\tau(x_{1})`$,
    $`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}=\frac{e^{O(\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j})}}{e^{O(\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j})}+d}`$;
2.  2.
    otherwise,
    $`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}=O\big(\frac{1}{d}\big)\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{\tau(x_{1})}\Big)`$.

###### Proof.

If $`j=\tau(x_{1})`$, by
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[Claim A.1](#A1.Thmclaim1 "Claim A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 0\leq F_{4,j}^{(t)}(\mathbf{Z}^{1,0})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r\in[m]}[\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}]^{+}\leq\big(\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j}+O(\frac{\max\{\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j},\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j^{*}}\}}{|\mathcal{G}|})\big)+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})+O(m\varrho\log d)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\big(\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j}+O(\frac{\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j}}{|\mathcal{G}|})\big)+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})+O(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}d}).`$ |  |

For $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})\not=\tau(x_{1})`$,
$`F_{4,j}^{(t)}(\mathbf{Z}^{1,0})\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})+O(\frac{\max\{\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j},\Phi^{(t)}_{4,j^{*}}\}}{|\mathcal{G}|})`$;
else $`F_{4,j}^{(t)}(\mathbf{Z}^{1,0})\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})`$.
Combining them together, we complete the proof. ∎

### A.3 Gradient Lemma

Starting with the gradient computation from
[Fact A.1](#A1.Thmfact1 "Fact A.1. ‣ Notations for gradient expressions ‣ A.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"):

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,p}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{E}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{4,j,r}\big)\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{1,0}}\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k},p}\Big],`$ |  |

we first consider the gradient for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$

###### Lemma A.1.

For $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, we have

1.  1.
    for $`\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,1}`$, $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$

    1.  (a)
        if $`s=j`$,
        $`\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j}\Big]`$;
    2.  (b)
        $`s\not=j`$,
        $`\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Big]`$.
2.  2.
    for $`\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,2}`$, $`s=\tau(g)`$ for $`g\in\mathcal{G}`$

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j,g_{1}=g}`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})\not=j,g_{1}=g}\Big].`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for $`\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,p}`$ with $`p\in\{3,4\}`$,
    $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$

    1.  (a)
        $`s=j`$,
        $`\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=j}\Big]`$;
    2.  (b)
        $`s\not=j`$

        |  |  |  |  |
        |----|----|----|----|
        |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s,\tau(x_{1})=j}`$ |  |
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s,j\not\in\tau(\widehat{X})}\Big].`$ |  |
4.  4.
    for $`\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,5}`$, $`s=\tau(y)`$ for $`g\in\mathcal{Y}`$

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,5}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j,y_{0}=y}`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})\not=j,y_{0}=y}\Big].`$ |  |

Move on to $`j\not\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, we can obtain

###### Lemma A.2.

For $`j\not\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, we have

1.  1.
    for $`\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,1}`$, $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$,

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Big].
    ``` |  |
2.  2.
    for $`\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,2}`$, $`s=\tau(g)`$ for $`g\in\mathcal{G}`$,

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{g_{1}=g}\Big].
    ``` |  |
3.  3.
    for $`\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,p}`$ with $`p\in\{3,4\}`$,
    $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$,

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,p}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s}\Big].
    ``` |  |
4.  4.
    for $`\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,5}`$, $`s=\tau(y)`$ for $`g\in\mathcal{Y}`$

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,5}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{y_{0}=y}\Big].`$ |  |

### A.4 Growth of Gamma

###### Lemma A.3 (Growth).

Given $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, suppose
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at iteration $`t`$, when $`\Phi_{4,j}^{(t)}\leq 0.01\log d`$ or
$`\Gamma_{4,j}^{(t)}\leq\frac{0.01\log d}{m}`$, then it satisfies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{4,j}^{(t+1)}=\Gamma_{4,j}^{(t)}+\Theta\big(\frac{\eta}{d}\big)\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{4,j}^{(t)}).`$ |  |

###### Proof.

By
[Lemma A.1](#A1.Thmlemma1 "Lemma A.1. ‣ A.3 Gradient Lemma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j}\Big].`$ |  |

By
[Claim A.2](#A1.Thmclaim2 "Claim A.2. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
when $`\Phi_{4,j}^{(t)}\leq 0.01\log d`$,
$`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}=\frac{O(e^{0.01\log d})}{O(e^{0.01\log d})+d}\ll 1`$
when $`j=\tau(x_{1})`$; and combing with the fact that the event
$`\{\tau(x_{1})=j\}`$ happens with probability
$`\frac{1}{|\mathcal{X}|}`$, we complete the proof.∎

[Lemma A.3](#A1.Thmlemma3 "Lemma A.3 (Growth). ‣ A.4 Growth of Gamma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
combined with the growth of the tensor power method, immediately gives
the following corollary.

###### Lemma A.4.

Suppose
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations. Define threshold
$`\Lambda^{-}=\Theta(\frac{1}{m})`$. Let $`T_{1,j}`$ be the first
iteration so that $`\Gamma_{4,j}^{(t)}\geq\Lambda^{-}`$, and
$`T_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{def}}}{{=}}\Theta(\frac{d}{\eta\sigma_{0}^{q-2}})`$.
Then we have $`T_{1}\geq T_{1,j}`$ for every $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$,
i.e., for $`t\geq T_{1}`$, it satisfies
$`\Gamma_{4,j}^{(t)}\geq\Lambda^{-}`$.

###### Lemma A.5 (Upper bound).

Suppose
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, we have
$`\Phi_{4,j}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(1)`$, for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$.

###### Proof.

We only need to consider the time $`t\geq T_{1}`$. Notice that the
gradient descent update in
[Lemma A.1](#A1.Thmlemma1 "Lemma A.1. ‣ A.3 Gradient Lemma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j}\Big]`$ |  |

Therefore, for sufficiently small $`\eta`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Phi_{4,j}^{(t+1)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Phi_{4,j}^{(t)}+\sum_{r\in\mathcal{A}_{4,j}^{(t)}}\frac{\eta}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j}\Big]+O(\varrho\log d)\cdot|\mathcal{A}_{4,j}^{(t+1)}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{4,j}^{(t)}|`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\Phi_{4,j}^{(t)}+\sum_{r\in\mathcal{A}_{4,j}^{(t)}}\frac{\eta}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j}\Big]+\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}d}.`$ |  |

When there exists $`\widetilde{T}`$, s.t.,
$`\max_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})}\Phi_{4,j}^{(\widetilde{T})}>\Omega(\log^{1.5}d)`$,
by
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[Claim A.1](#A1.Thmclaim1 "Claim A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
given an input sequence $`\mathbf{Z}^{1,0}`$ with
$`\tau(x_{1})=\widetilde{j}=\arg\max_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})}\Phi_{4,j}^{(\widetilde{T})}`$,
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{4,j}^{(\widetilde{T})}(\mathbf{Z}^{1,0})\geq\sum_{r\in\mathcal{A}_{4,\widetilde{j}}^{(t)}}\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,\widetilde{j},r}\geq\bigg(1-O\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{G}|}\Big)\bigg)\Phi^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,\widetilde{j}}-\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})>\Omega(\log^{1.5}d).`$ |  |

Following the similar analysis as
[Claim A.2](#A1.Thmclaim2 "Claim A.2. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
$`F_{4,j^{\prime}}^{(\widetilde{T})}(\mathbf{Z}^{1,0})\leq O(\frac{\Phi^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,j^{\prime}}}{|\mathcal{G}|})`$
for other $`j^{\prime}\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, and
$`F_{4,j^{\prime}}^{(\widetilde{T})}(\mathbf{Z})\leq o(1)`$ for
$`j^{\prime}\notin\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, which implies
$`1-\mathbf{logit}_{4,j}^{(\widetilde{T})}=e^{-\Omega(\log^{1.5}d)}`$.
Therefore, we derive that for
$`t\in[\widetilde{T}+1,\frac{\mathsf{poly}d}{\eta})`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Phi_{4,j}^{(t)}\leq\Phi^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,j}+\widetilde{O}(\mathsf{poly}d\cdot e^{-\Omega(\log^{1.5}d)})+O(\rho\log d)\cdot m,`$ |  |

which implies $`\Phi_{4,\widetilde{j}}^{(t)}\leq O(\log^{1.5}d)`$ since
$`\varrho\ll\frac{1}{m\log d}`$. ∎

### A.5 Group and Value Correlations Are Not Large

###### Lemma A.6.

Suppose
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, then for any $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$
and $`s=\tau(g),g\in\mathcal{G}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2},e_{\tau(g)}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})+O\big(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{G}|}\big)\max\Big\{\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1},e_{j}\rangle,\min_{r^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{4,j^{*}}^{(t)}}\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{*},r^{\prime},1},e_{j^{*}}\rangle\Big\}`$ |  |

###### Proof.

By
[Lemma A.1](#A1.Thmlemma1 "Lemma A.1. ‣ A.3 Gradient Lemma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j,g_{1}=g}-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})\not=j,g_{1}=g}\Big].`$ |  |

Clearly, the positive gradient can be upper-bounded by
$`O\big(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{G}|}\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j}\rangle\big)`$.
Moreover, for the negative gradient, by
[Claim A.1](#A1.Thmclaim1 "Claim A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have a naive bound

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\big|_{\tau(x_{1})\not=j,g_{1}=g}\leq O\big(1\big)\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\big|_{\tau(x_{1})=j,g_{1}=g}.`$ |  |

When $`t\leq T_{1}`$, by
[Claim A.2](#A1.Thmclaim2 "Claim A.2. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have
$`1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mid_{j=\tau(x_{1})}\geq\Omega(1)`$ and
$`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mid_{j\not=\tau(x_{1})}\leq O(\frac{1}{d})`$,
which implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})\not=j,g_{1}=g}\Big]\leq O(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{G}|})\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j}\rangle.`$ |  |

Therefore, for $`t\leq T_{1}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2},e_{\tau(g)}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{G}|}\Big)\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1},e_{j}\rangle.`$ |  |

For $`t\geq T_{1}`$, notice that by
[Lemma A.4](#A1.Thmlemma4 "Lemma A.4. ‣ A.4 Growth of Gamma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
$`\mathcal{A}_{4,j^{\prime}}^{(t)}\not=\emptyset`$ for
$`j^{\prime}\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, thus for
$`r^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{4,j^{*}}^{(t)}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\big|_{\tau(x_{1})\not=j,g_{1}=g}\leq\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j^{*},r^{\prime}}\big)\big|_{\tau(x_{1})=j^{*},g_{1}=g}.`$ |  |

Furthermore,
$`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mid_{j\not=\tau(x_{1})}\leq O(\frac{1}{d})(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j^{*}}\mid_{j^{*}=\tau(x_{1})})`$,
which implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})\not=j,g_{1}=g}\Big]\leq O(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{G}|})\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{4,j^{*},r^{\prime},1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j^{*}}\rangle.`$ |  |

Due to the arbitrary of $`r^{\prime}`$, we can conclude that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2},e_{\tau(g)}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{G}|}\Big)\min_{r^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{4,j^{*}}^{(t)}}\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{*},r^{\prime},1},e_{j^{*}}\rangle.`$ |  |

∎

###### Lemma A.7.

Suppose
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, then for any $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$
and $`s=\tau(y),y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,5},e_{\tau(y)}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})+O\big(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Y}|}\big)\max\Big\{\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1},e_{j}\rangle,\min_{r^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{4,j^{*}}^{(t)}}\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{*},r^{\prime},1},e_{j^{*}}\rangle\Big\}.`$ |  |

###### Proof.

The proof is similar to
[Lemma A.6](#A1.Thmlemma6 "Lemma A.6. ‣ A.5 Group and Value Correlations Are Not Large ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
and we omit the details here. ∎

### A.6 Off-diagonal Correlations Are Small

###### Lemma A.8 (off-diagonal bound).

Given $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, suppose
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at all iterations $`<t`$, for $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})\not=j`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1},e_{s}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |

###### Proof.

By
[Lemma A.1](#A1.Thmlemma1 "Lemma A.1. ‣ A.3 Gradient Lemma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Big].`$ |  |

Notice that by
[Claim A.1](#A1.Thmclaim1 "Claim A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\big|_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\leq O\big(1\big)\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,s,r}\big)\big|_{\tau(x_{1})=s},`$ |  |

combined with
[Claim A.2](#A1.Thmclaim2 "Claim A.2. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
$`\mathbf{logit}_{4,j}\leq O(\frac{1}{d})(1-\mathbf{logit}_{4,s}^{(t)})`$
when $`s=\tau(x_{1})`$, thus

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Big]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[O(\frac{1}{d})(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,s})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,s,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Big]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq O(\frac{1}{d})\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{4,s,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{s}\rangle.`$ |  |

From
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,1},e_{s}\rangle\big|\leq O(\frac{1}{d})\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,s,r,1},e_{s}\rangle\big|+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})=\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).
``` |  |

∎

###### Lemma A.9.

Given $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, suppose
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at all iterations $`<t`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,p}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}),\quad\text{ for $p\in\{3,4\}$ and all $s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})$}`$ |  |

###### Proof.

When $`s=j`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,p}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j}\rangle`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=j}\Big]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\sum_{s\not=j}\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=j,\tau(x_{1})=s}\Big].`$ |  |

Therefore, we can bound the above gradient in the similar way as the
off-diagonal case, and obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,p},e_{j}\rangle\big|\leq O(\frac{1}{d})\max_{s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})}\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,s,r,1},e_{s}\rangle\big|+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).
``` |  |

When $`s\not=j`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,p}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s,\tau(x_{1})=j}-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s,j\not\in\tau(\widehat{X})}\Big].`$ |  |

Noticing that $`\{\tau(x_{0})=s,\tau(x_{1})=j\}`$ happens with
probability $`\frac{1}{|\mathcal{X}|(|\mathcal{X}|-1)}`$, thus the
positive gradient can be upper bounded by
$`O(\frac{1}{d})\cdot|\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{4,j,r,1}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{j}\rangle|`$.
Furthermore, the negative part can be upper bounded in the similar way
as previous off-diagonal negative gradient. Putting it together, we
complete the proof. ∎

### A.7 Non-target Correlations Are Negligible

###### Lemma A.10.

Suppose
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at all iterations $`<t`$, for
$`j^{\prime}\not\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for $`p\in[5]`$ and $`s\in[d]`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{\prime},r,p},e_{s}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |

###### Proof.

By
[Lemma A.2](#A1.Thmlemma2 "Lemma A.2. ‣ A.3 Gradient Lemma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
$`\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{\prime},r,p},e_{s}\rangle`$ for
$`p\in\{1,3,4\}`$ and $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$ can be bounded in the
similar way previous off-diagonal negative gradient.

We can observe that for $`j^{\prime}\not\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, all the
non-zero gradient on the different directions are negative gradient,
which implies
$`\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{\prime},r,p},e_{s}\rangle\leq\langle\mathbf{W}^{(0)}_{4,j^{\prime},r,p},e_{s}\rangle=\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})`$.
Moreover,
$`\Lambda_{4,j^{\prime},r}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})`$ is also
non-increasing.

For $`s=\tau(g),g\in\mathcal{G}`$, whenever
$`\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{\prime},r,2},e_{s}\rangle`$ reaches
$`-3\mu`$, we have
$`\varLambda_{4,j^{\prime},r}^{(t)}\big|_{g_{1}=g}\leq-3\mu+\frac{5}{2}\mu+\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\leq 0`$,
and thus
$`\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j,r,2}}\mathsf{Loss}^{1},e_{s}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{4,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(t)}_{4,j,r}\big)\mathds{1}_{g_{1}=g}\Big]=0`$,
which implies
$`\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{\prime},r,2},e_{s}\rangle\geq-3\mu`$.
Hence,
$`|\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{\prime},r,2},e_{s}\rangle|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})`$.
Following the similar argument, we can prove the result for
$`\langle\mathbf{W}^{(t)}_{4,j^{\prime},r,5},e_{s}\rangle`$ for
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$. ∎

### A.8 Convergence

###### Lemma A.11.

For
$`|\mathcal{G}|\geq|\mathcal{Y}|\geq\Omega(\frac{\log\log d}{\log\log\log d})`$,
$`\mathrm{polylog}d\geq m\geq|\mathcal{Y}|`$,
$`\varrho\ll\frac{1}{m\log d}`$ and sufficiently small
$`\eta\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}`$,
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\leq T=\frac{\mathsf{poly}d}{\eta}`$.

###### Proof.

Putting the results in
[Lemmas A.5](#A1.Thmlemma5 "Lemma A.5 (Upper bound). ‣ A.4 Growth of Gamma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[A.6](#A1.Thmlemma6 "Lemma A.6. ‣ A.5 Group and Value Correlations Are Not Large ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[A.7](#A1.Thmlemma7 "Lemma A.7. ‣ A.5 Group and Value Correlations Are Not Large ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[A.8](#A1.Thmlemma8 "Lemma A.8 (off-diagonal bound). ‣ A.6 Off-diagonal Correlations Are Small ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [A.10](#A1.Thmlemma10 "Lemma A.10. ‣ A.7 Non-target Correlations Are Negligible ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we can directly establish the results in
[Induction A.1](#A1.Thminduction1 "Induction A.1. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
∎

###### Lemma A.12 (Convergence).

For sufficiently large $`T_{1}\leq t=\frac{\mathsf{poly}d}{\eta}`$, we
have

1.  1.
    Objective convergence:
    $`\mathsf{Loss}^{1}\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}`$;
2.  2.
    Successful learning of diagonal feature:
    $`\Phi_{4,j}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\log d)`$ for any
    $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$.

###### Proof.

Assuming for some sufficiently large constant $`n>0`$,
$`\mathbb{E}[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{4,j^{*}}^{(t)})\mid{\tau(x_{1})=j^{*}}]\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{d^{n}})`$
for $`t\in(T_{1},T_{1}+\frac{d^{n+1}\log^{2}d}{\eta}]`$ then by
[Lemma A.1](#A1.Thmlemma1 "Lemma A.1. ‣ A.3 Gradient Lemma ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{4,j^{(*)}}^{(T_{1}+\frac{d^{2}\log^{2}d}{\eta})}\geq\Omega\Big(\frac{\eta}{d^{n+1}}\Big)\cdot\frac{d^{n+1}\log^{2}d}{\eta}+\Gamma_{4,j^{(*)}}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\log^{2}d),`$ |  |

which contradicts with
$`\Gamma_{4,j^{(*)}}^{(t)}\leq\Phi_{4,j^{(*)}}^{(t)}\leq O(\log^{1.5}d)=\widetilde{O}(1)`$
in the polynomial time. This implies after sufficiently large iteration
$`t`$, we must have
$`\mathbb{E}[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{4,j}^{(t)})\mid{\tau(x_{1})=j}]\leq O(\frac{1}{d^{n}})`$
for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$. Hence

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathsf{Loss}^{1}=\mathbb{E}[-\log\mathbf{logit}_{4,\tau(x_{1})}^{(t)}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})}\mathbb{E}[-\log\mathbf{logit}_{4,j}^{(t)}\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j}]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{X})}\mathbb{E}[O(1)\big(1-\mathbf{logit}_{4,j}^{(t)}\big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=j}]`$ |  | ($`\mathbf{logit}_{4,j}^{(t)}`$ is very close to $`1`$) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}\Big).`$ |  |  |

By
[Claim A.2](#A1.Thmclaim2 "Claim A.2. ‣ Proof Plan. ‣ A.2 Induction Hypothesis ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
at the time of convergence, we must have
$`\Phi_{4,j}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\log d)`$. ∎

## Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions

### B.1 Preliminaries

###### Definition B.1 (Combinations $`\phi,\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}`$).

Let $`\mathcal{G}`$, $`\mathcal{Y}`$ be defined as in
[Definition 2.2](#S2.Thmdefinition2 "Definition 2.2 (LEGO language []). ‣ 2.2 LEGO for State Tracking ‣ 2 Background ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
For any pair $`(g,y)\in\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{Y}`$, we call
$`\phi=(g,y)`$ a combination. We write $`\phi_{1}=g`$ and $`\phi_{2}=y`$
to denote the corresponding components. We further define the set of
correct combinations $`\Phi^{\star}`$ by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Phi_{j}^{\star}=\{\phi=(g,y)\mid j=\tau(\alpha(g,y))\},\ \forall j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y});\qquad\Phi=\bigcup_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\Phi_{j}^{\star}
``` |  | (24) |

The combinations $`\phi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}`$ are the ones that can be
composed to get $`\widetilde{y}=\tau^{-1}(j)\in\mathcal{Y}`$. We also
define the set of incorrect combinations $`\Phi^{\dagger}_{j}`$ for each
$`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ to be

|     |                                                             |     |      |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \Phi^{\dagger}_{j}=\{\phi\in\Phi\setminus\Phi_{j}^{\star}\}
       ```                                                          |     | (25) |

For simply transitive action
$`\alpha:\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{Y}`$, any combination
$`\phi\in\Phi^{\dagger}_{j}`$ is a *incorrect* pair of $`g`$ and $`y`$.
That is, it holds that any $`y^{\prime}\neq y\in\mathcal{Y}`$ and
$`g^{\prime}\neq g\in\mathcal{G}`$ satisfy
$`\alpha(g,y^{\prime})=\alpha(y,g^{\prime})\neq\tau^{-1}(j)`$.

For each combination, we define the following neural-features:

###### Definition B.2 (combination features $`\psi,\Psi,\Psi^{\star},\Psi^{\dagger}`$).

Given a combination $`\phi=(g,y)\in\Phi`$, token index
$`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and neuron index $`r\in[m]`$, we define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}(g)=\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,2},e_{g}\rangle,\quad V_{j,r}(y)=\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,5},e_{y}\rangle,\quad V_{j,r}(\phi):=\frac{1}{2}(V_{j,r}(g)+V_{j,r}(y))`$ |  |

and we call $`V_{j,r}(\phi)`$ the *features for combination* $`\phi`$ in
neuron $`(j,r)\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times[m]`$. We write
$`\psi=(j,r,\phi)`$ and $`V_{\psi}\equiv V_{j,r}(\phi)`$ to make the
notation concise. Similar to above, we further define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Psi:=\{\psi=(j,r,\phi)\times\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times[m]\times\Phi\}
``` |  |

and $`\Psi^{\star}`$ that contains the desirable features for each class
$`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Psi^{\star}_{j,r}=\{\psi=(j,r,\phi)\mid\phi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}\},\quad\Psi^{\star}=\bigcup_{(j,r)\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times[m]}\Psi_{j,r}`$ |  | (26) |

and $`\Psi^{\dagger}`$ that contains the incorrect feature combinations:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Psi^{\dagger}_{j,r}=\{\psi=(j,r,\phi)\mid\phi\in\Phi\setminus\Phi_{j}^{\star}\},\quad\Psi^{\dagger}=\bigcup_{(j,r)\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times[m]}\widehat{\Psi}_{j,r}`$ |  | (27) |

##### Events of combination appearance.

Let us first define some useful notations. For a combination
$`\phi=(g,y)`$, we write $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$ to denote the event when
$`\phi`$ appears in the sequence $`\mathbf{Z}^{1}`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{H}_{\phi}:=\{(g_{1},y_{0})=\phi\},\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\phi_{1}}=\mathcal{H}_{g}=\{g_{1}=g\},\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\phi_{2}}=\mathcal{H}_{y}=\{y_{0}=y\}.
``` |  |

Further, we write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi,1}=\{g_{1}\neq g,y_{0}=y\},\quad\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi,2}=\{g_{1}=g,y_{0}\neq y\},\quad\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi}=\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi,1}\cup\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi,2}
``` |  |

to denote the event where $`\phi`$ did not appear but its group element
or value is the same.

Finally we define a notion called *learning curriculum*, that sits at
the center of our proof.

###### Definition B.3 (Learning curriculum).

We define an order on the set $`\Sigma`$, defined by the following
process: Let $`\Sigma_{0}=\Psi`$. At each $`i\in[n_{y}^{2}]`$, we choose

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\psi_{i}=\arg\max_{\psi\in\Sigma_{i}}V_{\psi}^{(0)}`$ |  |

Let’s write $`\psi_{i}=(j,r,\phi)`$ where $`\phi=(g,y)`$, then we define
$`\Sigma_{i+1}`$ by excluding the following features:

1.  1.
    Exclude the confusing combinations in neuron $`(j,r)`$;

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,1}\equiv\Sigma_{i}^{\dagger,1}=\{\psi^{\prime}=(j,r,\phi^{\prime})\in\Sigma_{i},\phi^{\prime}=(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\mid(g^{\prime}=g)\textbf{ XOR }(y^{\prime}=y)\}
    ``` |  |
2.  2.
    Exclude the unselected combinations in neuron $`(j,r)`$;

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,2}\equiv\Sigma_{i}^{\dagger,2}=\{\psi^{\prime}=(j,r,\phi^{\prime})\in\Sigma_{i},\phi^{\prime}=(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\mid g^{\prime}\neq g,y^{\prime}\neq y\}
    ``` |  |
3.  3.
    Exclude the feature indices of the same combination in other neurons
    $`(j,r^{\prime}),r^{\prime}\neq r`$;

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,3}\equiv\Sigma_{i}^{\dagger,3}=\{\psi^{\prime}=(j,r^{\prime},\phi)\in\Sigma_{i},\forall r^{\prime}\neq r\in[m]\}.
    ``` |  |

Which returns
$`\Sigma_{i+1}=\Sigma_{i}\setminus\{\psi=(j,r^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\Sigma_{i}\mid\text{either }r^{\prime}=r\text{ or }\phi^{\prime}=\phi\}`$.
Iterate over the whole set $`\Psi`$, we will arive at
$`\Sigma_{n_{y}^{2}}=\varnothing`$. This gives rise to an ordered
sequence $`\Sigma^{\star}`$ and a set $`\Sigma^{\dagger}`$:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Sigma^{\star}:=(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\dots,\psi_{n_{y}^{2}});\qquad\Sigma^{\dagger}:=\bigcup\nolimits_{\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}}\Sigma^{\dagger}_{\psi},\qquad\Sigma^{\dagger}_{\psi}:=\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,1}\cup\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,2}\cup\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,3}`$ |  |

By our construction $`V_{\psi_{i}}^{(0)}\geq V_{\psi_{i+1}}^{(0)}`$.We
write $`\psi\prec_{\Sigma}\psi^{\prime}`$ to denote that $`\psi`$ is
ahead of $`\psi^{\prime}`$ in $`\Sigma^{\star}`$.

Intuitively, $`\Sigma^{\star}`$ encodes the order at which the features
$`V_{\psi}`$ grow in magnitude and $`\Sigma^{\dagger}`$ contains all the
unlearned features during the process.

Throughout the analysis of the FFN layer, we further make the following
assumptions.

###### Assumption B.1.

For $`(j,r,p)\in[d]\times[m]\times[5]`$, we fix
$`\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p}^{(t)}\equiv\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p}^{(0)}`$ for
$`p\in\{1,3,4\}`$ at initialization for simplicity of proof.

###### Assumption B.2.

We use a modified smoothed ReLU as our activation function. This
technical assumption is to avoid many pathologies appearing in the
learning dynamics.

|     |                                                       |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \mathbf{sReLU}(x):=\begin{cases}\varpi B&x\leq-B\\
       -\varpi x&x\in(-B,-\varpi]\\
       \frac{1}{2}x^{2}&x\in(-\varpi,0];\\
       x^{q}/(\varrho^{q-1}q)&x\in(0,\varrho];\\
       x-\varrho\left(1-\frac{1}{q}\right)&x\in(\varrho,B]\\
       B-\varrho\left(1-\frac{1}{q}\right)&x>B\end{cases}
       ```                                                    |     |

where $`q=O(1)`$ is a large even integer and
$`\varrho=\Theta(1/\mathrm{polylog}(d)),\varpi\in(d^{2}\mu^{q-1},\frac{\lambda}{d^{q/3}})`$,
$`B=C\log d,C=\Theta(1)\in(5,\frac{q-1}{3})`$ and
$`\lambda=\frac{d-1}{d-1+e^{B}}`$.

#### B.1.1 Theorem Statement

We will try to prove the following theorem:

###### Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions).

Suppose $`F^{(t)}`$ is returned by
Algorithm [1](#algorithm1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Learning CoT on Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
at $`t=T_{1}`$, and let
$`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu,\delta_{2}=\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}}`$, it holds
that with probability $`\geq 1-o(1)`$:

1.  1.
    For all $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, we have
    $`V_{\psi}^{(T_{1})}=B\pm O(\delta_{1})`$;
2.  2.
    For any $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\dagger,1}`$,
    $`|V_{\psi}^{(T_{1})}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\delta_{2})`$;
3.  3.
    For any $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\dagger,2}`$,
    $`V_{\psi}^{(T_{1})}\leq-B+O(\delta_{1})`$;
4.  4.
    For any $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\dagger,3}`$,
    $`V_{\psi}^{(T_{1})}\leq O(\delta_{1})`$;
5.  5.
    For $`\psi=(j,r,(g,y))\in\Sigma^{\star}`$,
    $`|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)|\leq\widetilde{O}(\delta_{2})`$.

In fact, we can decompose the statement of
[theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
into the following statement for every feature
$`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$.

###### Definition B.4 (Feature Shape).

Let $`\delta=(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ be a tuple of error parameters.
Let $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ be a feature in the learning curriculum.
We say the feature $`\psi`$ reached *feature shape*
$`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ with error $`\delta`$ if:

1.  1.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}(\delta_{1})`$:
    $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq B-O(\delta_{1})`$;
2.  2.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}(\delta_{2})`$: For any
    $`\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,1}`$, it holds that
    $`|V_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\delta_{2})`$;
3.  3.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,3}(\delta_{1})`$: For any
    $`\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,2}`$, it holds that
    $`V_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\leq-B\pm O(\delta_{1})`$;
4.  4.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,4}(\delta_{1})`$: For any feature
    $`\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,3}`$, it holds that
    $`V_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\leq O(\delta_{1})`$.
5.  5.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,5}(\delta_{2})`$: Writing $`\psi=(j,r,(g,y))`$,
    then it holds that
    $`|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)|\leq\widetilde{O}(\delta_{2})`$;

Obviously if we proved the condition $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ of
all $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ are reached for
$`\delta=(d^{c_{1}}\mu,\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$ at $`T_{1}`$, then
[theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
is proven. We do this by following a induction process sequentially for
each $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, and provide a guarantee that all
features in $`\Sigma^{\star}`$ reach the claimed convergence condition
together at $`t=T_{1}`$.

#### B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition

To prove
[theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
or equivalently, to prove the convergence condition in
[Definition B.4](#A2.Thmdefinition4 "Definition B.4 (Feature Shape). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
is reached for every feature $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, we shall
charaterize the dynamics of each feature and prove that they eventually
arrive at the desired shape.

##### Phase Decomposition.

Let us define the following timestamps for different phases of learning
each $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Psi\cup\widehat{\Psi}`$. Let
$`c_{1}=\frac{1}{1000}`$ be a small constant:

1.  1.
    Phase I: $`t\in[0,T_{\psi}^{1}]`$, where
    $`T_{\psi}^{1}:=\min\{t\geq 0\mid V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq d^{c_{1}}\mu\}`$;
2.  2.
    Phase II.1: $`t\in(T_{\psi}^{1},T_{\psi}^{2,1}]`$, where
    $`T_{\psi}^{2,1}:=\min\{t\geq 0\mid V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq\frac{1}{2}\log d\}`$;
3.  3.
    Phase II.2: $`t\in(T_{\psi}^{2,1},T_{\psi}^{2,2}]`$, where
    $`T_{\psi}^{2,2}:=\min\{t\geq 0\mid\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]\geq 1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\}`$;
4.  4.
    Phase II.3: $`t\in(T_{\psi}^{2,2},T_{\psi}^{2}]`$, where
    $`T_{\psi}^{2}=\max\{T_{\psi}^{2,3},T_{\psi}^{2,2}+\Omega(\frac{1}{\eta(d^{c_{1}}\mu)^{q-2}})\}`$
    and $`T_{\psi}^{2,3}`$ is defined as:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    T_{\psi}^{2,3}:=\min\Big\{t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\text{ holds, where }\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0},\delta_{2}=(\frac{n_{y}^{2}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}\Big\}
    ``` |  |
5.  5.
    Phase III.1: $`t\in(T^{2}_{\psi},T_{1,1}]`$, where
    $`T_{1,1}=T_{\psi_{n_{y}^{2}}}^{2}`$ and $`\psi_{n_{y}^{2}}`$ is the
    last feature in $`\Sigma^{\star}`$. This is the convergence phase
    where the rest of the features are learned and the feature converge
    to a perfect shape.
6.  6.
    Phase III.2: $`t\in(T_{1,1},T_{1}]`$, the end phase where all
    features are in perfect shape and have stablized.

Now we state the following induction hypotheses which naturally results
in
[theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").

###### Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases).

For all $`t\leq T_{1}`$, the following holds:

1.  1.
    $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}+b_{i,j,r}\geq\Omega(\mu)`$.
2.  2.
    $`T_{\psi^{\prime}}^{2}<T_{\psi}^{1}`$ if
    $`\psi^{\prime}\prec_{\Sigma}\psi`$ for
    $`\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, i.e., the intervals
    $`\{[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{\psi}^{2}]\}_{\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}}`$ are
    non-overlapping;

In order to prove
[Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we introduce the following feature based induction hypothesis to prove
that $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}`$ defined in
[Definition B.4](#A2.Thmdefinition4 "Definition B.4 (Feature Shape). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for some error parameters at $`t=T_{\psi}^{2}`$.

###### Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Sigma^{\star},\phi=(g,y)`$, at $`t\leq T_{1}`$,
the following holds:

1.  1.
    At $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$, any $`g\neq g^{\prime},y^{\prime}\neq y`$
    has
    $`V_{j,r}(g^{\prime}),V_{j,r}(y^{\prime})\leq\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$.
2.  2.
    During $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$, there are at most
    $`\widetilde{O}(\sqrt{d}/\eta)`$ iterations where
    $`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}`$ conditioned on
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$.
3.  3.
    Let $`\psi^{\prime}\prec\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, the feature shape
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi^{\prime}}(d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0},(\frac{n_{y}^{2}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$
    holds throughout $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{1}]`$;
4.  4.
    Let $`\psi^{\prime}\succ\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, then
    $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})`$ throughout
    $`t\in[0,T_{\psi}^{2}]`$.

#### B.1.3 Technical Calculations

Let us recall some facts about gradient computation for $`\mathbf{W}`$.

###### Fact B.1 (gradient computation).

The gradient with respect to $`\mathbf{W}_{i,j,r,p}`$ for $`i=5`$,
$`j\in[d]`$, $`r\in[m]`$, $`p\in[5]`$ and $`v\in\mathcal{V}`$ is

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{v}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{E}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{1,0}}\langle\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k},p},e_{v}\rangle\Big]
``` |  | (28) |

where
$`\mathcal{E}_{5,j}=\mathds{1}_{v=Z_{\mathsf{ans},1,p}}-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}`$
is the loss derivatives. We compute more precise expressions when $`j`$,
$`p`$ and $`v`$ varies:

1.  1.
    when $`p=2`$, for $`g\in\mathcal{G}`$, let
    $`\phi=(g,y)\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$ be the combination with
    $`\phi_{1}=g`$, we have for all $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y}),r\in[m]`$,

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,2}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{g}\rangle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\Big]`$ |  |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi,2}}\Big]`$ |  | (29) |
2.  2.
    when $`p=5`$, for $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, let
    $`\phi=(g,y)\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$ be the combination with
    $`\phi_{2}=y`$, we have for all $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y}),r\in[m]`$,

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,5}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{y}\rangle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\Big]`$ |  |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi,1}}\Big]`$ |  | (30) |
3.  3.
    For any combination of $`p`$, $`v`$ not included above, we have due
    to our assumptions of the distribution and update rule:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{v}\rangle\equiv 0`$ |  |

###### Definition B.5 ($`\Gamma`$-notations).

To simplify gradient expression, we define the following notation: let
$`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Psi`$, we write

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,\phi}=\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{\psi}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\Big]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,\phi}=\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{\psi}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi}}\Big]`$ |  | (31) |

we can further define $`\Gamma`$ for each $`g\in\mathcal{G}`$ and
$`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$: Let $`v\in\mathcal{G}\cup\mathcal{Y}`$, we define

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,v}=\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{\psi,v}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{v}}\Big]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,v}=\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{\psi,v}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{v}}\Big]`$ |  | (32) |

This allows us to define the following gradient condition:

###### Definition B.6 (gradient condition).

At $`t\leq T_{1}`$, letting $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Psi`$, we write
$`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ for $`\delta>0`$ to denote that the
gradient update for $`V_{\psi}`$ is smaller than $`\delta`$, formally:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{j,r,v}(\delta)\text{ holds }\implies|\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{-,(t)}|\leq\delta`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{j,r,v}^{+}(\delta)\text{ holds }\implies|\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}|\leq\delta`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{j,r,v}^{-}(\delta)\text{ holds }\implies|\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{-,(t)}|\leq\delta`$ |  |

We further write

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\psi}(\delta)\text{ holds }\implies\mathcal{C}_{j,r,g}(\delta)\wedge\mathcal{C}_{j,r,y}(\delta)\text{ holds }`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{+}(\delta)\text{ holds }\implies\mathcal{C}_{j,r,g}^{+}(\delta)\wedge\mathcal{C}_{j,r,y}^{+}(\delta)\text{ holds }`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{-}(\delta)\text{ holds }\implies\mathcal{C}_{j,r,g}^{-}(\delta)\wedge\mathcal{C}_{j,r,y}^{-}(\delta)\text{ holds }`$ |  |

Now we proceed to characterize the lower bound of logits

###### Fact B.2 (logit lower bound).

We list some basic facts about the architecture
[Definition 3.5](#S3.Thmdefinition5 "Definition 3.5 (Transformer language model). ‣ Transformer architecture. ‣ 3.2 Transformer Architecture ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
here.

1.  1.
    For any $`Z^{1}\in\mathrm{supp}(\mathcal{D}^{1})`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \lambda:=\min_{i,j,F,Z}(1-\mathbf{logit}_{i,j}(F,Z))=\frac{d-1}{d-1+e^{B}}
    ``` |  |
2.  2.
    Suppose for some $`i\in[5]`$, $`F_{i,j}=o(1)`$ for all
    $`j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, then

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \min_{i,j,F,Z}\mathbf{logit}_{i,j}(F,Z)=\frac{1}{(1+o(1))d+n_{y}e^{B}}=O(\frac{\lambda}{n_{y}d})
    ``` |  |

### B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition

We shall prove the following properties at initialization.

###### Fact B.3 (initialization range).

At $`t=0`$, the following holds with probability $`\geq 1-o(1)`$:

1.  1.
    $`|\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p}^{(0)},e_{v}\rangle|\leq O(\sigma_{0}\sqrt{\log d})`$
    For all $`j,r,p`$ and $`v\in\mathcal{V}`$;
2.  2.
    $`V_{\psi}^{(0)}\geq V_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(0)}+\gamma`$, where
    $`\gamma=\Omega(\frac{\sigma_{0}}{n_{y}^{4}m^{2}\log d})`$, for any
    pair $`\psi\prec\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ in
    Def. [B.3](#A2.Thmdefinition3 "Definition B.3 (Learning curriculum). ‣ Events of combination appearance. ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization");

###### Proof.

[Fact B.3](#A2.Thmfact3 "Fact B.3 (initialization range). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
can easily verified from our initialization
$`\mathbf{W}_{i,j,r,p}^{(0)}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{0}I_{d})`$. For
[Fact B.3](#A2.Thmfact3 "Fact B.3 (initialization range). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b,
we give a straightforward proof that every pair of
$`V_{\psi}^{(0)},\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ has a gap of
$`\frac{\sigma_{0}}{n_{y}^{4}m^{2}\log d}`$. First note that
$`V_{\psi}^{(0)}`$ of different $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ are
independent and identically distributed on the randomness of
$`\mathbf{W}^{(0)}`$, due to the orthogonality of embeddings
$`e_{v},v\in\mathcal{V}`$. Then, by the basic property of a Gaussian
variable (notice that $`V_{\psi}^{(0)}-V_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(0)}`$ is also
Gaussian with variance $`2\sigma_{0}`$) (all though different pairs
could be dependent), we have with probability
$`1-\frac{1}{n_{y}^{4}m^{2}\log d}`$ that their gap is at least
$`\gtrsim\frac{\sigma_{0}}{n_{y}^{4}m^{2}\log d}`$ for each pair. Then
by a union bound over $`O(m^{2}n_{y}^{4})`$-many all possible pairs we
can conclude the proof. ∎

We give another characterization of the initial activations.

###### Fact B.4 (activation magnitude).

At $`t=0`$, with high probability it holds that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(0)}=(1+O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log d}}))\mu=\Theta(\sigma\log d)\ll\varrho
``` |  |

and thus $`\mathbf{sReLU}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})=\Theta(\frac{1}{q}\mu^{q})`$,
$`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})=\Theta(\mu^{q-1})`$ and
$`F_{5,j}=\Theta(\frac{m}{q}\mu^{q})`$.

###### Proof.

Combining
[Definitions 3.4](#S3.Thmdefinition4 "Definition 3.4 (Smooth ReLU). ‣ 3.2 Transformer Architecture ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[3.5](#S3.Thmdefinition5 "Definition 3.5 (Transformer language model). ‣ Transformer architecture. ‣ 3.2 Transformer Architecture ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[3.3](#S3.Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3.3 (Initialization). ‣ 3.3 LEGO Task via CoT Reasoning and Training Objective ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.3](#A2.Thmfact3 "Fact B.3 (initialization range). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
gives the fact. ∎

We establish some properties in Phase I.

###### Lemma B.1 (Key properties in Phase I).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,(g,y))\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, and assume
[Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$, then

1.  1.
    $`|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(v)|\leq O(\mu/\sqrt{\log d})`$ if
    $`v\neq g,v\neq y`$;
2.  2.
    $`\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)}(\mathbf{Z})\geq 0`$ for
    $`\mathbf{Z}\in\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$;
3.  3.
    $`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}=O(1/d)`$ whenver
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}^{\dagger}`$ happens.

###### Proof.

[lemma B.1](#A2.Thmlemma1 "Lemma B.1 (Key properties in Phase I). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
is from both the initialization of weights
[Fact B.3](#A2.Thmfact3 "Fact B.3 (initialization range). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a.
[lemma B.1](#A2.Thmlemma1 "Lemma B.1 (Key properties in Phase I). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b
is from
[lemma B.1](#A2.Thmlemma1 "Lemma B.1 (Key properties in Phase I). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
and
[Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a.
[lemma B.1](#A2.Thmlemma1 "Lemma B.1 (Key properties in Phase I). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c
is due to both
[Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b
and
[Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b.
∎

#### B.2.1 Competition between feature combinations.

In this phase we compare the growth of different features using a proxy.

###### Lemma B.2 (Approximating gradient with proxy).

Assume
[Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
For a fixed $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, define the auxiliary
sequence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(0)}:=V_{\psi}^{(0)},\qquad\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t+1)}:=\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}+\eta\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\Big],
``` |  |

where in $`\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(t)}`$ the weight $`V_{\psi}`$
is replaced by $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}`$ (all other weights are
unchanged). Then for any feature $`\psi\succ\psi_{i}`$ or
$`\psi\in\Sigma_{i}\setminus\{\psi_{i}\}`$ and all
$`t\leq T^{1}_{\psi}`$,
$`|V_{\psi}^{(t)}-\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}\big|\ \leq\ O(\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}/d^{1/2-3c_{1}})`$.

###### Proof.

We need to prove the result by induction. Firstly we notice that the
newly defined proxy sequence $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ is
monotonically increasing. Moreover, as $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ is
initialized the same as $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}`$, we can use
[lemmas B.12](#A2.Thmlemma12 "Lemma B.12 (TPM in expectation). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.11](#A2.Thmlemma11 "Lemma B.11 (concentration inequality for pseudo-U-statistics). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
to argue its growth by taking

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle x_{t}=\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}+b_{i,j,r},\quad\xi=\sum_{p=3,4}\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p},e_{x_{0}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,1},e_{x_{1}}\rangle,\quad x_{t+1}\geq x_{t}+\eta\frac{1}{n_{y}^{2}}\mathbb{E}[(x_{t}+\mu)^{q-1}]`$ |  |

And
[lemmas B.12](#A2.Thmlemma12 "Lemma B.12 (TPM in expectation). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.13](#A2.Thmlemma13 "Lemma B.13 (TPM, adapted from []). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
guarantee $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq d^{c_{1}}\mu`$ after
$`\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta\mu^{q-2}})`$ steps. Now assume the bound
of difference between $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ and
$`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ holds at a $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$. Since
$`t\leq T^{1}_{\psi}`$, we have
$`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\leq d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0}`$ by definition of
$`T^{1}_{\psi}`$. By
[Fact B.1](#A2.Thmfact1 "Fact B.1 (gradient computation). ‣ B.1.3 Technical Calculations ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
the exact update of $`V_{\psi}`$ is

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{\psi}^{(t+1)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=V_{\psi}^{(t)}+\eta\Big(\mathbb{E}[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi}}]\Big).`$ |  |

Compare the above with the definition of $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}`$,
we first we notice that $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ is dominating
$`V_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ for all iterations $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$. Then, we
can bound the difference between the two sequences by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\big|\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t+1)}-V_{\psi}^{(t+1)}\big|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\big|\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}-V_{\psi}^{(t)}\big|+\eta\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{(t)}_{5,j,r})-\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\big]\Big|`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\eta\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\big]\Big|+\frac{\eta}{2}\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi}}\big]\Big|`$ |  |

We bound the three error terms for $`t\leq T^{1}_{\psi}`$:

- A.
  Activation perturbation: by the smoothness of
  $`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}`$ on $`[0,\varrho]`$ and that both
  pre-activations are in this range during Phase I, we have

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \Big|\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(t)})-\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)}))\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]\Big|\leq\,|\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}-V_{\psi}^{(t)}|\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(t)})^{q-2}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]
  ``` |  |

  where we have used the fact that
  $`\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}\geq\Lambda_{5,j,r}`$ because of the
  dominance of $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}`$.
- B.
  Outside a set of iterations
  $`\mathcal{A}_{\phi}=\{t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}\mid\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\text{ conditioned on }\mathcal{H}_{\phi}\}`$
  whose cardinality is no more than
  $`\widetilde{O}(d^{\frac{1}{2}+c_{1}}/\eta)`$ by
  [Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a,
  we have $`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\leq d^{-1/2}`$. Hence for
  $`t\notin\mathcal{A}_{\phi}`$

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \Big|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]\Big|\ \leq\ O(d^{-1/2})\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]\
  ``` |  |

  Now we can sum over the iterations to get

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\left(\sum_{s\leq t,s\in\mathcal{A}_{\phi}}+\sum_{s\leq t,s\notin\mathcal{A}_{\phi}}\right)\eta\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}^{(s)}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(s)}_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\big]\Big|`$ |  |
  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle O(\frac{d^{\frac{1}{2}+c_{1}}}{\eta})\cdot\eta(d^{c_{1}}\mu)^{q-1}+O(d^{-1/2})(\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}-\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(0)})`$ |  |
  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle O(\mu/d^{1/2-c_{1}})`$ |  |
- C.
  Confusing events: before $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq\frac{1}{2}\log d`$, we
  have $`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}=O(1/d)`$ in Phase I by
  [lemma B.1](#A2.Thmlemma1 "Lemma B.1 (Key properties in Phase I). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
  So we get

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \sum_{s\leq t}\eta\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\,|\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})|\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\phi}}]\ \leq\ O(\frac{1}{\eta\mu^{q-2}})\eta\Big(\frac{(d^{c_{1}}\mu)^{q-1}}{d}\Big)\ll O(\mu/\sqrt{d})
  ``` |  |

So we have that the accumulated errors from the last two terms are
smaller than $`O(\mu/d^{1/2-c_{1}})`$. This allow us to bound the
difference by using the more naive approach: we can assume the
difference started with $`O(\mu/d^{1/2-c_{1}})`$ and now we only need to
bound the following sequence for $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\delta_{t+1}=\delta_{t}+O(\eta\delta_{t})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(t)}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\bigg],\quad\delta_{0}\leq O(\mu/d^{1/2-c_{1}})`$ |  |

Now let $`\delta^{\prime}>\eta`$ be some small parameter, we can do some
slicing of time $`t_{0},t_{1},\dots,t_{n}`$ where
$`t_{i}=\min\{t\geq 0\mid\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq(1+\delta^{\prime})^{i}\mu\}`$
and
$`(1+\delta^{\prime})^{n}\mu\leq\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t+1)}\leq(1+\delta^{\prime})^{n+1}\mu`$.
This produce the following bound:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\log\delta_{t+1}-\log\delta_{0}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\sum_{s\in[t_{i},t_{i-1}]}\log\left(1+O(\eta)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(s)}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(s)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\bigg]\right)`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\sum_{s\in[t_{i},t_{i-1}]}O(\eta)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(s)}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(s)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\bigg]`$ |  | ($`\log(1+x)\leq x`$ when $`x>0`$) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}O\bigg(\frac{1}{\min_{s\in[t_{i},t_{i-1}],\mathbf{Z}\in\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(s)}(\mathbf{Z})}\bigg)\sum_{s\in[t_{i},t_{i-1}]}\eta\Big|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{5,j,r}^{(s)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]\Big|`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}O\bigg(\frac{1}{(1+\delta^{\prime})^{i}\mu}\bigg)(\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t_{i+1})}-\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t_{i})})`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{\prime}\log(\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}/\mu)}{\log(1+\delta^{\prime})}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle 2\log(\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}/\mu)`$ |  | ($`\log(1+\delta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{\delta^{\prime}}}<2`$ if $`\delta^{\prime}`$ is small enough) |

So when $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}\in[d^{c_{1}}\mu,2d^{c_{1}}\mu]`$,
it holds that
$`\delta_{t}\lesssim d^{2c_{1}}\delta_{0}\lesssim\mu/d^{1/2-3c_{1}}`$.
This proves the claim. ∎

###### Lemma B.3 (competition).

Assuming
[Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
and let $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, we shall have for any feature
$`\psi^{\prime}\succ\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ or
$`\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\psi}\setminus\{\psi\}`$ that
$`V_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(T_{\psi}^{1})}\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})`$.

###### Proof.

We can approximate the sequence $`V_{\psi_{i}}`$ using
$`\widetilde{V}_{\psi_{i}}^{(t)}`$ and straightforwardly use
[lemma B.12](#A2.Thmlemma12 "Lemma B.12 (TPM in expectation). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
to compare $`\psi_{i}`$ with any
$`\psi^{\prime}\succ\psi_{i}\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. In fact, let’s compared
the time they reach $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0}`$. By
[lemma B.2](#A2.Thmlemma2 "Lemma B.2 (Approximating gradient with proxy). ‣ B.2.1 Competition between feature combinations. ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we can just compare $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ before
$`t=T_{\psi}^{1}`$, now since by
[Fact B.3](#A2.Thmfact3 "Fact B.3 (initialization range). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
we know
$`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(0)}\geq\widetilde{V}_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(0)}+\gamma`$,
we can use
[lemma B.12](#A2.Thmlemma12 "Lemma B.12 (TPM in expectation). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
to get that the first time when
$`\widetilde{V}_{\psi_{i}}^{(t)}\geq d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0}`$, we have
$`\widetilde{V}_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})`$. This
combined with
[lemma B.2](#A2.Thmlemma2 "Lemma B.2 (Approximating gradient with proxy). ‣ B.2.1 Competition between feature combinations. ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
concludes the result. Moreover, we can also obtain bounds on all
$`\psi\in\Psi`$ by discussing different $`\psi`$:

- •
  For all $`\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}`$ such that there exists
  $`\psi^{\prime}=(j^{\prime},r^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\prec\psi_{i}`$,
  $`\phi^{\prime}`$ is learned. That means any $`\psi`$ contains
  $`\phi^{\prime}`$) has gradient too small because of
  [B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c
  and therefore
  $`\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{5,j}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi^{\prime}}}]\leq\widetilde{O}(\lambda|\Gamma_{\psi}^{(t)}|)`$
  and therefore wouldn’t be learned.
- •
  All the rest of $`\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}`$ has their
  corresponding $`\psi^{\prime}\succ\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ and
  therefore lost in the competition and has small growth.
- •
  All other
  $`\psi\in\Psi\setminus\cup_{i^{\prime}\leq i}\Sigma_{i^{\prime}}`$
  lost the competition as well for the same reason.

This concludes the proof. ∎

###### Proof of [Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization") and [B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization") in Phase I.

Let $`\psi_{i}\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. Assume
[Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for some $`t\leq T_{\psi_{i-1}}^{3}`$, then we shall prove that it
continues to hold for $`t\in[T_{\psi_{i-1}}^{3},T_{\psi_{i}}^{1}]`$.

- •
  [Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a:
  This is simple as all $`V_{\psi}`$ for $`\psi\succ\psi_{i}`$ has
  updates approximated by $`\widetilde{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}`$, which is
  monotonically increasing, and has minimal update by
  [lemma B.2](#A2.Thmlemma2 "Lemma B.2 (Approximating gradient with proxy). ‣ B.2.1 Competition between feature combinations. ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
- •
  [Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b:
  We proved in
  [lemma B.3](#A2.Thmlemma3 "Lemma B.3 (competition). ‣ B.2.1 Competition between feature combinations. ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  that whenever $`\psi\prec\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\star}`$,
  $`T_{\psi}^{1}\leq T_{\psi^{\prime}}^{1}`$. This does not violate
  [Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b
  so the non-overlapping is preserved.
- •
  [Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a:
  This is proven in
  [lemma B.3](#A2.Thmlemma3 "Lemma B.3 (competition). ‣ B.2.1 Competition between feature combinations. ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
- •
  [Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b-c:
  They will be proven when we analyze the end phase of feature learning.
  They are the corollaries of the end phase guarantee of the previous
  features.
- •
  [Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c:
- •
  [Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")e:
  We did not violate
````
