````math
  [Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")e
  either.
- •
  [Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-d
  will be proved in Phase III where we argue the end phase of each
  feature learned.

∎

### B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations

Below we present the induction hypothesis for phase II.

###### Induction B.3 (Phase II).

For all $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ and
$`t\in[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{\psi}^{2}]`$, the following holds

1.  1.
    For $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{\psi}^{2,1}]`$,
    $`\mathcal{E}_{5,j}^{(t)}\geq 1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}`$ conditioned on
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$;
2.  2.
    For $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{2,1}`$, for all $`j^{\prime}\neq j`$, we have
    $`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j^{\prime}}^{(t)}\leq O(\sqrt{d})\cdot\mathbf{logit}_{5,j^{\prime}}^{(T_{\psi}^{1})}`$.
3.  3.
    For any $`\phi^{\prime}=(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\neq\phi`$,
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime}),V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y^{\prime})\in(-B-O(1),\widetilde{O}(\mu))`$;
4.  4.
    For $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{2,2}`$, the number of iterations where
    $`V_{\psi}^{(t+1)}-V_{\psi}^{(t)}<0`$ is bounded by
    $`\widetilde{O}(1/d^{c_{1}}\eta)`$.

#### B.3.1 Technical Lemmas

###### Lemma B.4 (gradient estimation).

Assuming
[Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.3](#A2.Thminduction3 "Induction B.3 (Phase II). ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
let $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Sigma^{\star},\phi=(g,y)`$ and
$`t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T_{\psi}^{2}]`$, then

1.  1.
    At any $`t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,1}`$, if
    $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\in(\frac{1}{3}\log d,\frac{2}{3}\log d)`$, then
    $`\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(1)`$.
2.  2.
    Any
    $`g^{\prime}\neq g\in\mathcal{G},y^{\prime}\neq y\in\mathcal{Y}`$
    has the following gradient approximation

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,g^{\prime}}^{(t)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}}]\pm O(\varpi/n_{y})`$ |  |
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,y^{\prime}}^{(t)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g,y^{\prime})}}]\pm O(\varpi/n_{y})`$ |  |
3.  3.
    let
    $`\phi^{\prime}=(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\neq\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$
    if $`V_{j,r}(g,y)\geq B-o(1)`$ and
    $`V_{j,r}(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})<-B-2\mu`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,g^{\prime}}^{(t)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{\widetilde{y}\in\mathcal{Y},V_{j,r}(g^{\prime},\widetilde{y})\in(-B,-\varpi-\mu)}\Omega(\frac{\lambda\varpi}{dn_{y}^{3}})-\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}}\Big]`$ |  |

    and similarly for $`\Gamma_{j,r,y^{\prime}}^{(t)}`$.

###### Proof.

Firstly (a) is simple to prove, because when
$`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\in(\frac{1}{3}\log d,\frac{2}{3}\log d)`$, we have
$`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}^{\dagger}}\leq O(\frac{1}{d^{c_{1}}})`$,
which is from the calculation $`F_{5,j}\leq\frac{2}{3}\log d+O(1)`$
guaranteed by
[Inductions B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.3](#A2.Thminduction3 "Induction B.3 (Phase II). ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
For (b), we can estimate the gradient update of
$`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime})`$ where $`g^{\prime}\neq g`$. In fact

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{V_{j,r}(g^{\prime})}\mathsf{Loss}^{(t)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Gamma_{j,r,g^{\prime}}^{(t)}=\Gamma_{j,r,g^{\prime}}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,g^{\prime}}^{-,(t)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\Gamma_{j,r,g^{\prime}}^{+,(t)}-\sum_{y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})}}\Big]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}}]\pm\max\{\widetilde{O}(\mu^{q-1}),\varpi\}`$ |  |

where the last one is because for $`y^{\prime}\neq y`$, the activation
gradient $`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})`$ is either
positive and bounded by $`\widetilde{O}(\mu^{q-1})`$ or negative and
bounded by $`\varpi`$ while
$`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi^{\prime}}}\leq 1`$.
Similarly we can also get the same for $`V_{j,r}(y^{\prime})`$ where
$`y^{\prime}\neq y`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{V_{j,r}(y^{\prime})}\mathsf{Loss}^{(t)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Gamma_{j,r,y^{\prime}}^{(t)}=-\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}}]\pm O(\varpi/n_{y}^{2})`$ |  |

To prove (c), notice that when
$`V_{j,r}(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})<-B-2\mu`$, the part of
$`\Gamma_{j,r,g^{\prime}}^{+,(t)}=0`$ due to $`\Lambda_{5,j,r}`$
exceeding the boundary $`-B`$. We can group different
$`\widetilde{y}\neq y\in\mathcal{Y}`$ by their feature magnitude and
obtain the lower bound. The factor of $`\frac{\lambda}{dn_{y}}`$ is due
to the logit lower bound from
[Fact B.2](#A2.Thmfact2 "Fact B.2 (logit lower bound). ‣ B.1.3 Technical Calculations ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
∎

###### Lemma B.5 (Feature Magnitude).

Assume
[Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.3](#A2.Thminduction3 "Induction B.3 (Phase II). ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds, then for any $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$,

1.  1.
    at $`t=T_{\psi}^{2,1}`$, we have
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)=\frac{1}{4}\log d\pm o(1)`$,
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)=\frac{1}{4}\log d\pm o(1)`$;
2.  2.
    when $`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{+}(\delta)`$ hold for some
    $`\delta\leq O(\lambda/d^{c_{1}})`$ at some
    $`t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,1}`$, we have
    $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$, that is,
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$ holds.

###### Proof.

Let’s proceed the proof one by one.

- •
  Proof of (a) :This can be computed by comparing the gradient of
  $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)`$ which is
  $`\Gamma_{j,r,g}^{(t)}=\Gamma_{j,r,g}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,g}^{-,(t)}`$
  and the gradient of $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)`$ which is
  $`\Gamma_{j,r,g}^{(t)}=\Gamma_{j,r,y}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,y}^{-,(t)}`$.
  Now since

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,g}^{+,(t)}=\Gamma_{j,r,y}^{+,(t)}\geq(1-O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)+O(\mu))`$ |  |

  and that

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \Gamma_{j,r,g}^{-,(t)}=O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)+O(\mu))|),\quad\Gamma_{j,r,y}^{-,(t)}=O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)+O(\mu))|)
  ``` |  |

  one could see that
  $`\sum_{t\leq T_{\psi}^{2,1}}|\Gamma_{j,r,g}^{(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,y}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})`$
  and thus (a) holds.
- •
  Proof of (b): from
  [lemma B.4](#A2.Thmlemma4 "Lemma B.4 (gradient estimation). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
  we know that when $`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{+}(\delta)`$ holds for some
  small $`\delta=O(\frac{\lambda}{d^{c_{1}}})`$ it is not because
  $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ dropped below $`\frac{1}{2}\log d`$, thus when
  [Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  and [B.3](#A2.Thminduction3 "Induction B.3 (Phase II). ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  holds, it can only be because
  $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$, otherwise

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}=\mathbb{E}[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]\geq\lambda\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{H}_{\phi})\gg\frac{\lambda}{d^{c_{1}}}`$ |  | (if $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\leq B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$) |

  which concludes the proof.

∎

###### Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity).

Let $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, assume
[Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{\psi}^{2}]`$, and choose
$`\delta\gg n_{y}\varpi`$, then we have the following guarantees:

- (a)
  Define
  $`\mathcal{B}_{\delta}^{+}=\{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T_{\psi}^{2}]\mid\mathcal{C}^{+}_{\psi}(\delta)\text{ doesn't hold }\}`$,
  then
  $`|\mathcal{B}_{\delta}^{+}|\leq O(\frac{n_{y}\log^{2}d}{\eta\delta})`$;
- (b)
  Define
  $`\mathcal{B}_{\delta}^{-}=\{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T_{\psi}^{2}]\mid\mathcal{C}^{-}_{\psi}(\delta)\text{ doesn't hold }\}|`$,
  then
  $`|\mathcal{B}_{\delta}^{-}|\leq O(\frac{n_{y}\log^{3}d}{\eta\delta})`$;
- (c)
  Finally, at $`t=T_{\psi}^{2}`$, the feature shape
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ holds with
  $`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu`$ and
  $`\delta_{2}=(\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}`$.

###### Proof.

Let us start with (a). First by writing $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)`$, we define
the following quantity

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Upsilon^{(t)}:=V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi^{\prime})
``` |  | (33) |

which sits at the central of our proof of counter-argument.Now we have
for any $`T\geq T_{\psi}^{2,1}`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Upsilon^{(t)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=V_{j,r}^{(T)}(\phi)-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}}V_{j,r}^{(T)}(\phi^{\prime})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=V_{j,r}^{(T_{\psi}^{2,1})}(\phi)-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}}V_{j,r}^{(T_{\psi}^{2,1})}(\phi^{\prime})+\sum_{s\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},t)}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{(t)}-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}}\sum_{s\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},t)}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}`$ |  |

We can further rewrite the $`\Gamma`$s on the RHS to

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T)}\eta\Bigg(\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{(t)}-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\Bigg)`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T)}\eta\Bigg(\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{-,(t)}-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\neq\phi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}}\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{+,(t)}+\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\neq\phi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}}\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{-,(t)}\Bigg)`$ |  | (34) |

Now we can start to estimate the RHS. Define
$`P_{1}=\{\phi^{\prime}\neq\phi\in\Phi^{\star}\mid\phi^{\prime}\text{ learned}\}`$⁷⁷
7 By $`\phi^{\prime}`$ is learned we mean there is a feature
$`\psi^{\prime}\prec\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ such that
$`\psi^{\prime}=(j,r,\phi^{\prime})`$. and
$`P_{2}=\{\phi^{\prime}\neq\phi\in\Phi^{\star}\mid\phi^{\prime}\text{ not learned}\}`$.
The sum
$`\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\neq\phi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}}\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{+,(t)}`$
can be decomposed and bounded by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-O(\eta n_{y}\varpi)\leq\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in P_{1}}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}+\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in P_{2}}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\eta\lambda\mu^{q-2})`$ |  |

and then by the same methods of bounding the gradients, we have that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\neq\phi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}}\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{-,(t)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{\phi^{\prime}=(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}\setminus\{\phi\}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\Big(\sum_{g^{\prime\prime}\neq g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime\prime},y^{\prime})}}+\sum_{y^{\prime\prime}\neq y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime})}}\Big)\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ②}}{{}}=\sum_{g^{\prime}\neq g}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}}]+\sum_{y^{\prime}\neq y}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g,y^{\prime})}}]\pm O(\varpi)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{-,(t)}\pm O(\varpi)`$ |  |

where ① is because for any incorrect feature combination
$`\phi^{\prime}=(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\notin\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$ and does
not share a component with $`\phi`$, the activation of
$`\Lambda_{5,j,r}`$ conditioned on $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi^{\prime}}`$ is
within $`[-B,\widetilde{O}(\mu^{q-1})]`$ and thus the negative gradient
is upper bounded by $`\varpi/n_{y}^{2}`$ and lower bounded by
$`-\widetilde{O}(\mu^{q-1})`$. Inserting this back to
[Eq. 34](#A2.E34 "In Proof. ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we shall have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T)}\eta\Bigg(\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{(t)}-\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\Bigg)=\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T)}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}\pm O((T-T_{\psi}^{2,1})\eta\varpi)`$ |  |

Now we have arrived at the desired estimate of the update, finally, we
have the following counter-argument: if
$`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{+}(\delta)`$ does not hold for
$`\Omega(\frac{n_{y}\log^{2}d}{\eta\delta})`$ iterations, letting
$`T-1`$ be the last iteration which $`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{+}(\delta)`$
does not hold, we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Upsilon^{(T)}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T)}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T]\setminus B_{\delta}^{+}}\eta\delta+\Omega((T-T_{\psi}^{2,1})\eta\varpi)`$ |  | (For $`t\in B_{\delta}^{+}`$, $`\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}\geq 0`$) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},T]\setminus B_{\delta}^{+}}\eta\delta-O(\frac{n_{y}\log^{2}d}{\eta\delta}\eta\varpi)`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\frac{n_{y}\log^{2}d}{\eta\delta})\eta\delta`$ |  | (by choosing $`\delta\gg\varpi`$) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\Omega(n_{y}\log^{2}d)`$ |  |  |

which is impossible because the gradient would have vanished when
$`\Upsilon^{(T)}\geq n_{y}\log^{1.5}d`$ as that would mean
$`\exists\phi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}`$ such that
$`|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)|\geq\log^{2}d`$ and it is forbidden in our
setting, which proves the claim via contradiction.

Proof of
[lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b.
Actually, by applying (a) combined with
[lemma B.5](#A2.Thmlemma5 "Lemma B.5 (Feature Magnitude). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b,
we know that for some
$`T\geq T_{\psi}^{2,1}+\widetilde{\Omega}(\frac{1}{\eta\lambda})`$ it
holds $`V_{\psi}^{(T)}\geq B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$. From this iteration
forward we shall prove that $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ stays above
$`B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$ and can help. In fact, at $`t=T`$,
$`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(V)\in(-O(B),\widetilde{O}(\mu))`$ for
$`v\in\mathcal{G}\cup\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{g,y\}`$ by
[Induction B.3](#A2.Thminduction3 "Induction B.3 (Phase II). ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
Say $`v=g^{\prime}\neq g`$, then due to
[lemma B.4](#A2.Thmlemma4 "Lemma B.4 (gradient estimation). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
its gradient is approximated by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}}]\pm O(\varpi/n_{y})`$ |  | (35) |

Let $`\delta\geq\varpi`$, we will find an iteration
$`t\leq T_{\psi}^{2,1}+O(\frac{n_{y}\log^{3}d}{\eta\delta})`$ that
satisfy $`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{-}(\delta)`$. By applying (a), we first
get that $`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{+}(\delta)`$ holds for no more than
$`O(\frac{n_{y}\log^{2}d}{\eta\delta})`$ iters after $`T_{\psi}^{2,1}`$
and before $`T_{\psi}^{2}`$.

Let’s assume this is the case, that $`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{-}(\delta)`$
doesn’t hold for more than $`\Omega(\frac{n_{y}\log^{3}d}{\eta\delta})`$
iterations. In this case, it must be that the gradient
$`\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{-,(t)}<-\delta`$ is negative, since when the
gradient is positive it has an upper bound $`O(\varpi/n_{y})=o(\delta)`$
and therefore cannot violate the condition
$`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}^{+}(\delta)`$. Therefore the updates
$`\sum_{s\leq t}\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{-,(s)}`$ must accumulate to lower
than

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\leq\Upsilon^{(t)}-\Omega(\frac{n_{y}\log^{3}d}{\eta\delta})\times\eta\delta=-\Omega(n_{y}\log^{3}d)
``` |  |

which is impossible in our setting. This proved the result for any
$`\delta\gg n_{y}\varpi`$.

Proof of
[lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c.
Let $`\delta=n_{y}^{2}\varpi`$, which is covered by
[lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-b,
we can find an iteration where
$`|\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}|\leq\delta`$
$`\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}`$. This implies we
have found a $`t`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi^{\prime}}}]|\leq n_{y}^{2}\varpi\implies|\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi^{\prime})+O(\mu))|\leq O(\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})`$ |  | (We lower bounded the logit by [Fact B.2](#A2.Thmfact2 "Fact B.2 (logit lower bound). ‣ B.1.3 Technical Calculations ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")) |

Thus we arrive at
$`|V_{j,r}(\phi^{\prime})|\leq O((\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$
Remember in the proof of b,
$`V_{\psi}^{(t)}=\frac{1}{2}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y))\geq B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$
at $`t\geq T+\widetilde{\Omega}(\frac{d^{c_{1}}}{\eta\lambda})`$. So we
have for any
$`\phi^{\prime}=(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{2}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime})+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y^{\prime}))\leq-\frac{1}{2}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y))+O(\frac{1}{d^{1/3q}})\leq-B+O((\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})
``` |  |

Now we have verified that given $`\delta=n_{y}^{2}\varpi`$, there is a
iteration $`T_{\psi}^{2,3}`$ which gives the near-perfect feature shape
$`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ with
$`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu,\delta_{2}=(\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}`$.
We shall prove that this feature shape will hold until $`t=T_{2}`$.

For $`t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,3}`$, we verify
$`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ in
[Definition B.4](#A2.Thmdefinition4 "Definition B.4 (Feature Shape). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
one by one:

- 1.
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$: By
  [lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
  we shall find a step after
  $`t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,1}+O(\frac{d^{c_{1}}}{\eta\lambda})`$ at which
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$ is satisfied. In fact, the
  majority of the steps after
  $`T_{\psi}^{2,1}+O(\frac{d^{c_{1}}}{\eta\lambda})`$ will satisfy this.
  We shall prove that combined with the above feature shape guarantee,
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$ will hold true until
  $`t=T_{1}`$.
- 2.
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}((\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$:
  From the above argument that applies
  [lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b,
  we shall find a time step where $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$
  and $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}(d^{-1/3q})`$ hold. Now we discuss the
  possible values of the gradient
  $`\Gamma_{\psi^{\prime}}^{-,(t)},\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,1}`$.
  In fact, let
  $`\phi^{\prime}=(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}`$,
  we have

  - –
    Regime A: If $`V_{j,r}(\phi^{\prime})<-B-2\mu`$, then there is no
    negative gradient for $`V_{j,r}(g^{\prime})`$ and
    $`V_{j,r}(y^{\prime})`$ unless $`V_{j,r}(g^{\prime},y)`$ or
    $`V_{j,r}(g,y^{\prime})`$ is positive. In fact, only one of the
    feature can be positive to have negative gradient, and in that case
    the gradient of
    $`\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{(t)},v\in\{g^{\prime},y^{\prime}\}`$ is pointing
    to the convergence direction $`|V_{j,r}(g^{\prime},y)|\to 0`$.
  - –
    Regime B: If $`V_{j,r}(\phi^{\prime})\geq-B-2\mu`$, then there is a
    possibility of negative gradient for $`V_{j,r}(g^{\prime})`$ and
    $`V_{j,r}(y^{\prime})`$ even when $`V_{j,r}(g^{\prime},y)`$ or
    $`V_{j,r}(g,y^{\prime})`$ is negative, In this case the feature
    $`V_{j,r}(g^{\prime},y)`$ or $`V_{j,r}(g,y^{\prime})`$ might not go
    the desired direction to converge to zero, but given a
    $`\delta>n_{y}^{2}\varpi`$, $`\Gamma_{j,r,(g^{\prime},y)}^{-,(t)}`$
    or $`\Gamma_{j,r,(g,y^{\prime})}^{-,(t)}`$ cannot stay above
    $`\delta`$, otherwise the term will dominate and revert the gradient
    direction. On the other hand, when one side has
    $`V_{j,r}(g,y^{\prime})<-\Omega((\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$,
    we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}(\phi^{\prime})\leq-V_{j,r}(\phi)-\Omega((\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})\leq-B-2\mu`$ |  |

    which is back in regime A and therefore will converge back.

  Induction over the above two regimes for all
  $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{2,3},T_{\psi}^{2}]`$ showed that the feature will
  indeed satisfy $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}(\delta)`$ for all
  $`\delta>n_{y}^{2}\varpi`$ at $`t`$.
- 3.
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,3}((\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$:
  We need to use both $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}`$ and
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}`$ to get the desired result. In fact, we have
  for any
  $`\psi^{\prime}=(j,r,(g^{\prime},y^{\prime}))\in\Sigma^{\dagger,2}`$,

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle V_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(t)}=V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime})+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y^{\prime})+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)=-B+O((\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$ |  |

  because $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y^{\prime})`$ and
  $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime})+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)`$ are bounded due to
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}`$.
- 4.
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,4}(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$: The number of iterations
  $`[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{\psi}^{2}]`$ is no more than
  $`\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta(d^{c_{1}}\mu)^{q-2}})`$, so by using
  [lemma B.13](#A2.Thmlemma13 "Lemma B.13 (TPM, adapted from []). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  along with the induction from the result at $`T_{\psi}^{1}`$ shall
  conclude the proof.
- 5.
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,5}(((\frac{n_{y}^{3}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}))`$:
  By using
  [lemma B.7](#A2.Thmlemma7 "Lemma B.7 (Symmetry of Features). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  and $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}`$ after $`t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,3}`$, we have
  the result.

This proved (c). ∎

###### Lemma B.7 (Symmetry of Features).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,(g,y))\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ and $`t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,3}`$,
we have that if $`|V_{\psi^{\prime}}|\leq\delta`$ for all
$`\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\dagger,1}`$, then

|     |                                                                    |     |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     | $`\displaystyle|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)|\leq O(\delta)`$ |     |

###### Proof.

For a feature $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Sigma^{\star},\phi=(g,y)`$, their
update can be represented as:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)`$ | $`\displaystyle=U_{g,y}-\sum_{y^{\prime}\neq y}R_{g,y^{\prime}}+V_{j,r}^{(0)}(g)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)`$ | $`\displaystyle=U_{g,y}-\sum_{g^{\prime}\neq g}R_{g^{\prime},y}+V_{j,r}^{(0)}(y)`$ |  |

where $`U_{g,y}=\sum_{s\leq t}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(s)}`$ and
$`R_{g,y^{\prime}}`$ is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle R_{g,y^{\prime}}=\sum_{s\leq t}\eta\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(s)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(s)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{g^{\prime},y}}]`$ |  |

Therefore:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{g}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)=\sum_{\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}}U_{\phi}-\sum_{g}\sum_{y^{\prime}\neq y}R_{g,y^{\prime}}=\sum_{\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}}U_{\phi}-\sum_{\phi^{\dagger}\in\Phi_{j}^{\dagger}}R_{\phi^{\dagger}}+V_{j,r}^{(0)}(g)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{y}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)=\sum_{\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}}U_{\phi}-\sum_{y}\sum_{g^{\prime}\neq g}R_{g,y^{\prime}}=\sum_{\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}}U_{\phi}-\sum_{\phi^{\dagger}\in\Phi_{j}^{\dagger}}R_{\phi^{\dagger}}+V_{j,r}^{(0)}(y)`$ |  |

Thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{g^{\prime}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime})=\sum_{y^{\prime}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y^{\prime})\pm O(\mu)`$ |  |

Moreover, we have assumed

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y^{\prime})|=O(\delta),\forall y^{\prime}\neq y,\quad|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime})|=O(\delta),\forall g^{\prime}\neq g`$ |  |

Thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{g^{\prime}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime})=V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-(n_{y}-1)V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)\pm O(n_{y}\delta)=V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-(n_{y}-1)V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)\pm O(n_{y}\delta)`$ |  |

Therefore

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)=V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)+O(\delta)+O(\mu)`$ |  |

inserting
$`\delta=O((\frac{n_{y}^{2}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$ which
is from
[lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
proves the claim. ∎

###### Lemma B.8 (Arrival time estimates).

Assuming
[Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.3](#A2.Thminduction3 "Induction B.3 (Phase II). ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
for $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, we have

1.  1.
    $`T_{\psi}^{2,1}-T_{\psi}^{1}\leq\widetilde{O}(1/\eta(d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0})^{q-2})`$;
2.  2.
    $`T_{\psi}^{2,2}-T_{\psi}^{2,1}\leq O(\frac{d^{\frac{1}{2}+c_{1}}}{\eta})`$;
3.  3.
    $`T_{\psi}^{2,3}-T_{\psi}^{2,2}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta\varpi})`$;
4.  4.
    $`T_{\psi}^{2}-T_{\psi}^{1}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta(d^{2c_{1}}\mu)^{q-2}})`$

###### Proof.

- •
  Proof of (a): Once $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0}`$, we can
  apply
  [lemma B.13](#A2.Thmlemma13 "Lemma B.13 (TPM, adapted from []). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  to compute the number of iteration needed to reach
  $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq\frac{1}{2}\log d`$. In fact, by
  [Induction B.3](#A2.Thminduction3 "Induction B.3 (Phase II). ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-b
  we know that

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle V_{\psi}^{(t+1)}\geq V_{\psi}^{(t)}+\eta(1-O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(V_{\psi}^{(t)}+O(\mu))\geq V_{\psi}^{(t)}+\Omega(\eta(V_{\psi}^{(t)})^{q-1})`$ |  |

  Since $`V_{\psi}^{(T_{\psi}^{1})}\geq d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0}`$, we have
  by
  [lemma B.13](#A2.Thmlemma13 "Lemma B.13 (TPM, adapted from []). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  that
  $`T_{\psi}^{2,1}-T_{\psi}^{1}\leq O(\frac{1}{\eta(d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0})^{q-2}})`$.
- •
  Proof of (b): Simply apply
  [lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  with $`\delta=\frac{1}{d^{\frac{1}{2}+c_{1}}}`$;
- •
  Proof of (c): Again by applying
  [lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  with $`\delta=n_{y}^{2}\varpi`$;
- •
  Proof of (d): Follow the definition of $`T_{\psi}^{2}`$.

∎

###### Proof of [Induction B.3](#A2.Thminduction3 "Induction B.3 (Phase II). ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").

The proof of time steps are all in
[lemma B.8](#A2.Thmlemma8 "Lemma B.8 (Arrival time estimates). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
We do not repeat here. ∎

### B.4 Phase III: Convergence

We present the induction hypothesis in this phase.

###### Induction B.4 (Phase III, final).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, for
$`t\in[T_{\psi}^{2},T_{1}]`$, the following holds:

1.  1.
    At $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{2},T_{1,1}]`$, we have
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ holds with
    $`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu`$,
    $`\delta_{2}=(\frac{n_{y}^{2}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}`$;
2.  2.
    At $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{3},T_{1}]`$, we have
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ holds with
    $`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu`$,
    $`\delta_{2}=(n_{y}^{2}\varpi)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}`$.

We need a lemma to describe the logit distribution when all features are
learned.

###### Lemma B.9 (Logit shape at convergence).

Assuming
[Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.4](#A2.Thminduction4 "Induction B.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
Let $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ be the last of $`\Sigma^{\star}`$, then at
$`t=T_{\psi}^{2}`$, the followings hold:

1.  1.
    For $`\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$,
    $`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\geq 1-O(\lambda)`$ conditioned on
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$.
2.  2.
    For $`\phi\notin\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$,
    $`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\leq O(\lambda/d)`$ conditioned on
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$.

###### Proof.

Since $`\psi`$ is the last feature in the learning curriculum
$`\Sigma^{\star}`$, all feature at $`t=T_{\psi}^{2}`$ has satisfied
$`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ for
$`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu`$,
$`\delta_{2}=(\frac{n_{y}^{2}d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}`$. Thus
we can bound the logit by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}=\frac{e^{F^{(t)}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z})}}{e^{F^{(t)}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z})}+d}=\frac{e^{B-o(1)}}{e^{B-o(1)}+d}=1-O(\lambda)`$ |  | (conditioned on $`\mathbf{Z}\in\mathcal{H}_{\phi},\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$) |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}=\frac{e^{F^{(t)}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z})}}{e^{F^{(t)}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z})}+d}=\frac{e^{o(1)}}{e^{B-o(1)}+d-1+e^{o(1)}}=O(\frac{\lambda}{d})`$ |  | (conditioned on $`\mathbf{Z}\in\mathcal{H}_{\phi},\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$) |

which is the desired result. ∎

###### Lemma B.10 (Gradient Bounds, Phase III).

Assuming
[Inductions B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [B.4](#A2.Thminduction4 "Induction B.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds, and let $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, then at
$`t\in[T_{1,1},T_{1}]`$, the followings hold

1.  1.
    $`\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}\geq\Omega(\lambda)`$ when
    $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\leq B-d^{c_{1}}\mu`$;
2.  2.
    $`|\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{-,(t)}|\leq O(\frac{\lambda\varpi}{dn_{y}})`$ for
    any $`v\in\mathcal{G}\cup\mathcal{Y}`$;
3.  3.
    For $`\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}\setminus\{\phi\}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{+,(t)}=\begin{cases}-\Theta(\lambda\varpi/n_{y}^{2}),&\text{if }V_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\in[-B+2\mu,-\varpi]\\
    \in[-\Theta(\lambda\varpi/n_{y}^{2}),-\Theta(\lambda\varpi/dn_{y}^{2})]\cup\{0\},&\text{if }V_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\in[-B-2\mu,-B+2\mu]\end{cases}`$ |  |

###### Proof.

[lemma B.10](#A2.Thmlemma10 "Lemma B.10 (Gradient Bounds, Phase III). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
is simple when
[lemma B.9](#A2.Thmlemma9 "Lemma B.9 (Logit shape at convergence). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
holds.
[lemma B.10](#A2.Thmlemma10 "Lemma B.10 (Gradient Bounds, Phase III). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b
is basically redoing the calculations in
[lemma B.4](#A2.Thmlemma4 "Lemma B.4 (gradient estimation). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
[lemma B.10](#A2.Thmlemma10 "Lemma B.10 (Gradient Bounds, Phase III). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c
is because when $`V_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\geq-B+2\mu`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}^{\prime}}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}^{\prime}}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\lambda\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(V_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}+\mu\pm o(\mu))\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{H}_{\phi^{\prime}})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq-\Theta(\lambda\varpi/n_{y}^{2})`$ |  |

And the rest is simply the property of $`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}`$ at
the negative boundary when some activations of $`x_{0},x_{1}`$ crosses
the boundary. ∎

#### B.4.1 Proof of Induction in Phase III and [theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")

###### Proof of [Inductions B.4](#A2.Thminduction4 "Induction B.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"), [B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization") and [B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").

We prove the induction by reusing the proof of
[lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").

- •
  Proof of
  [Induction B.4](#A2.Thminduction4 "Induction B.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a:
  The same as in the proof of
  [lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c,
  with longer time. We do not use any property of $`T_{\psi}^{3}`$ so no
  additional handling is needed.
- •
  Proof of
  [Induction B.4](#A2.Thminduction4 "Induction B.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b:
  When all feature is learned, that is, after $`t=T_{\psi}^{2}`$ for the
  last feature $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, we have perfect feature shape
  and therefore have much better logit shape as described by
  [lemma B.9](#A2.Thmlemma9 "Lemma B.9 (Logit shape at convergence). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
  We can reuse the proof of
  [lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-b
  to get the following result: for any
  $`\delta=\omega(\frac{\lambda\varpi}{dn_{y}})`$, we can guarantee that
  $`\mathcal{C}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ doesn’t hold for more than
  $`\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta\delta})`$ iterations. And beyond that,
  we can just reuse the argument in the proof of
  [lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c
  to obtain the desired result.
- •
  Proof of
  [Induction B.1](#A2.Thminduction1 "Induction B.1 (Simply Transitive Actions, All Phases). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-b:
  They are correct in Phase III because there is no violation.
- •
  Proof of
  [Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-b:
  They are proven in previous phases.
- •
  Proof of
  [Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c:
  For predecessor feature $`\psi^{\prime}\prec\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$,
  we proved in this phase that they maintain feature shape until
  $`T_{\psi^{\prime}}^{3}`$, and improved their feature at
  $`T_{\psi^{\prime}}^{3}`$.
- •
  Proof of
  [Induction B.2](#A2.Thminduction2 "Induction B.2 (Induction for Individual Feature). ‣ Phase Decomposition. ‣ B.1.2 Induction Hypotheses and Phase Decomposition ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")d:
  This is proven in previous phases.

∎

###### Proof of [theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").

Since all feature in $`\Sigma^{\star}`$ have shape
$`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ for
$`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu,\delta_{2}=\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}}`$ at the end
of
[Induction B.4](#A2.Thminduction4 "Induction B.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have proven
[theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
∎

### B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools

First we need a Bernstein inequality for U-statistics

###### Lemma B.11 (concentration inequality for pseudo-U-statistics).

Let $`x_{1},\dots,x_{n}`$ be different symbols, and let $`m\ll n`$ be
such that $`n\equiv 0\pmod{n}`$. Suppose for some function $`h`$ with
$`|h|\leq M`$ the random variables
$`h(x_{i_{1}},x_{i_{2}},\dots,x_{i_{m}})`$ and
$`h(x_{i^{\prime}_{1}},x_{i^{\prime}_{2}},\dots,x_{i^{\prime}_{m}})`$
are independent and identically distributed as long as
$`\{x_{i_{1}},x_{i_{2}},\dots,x_{i_{m}}\}\cap\{x_{i^{\prime}_{1}},x_{i^{\prime}_{2}},\dots,x_{i^{\prime}_{m}}\}=\emptyset`$,
then the pseudo-U-statistic

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle U_{m,n}=\frac{1}{\binom{n}{m}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{m}\leq n}h(x_{i_{1}},x_{i_{2}},\dots,x_{i_{m}})`$ |  |

satisfies
$`\mathbf{Pr}(|U_{n,m}-\mathbb{E}[U_{n,m}]|\geq t)\leq e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{mM^{2}}}`$

###### Proof.

The proof is the same as in \[[Rin16](#bib.bibx80)\]. ∎

We present two lemmas related to the tensor power method.

###### Lemma B.12 (TPM in expectation).

Let $`p>3`$ be an integer, and $`\mu,\sigma>0`$ satisfying
$`\mu\gg\sigma\sqrt{\log d}`$. Now suppose
$`\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma)`$ is a Gaussian variable and there are
two sequences $`x_{t},y_{t}`$ defined by the following update rules:

- •
  $`x_{t+1}\geq x_{t}+\eta C_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[(\xi+x_{t})^{p}]`$ for
  some $`C_{t}=\Theta(1)`$;
- •
  $`y_{t+1}\leq y_{t}+\eta C_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[(\xi+y_{t})^{p}]`$ for
  some $`S=\Theta(1)`$.

Then if
$`x_{0},y_{0}\geq\mu,x_{0}-y_{0}\geq\varepsilon=\Omega(\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(d)})`$,
for every $`t\geq 0`$ such that $`x_{t}\in[d^{0.01}x_{0},O(1)]`$, we
shall have $`y_{t}\leq O(\mathrm{polylog}(d))`$.

###### Proof.

Let $`\delta>0`$ be such that $`\delta\gg\frac{\mu}{\sigma}`$ and
$`\delta=o(1)`$. Let $`T_{i},i\geq 0`$ be defined as the first time when
$`x_{t}\geq(1+\delta)^{i}x_{0}`$, and
$`b:=\min\{i|(1+\delta)^{i}x_{0}\geq A\}`$. Now we can compute a growth
lower bound for $`\varepsilon_{t}:=x_{t}-y_{t}`$ at each step:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\varepsilon_{T_{i+1}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon_{T_{i}}+\sum_{t\in[T_{i},T_{i+1})}\eta C_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[(\xi+x_{t})^{p}-(\xi+y_{t})^{p}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon_{T_{i}}+\varepsilon_{T_{i}}\sum_{t\in[T_{i},T_{i+1})}\eta C_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[(\xi+x_{t})^{p-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon_{T_{i}}+\varepsilon_{T_{i}}\sum_{t\in[T_{i},T_{i+1})}\eta C_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[(\xi+x_{t})^{p}]\cdot\frac{1}{(1+\delta)x_{T_{i+1}}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon_{T_{i}}+\varepsilon_{T_{i}}\delta(1+\delta)^{i}x_{0}\frac{1}{(1+\delta)^{i+2}x_{0}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq(1+\frac{\delta}{(1+\delta)^{2}})\varepsilon_{T_{i}}`$ |  |

Therefore let $`i^{\prime}`$ be such that
$`(1+\delta)^{i^{\prime}}\geq\frac{\mu}{\varepsilon}`$, we have that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\varepsilon_{T_{i^{\prime}}}\geq(1+\frac{\delta}{(1+\delta)^{2}})^{i^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{0}\geq(1-o(1))\mu\gg\sigma\sqrt{\log d}`$ |  | (36) |

At $`t=T_{i^{\prime}}`$ we have
$`y_{T_{i^{\prime}}}\leq x_{T_{i^{\prime}}}\leq(1+o(1))\frac{\mu}{\varepsilon}x_{0}`$.
After $`t=T_{i^{\prime}}`$, we can construct a surrogate sequence
$`\widetilde{y}_{t}\geq y_{t}`$ for $`t\geq T_{i^{\prime}}`$ by
initializing
$`\widetilde{y}_{T_{i^{\prime}}}=y_{T_{i^{\prime}}}+\frac{\mu}{2}`$, and
updates by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{t+1}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\widetilde{y}_{t}+\eta C_{t}\widetilde{y}_{t}^{p}`$ |  |

This update guarantees that $`\widetilde{y}_{t}\geq y_{t}`$ because
$`\widetilde{y}_{t}^{p}\geq\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[(\xi+y_{t})^{p}]`$ at every
step. Now by applying
[corollary B.1](#A2.Thmcorollary1 "Corollary B.1 (TPM, from []). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
to the sequence of $`x_{t},\widetilde{y}_{t}`$ using the fact that
$`x_{T_{i^{\prime}}}\geq\widetilde{y}_{T_{i^{\prime}}}+\frac{\mu}{2}=\widetilde{y}_{T_{i^{\prime}}}(1+\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(d)})`$,
we can get the desired result for $`\widetilde{y}_{t}`$ and thus
$`y_{t}`$ at $`t=T_{b}`$, i.e, when $`x_{t}\geq A`$. ∎

###### Lemma B.13 (TPM, adapted from \[[AZL20](#bib.bibx6)\]).

Consider an increasing sequence $`x_{t}\geq 0`$ defined by
$`x_{t+1}=x_{t}+\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q-1}`$ for some integer $`q\geq 3`$ and
$`C_{t}=\Theta(1)>0`$, then we have for every $`A>x_{0}`$, for every
$`\delta>0`$, and every $`\eta\in(0,1)`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\delta(1+\delta)^{-1}}{(1+\delta)^{q-2}-1}\left(1-\left(\frac{(1+\delta)x_{0}}{A}\right)^{q-2}\right)-\frac{O(\eta A^{q-1})}{x_{0}}\frac{\log(A/x_{0})}{\log(1+\delta)}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-2}}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{(1+\delta)^{q-2}}{q-2}+\frac{O(\eta A^{q-1})}{x_{0}}\frac{\log(A/x_{0})}{\log(1+\delta)}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-2}}`$ |  |

This lemma has a corollary:

###### Corollary B.1 (TPM, from \[[AZL20](#bib.bibx6)\]).

Let $`q\geq 3`$ be a constant and $`x_{0},y_{0}=o(1)`$ and $`A=O(1)`$.
Let $`\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}`$ be two positive sequences updated as

- •
  $`x_{t+1}=x_{t}+\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q-1}`$ for some $`C_{t}=\Theta(1)`$;
- •
  $`y_{t+1}=y_{t}+\eta SC_{t}y_{t}^{q-1}`$ for some $`S=\Theta(1)`$.

Suppose
$`x_{0}\geq y_{0}S^{\frac{1}{q-2}}(1+\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(d)})`$,
letting $`T_{x}`$ be the first iteration s.t., $`x_{t}\geq A`$, then
$`y_{T_{x}}\leq\widetilde{O}(y_{0}).`$

## Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions

##### Roadmap.

We begin by introducing notation and defining fibers, followed by a
description of the learning curriculum and the key appearance events. We
then break the training process into clearly defined phases and prove
the main results for each phase: In Phase I, features emerge and
compete; In Phase II, features grow and mutually cancel, with precise
tracking of when different outcomes occur; In Phase III, features
converge to a desired shape.

Let us restate the action structure of $`\mathcal{G}`$ and
$`\mathcal{Y}`$ here.

###### Assumption C.1 (Assumption [5.1](#S5.Thmassumption1 "Assumption 5.1 (Symmetry group actions). ‣ 5 Learning CoT on Symmetry Group Actions on ℤ_𝑛 ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"), restated).

Let $`\textbf{LEGO}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{G},\mathcal{Y})`$ be the LEGO
language, where $`|\mathcal{Y}|=n_{Y}`$⁸⁸ 8 We use $`n_{y}`$ or
$`n_{Y}`$ interchangeably in the proof below. and $`\mathcal{G}`$ is a
group acting on on $`\mathcal{Y}`$. We assume the (left) group action
$`\alpha:\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{Y}`$ is transitive but
not free. In particular, we assume
$`n_{Y}=|\mathcal{Y}|\in[\omega(1),O(\log d)]`$ and the group
$`\mathcal{G}`$ satisfy
$`n_{G}=|\mathcal{G}|=\Theta(\mathrm{polylog}(d))>\frac{1}{\varrho}`$
for all $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$.

###### Remark C.1.

When the action $`\alpha:\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{Y}`$ is
transitive, there is only one orbit
$`\mathcal{Y}=\{g\cdot y\mid g\in\mathcal{G}\}\simeq\mathcal{G}/G_{y}`$
for all $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$. By orbit-stabilizer theorem, the
stabilizer$`G_{y}`$ at any point $`y`$ has the same cardinality.

###### Remark C.2.

The canonical action of a symmetry group $`S_{n}`$ on $`\mathbb{Z}_{n}`$
satisfy
[Assumption C.1](#A3.Thmassumption1 "Assumption C.1 (Assumption , restated). ‣ Roadmap. ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
with the choice $`n_{y}=\Theta(\frac{\log\log d}{\log\log\log d})`$ that
keeps $`n_{y}!=O(\mathrm{polylog}(d))`$.

### C.1 Preliminaries

We work with fiber-indexed features. We first define fibers and
super-combinations $`\varphi`$, then we define neuron features by its
index $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)`$, and finally set the curriculum and
appearance events in this notation.

###### Definition C.1 (Fiber of values).

Assuming $`\mathcal{G}`$ follows
[Assumption C.1](#A3.Thmassumption1 "Assumption C.1 (Assumption , restated). ‣ Roadmap. ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
For each $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, define the
fiber of $`j`$ at $`y`$ as:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}:=\{g\in\mathcal{G}\mid\tau(g\cdot y)=j\}`$ |  |

which collects all group elements that send $`y`$ to
$`y^{\prime}=\tau^{-1}(j)`$.

###### Remark C.3.

We denote $`\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ to be the set that transport $`y`$ to
$`y^{\prime}=\tau^{-1}(j)`$ because it is the pre-image of the predictor
map $`\phi_{y}(\cdot):g\mapsto\tau(g\cdot y)`$ over $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$.
Algebraically the fibers are the left-cosets
$`\{gG_{y}\}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}`$, where
$`G_{y}:=\{g\in\mathcal{G}\mid g\cdot y=y\}`$ is the stablizer subgroup.
Moreover, we have the following fact from the orbit-stablizer theorem.

###### Fact C.1 (cardinality of the fibers).

For every $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$,
$`|\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}|=|\mathcal{G}|/|\mathcal{Y}|=n_{G}/n_{Y}`$ from
the orbit-stablizer theorem.

###### Definition C.2 (Super-combinations).

Let $`(j,y)\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times\mathcal{Y}`$, we define the
super-combinations, which is a set of combinations:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\varphi_{j,y}:=\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}\times\{y\}\subseteq\mathcal{G}\times\mathcal{Y},\quad\varphi_{j,y}^{1}=\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},\quad\varphi_{j,y}^{2}=\{y\}
``` |  |

Let
$`\Phi:=\cup_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y}),\ y\in\mathcal{Y}}\varphi_{j,y}`$ be
the full set. For a fixed class $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, we denote the
correct set to be
$`\Phi_{j}^{\star}:=\cup_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\varphi_{j,y}`$, and the
incorrect set
$`\Phi_{j}^{\dagger}:=\cup_{j^{\prime}\neq j,y\in\mathcal{Y}}\varphi_{j,y}`$.
A base pair $`\phi=(g,y)`$ belongs to $`\varphi_{j,y}`$ if and only if
$`g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$. To simplify the proof, we also define for
each $`\varphi_{j,y}\subset\Phi`$, the confounding combinations

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Phi_{\varphi}^{\dagger}=\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\neq j\textbf{ XOR }y^{\prime}\neq y}\varphi_{j^{\prime},y^{\prime}}`$ |  |

That is, the set of combinations that intersect with exactly one
component with $`\varphi_{j,y}`$.

###### Definition C.3 (Neuron feature indices).

Let us index neuron features by $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)`$ with
$`\varphi\in\Phi`$ and $`(j,r)\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times[m]`$, and
define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Psi^{\star}_{j,r}:=\{(j,r,\varphi):\varphi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}\},\quad\Psi^{\dagger}_{j,r}:=\{(j,r,\varphi):\varphi\in\Phi_{j}^{\dagger}\}.
``` |  |

And we further define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Psi:=\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times[m]\times\Phi,\quad\Psi^{\star}=\bigcup_{(j,r)\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times[m]}\Psi^{\star}_{j,r},\quad\Psi^{\dagger}=\bigcup_{(j,r)\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})\times[m]}\Psi^{\dagger}_{j,r}
``` |  |

The set $`\Psi`$ contains all the neuron indices we care about, and
$`\Psi^{\star}`$ and $`\Psi^{\dagger}`$ contain the correct and
incorrect indices for class $`j`$.

##### $`V`$-Notations

We call a pair $`\phi=(g,y)`$ with $`g\in\mathcal{G}`$ and
$`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$ a *base pair*, or following the naming of
[appendix B](#A2 "Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
a *base combination*. For neuron $`(j,r)`$, we define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{j,r}(g):=\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,2},e_{g}\rangle,\quad V_{j,r}(y):=\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,5},e_{y}\rangle,\quad V_{j,r}(\phi):=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(V_{j,r}(g)+V_{j,r}(y)\big).
``` |  |

For a fixed value $`y`$ and class $`j`$, the fiber
$`\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ collects all permutations sending $`y`$ to
$`\tau^{-1}(j)`$. For $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi_{j,y})`$, we define:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{\psi}:=\frac{1}{n_{G}/n_{Y}}\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}V_{j,r}(g,y),\quad\overline{V}_{\psi}:=\max_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}V_{j,r}(g,y),\quad\underline{V}_{\psi}:=\min_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}V_{j,r}(g,y).
``` |  |

For compactness of notation, let us also write
$`V_{j,r}(x)=\sum_{p=3,4}\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,1},e_{x_{0}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,1},e_{x_{1}}\rangle`$
which is a random variable depending on the randomness of
$`x_{0},x_{1}`$.

The definition of learning curriculum for the symmetry group actions is
essentially the same with the simply transitive case, with a slight
difference of using the fiber-combination $`\varphi`$ instead of the
base combination $`\phi`$. We repeat here for reference.

###### Definition C.4 (Learning curriculum, symmetry group actions).

We generate an feature index set $`\Sigma^{\star}`$ over fiber-indexed
features. Letting $`\Sigma_{0}=\Psi`$, at each $`i\in[n_{y}^{2}]`$, we
choose

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\psi_{i}=\arg\max_{\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma_{i},\ \varphi\in\Phi_{j}^{\star}}V^{(0)}_{\psi}.
``` |  |

Write $`\psi_{i}=(j,r,\varphi)`$, we define the next iteration set
$`\Sigma_{i+1}`$ by excluding:

1.  1.
    The confusing combinations for $`\varphi`$ in the same neuron:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,1}\equiv\Sigma_{i}^{\dagger,1}=\{(j,r,\varphi^{\prime})\in\Sigma_{i},\varphi^{\prime}\in\Phi_{\varphi}^{\dagger}\};
    ``` |  |
2.  2.
    All other combinations in the same neuron $`(j,r)`$:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,2}\equiv\Sigma_{i}^{\dagger,2}=\{(j,r,\varphi^{\prime})\mid\varphi^{\prime}\in\Phi\setminus(\Phi_{\varphi}^{\dagger}\cup\{\varphi\})\};
    ``` |  |
3.  3.
    The same combination $`\varphi_{j,y}`$ in other neurons of the same
    class $`j,r^{\prime},r^{\prime}\neq r`$:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,3}\equiv\Sigma_{i}^{\dagger,3}=\{(j,r^{\prime},\varphi_{j,y})\in\Sigma_{i}:r^{\prime}\neq r\}.
    ``` |  |

The next iteration is given by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Sigma_{i+1}=\Sigma_{i}\setminus(\Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,1}\cup\Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,2}\cup\Sigma_{\psi_{i}}^{\dagger,3}\cup\{\psi_{i}\})
``` |  |

Eventually we obtain
$`\Sigma^{\star}=(\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{n_{y}^{2}})`$ and
$`\Sigma^{\dagger}:=\bigcup_{\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}}\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger}`$.
We write $`\psi\prec_{\Sigma}\psi^{\prime}`$ (or simply
$`\psi\prec\psi^{\prime}`$) if $`\psi`$ precedes $`\psi^{\prime}`$ in
$`\Sigma^{\star}`$. More generally, we also write
$`\psi\prec\psi^{\prime}`$ for $`\psi,\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi`$ if
$`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ and
$`\psi^{\prime}\in\cup_{\psi^{\prime\prime}\succ\psi}\Sigma^{\dagger}_{\psi^{\prime\prime}}`$
is from the .

##### Events of combination appearance.

These events specify whether a specific base combination $`\phi=(g,y)`$
appears, or only one of its component matches.

###### Definition C.5 (Events of appearance).

For $`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi_{j,y}`$ for some $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$
and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, we define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{H}_{\phi}:=\{g_{1}=g,\ y_{0}=y\},\quad\mathcal{H}_{g}:=\{g_{1}=g\},\quad\mathcal{H}_{y}:=\{y_{0}=y\},\quad
``` |  |

For the mismatched events $`\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}`$, the definition is a
little different from the simply transitive actions: since
$`\varphi_{j,y}=\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}\times\{y\}`$ and
$`g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, we write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\varphi}(g):=\{g_{1}=g,y_{0}\neq y\},\quad\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\varphi}(y):=\{g_{1}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},\ y_{0}=y\},
``` |  |

When considering the event of all the base pair
$`\phi\in\varphi_{j,y}`$, we also define the union events

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}:=\bigcup_{\phi\in\varphi}\mathcal{H}_{\phi},\quad\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\varphi}:=\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\varphi}(y)\cup\Big(\bigcup\nolimits_{\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi}\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\varphi}(g)\Big).
``` |  |

#### C.1.1 Theorem Statement

Now we present the theorem for learning symmetric group actions.

###### Theorem C.1 (Learning Symmetric Group Actions).

Assuming
[Assumption 5.1](#S5.Thmassumption1 "Assumption 5.1 (Symmetry group actions). ‣ 5 Learning CoT on Symmetry Group Actions on ℤ_𝑛 ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
and let $`F^{(t)}`$ be obtained by
Algorithm [2](#algorithm2 "Algorithm 2 ‣ 5 Learning CoT on Symmetry Group Actions on ℤ_𝑛 ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
at $`t=T_{1}`$, then the loss for the $`5`$th-token is minimized, i.e.,
$`\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{(T_{1})}\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}`$, and for
each $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, the followings hold:

1.  1.
    For any $`\phi\in\varphi`$, we have
    $`V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(\phi)\in[B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu),B+O(\mu)]`$;
2.  2.
    For any
    $`\psi^{\prime}=(j,r,\varphi^{\prime})\in\Sigma^{\dagger,1}`$ and
    $`\phi^{\prime}\in\varphi^{\prime}`$, we have
    $`|V^{(T_{1})}_{j,r}(\phi^{\prime})|\leq\widetilde{O}(\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$.
    Or more concretely:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \frac{1}{2}\Big|V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(g^{\prime})+V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(y^{\prime})\Big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}}),\quad\forall(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\varphi^{\prime}
    ``` |  |
3.  3.
    For any $`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi`$ the following relation holds:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \Big|V^{(T_{1})}_{j,r}(g)-C_{\alpha}V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(y)\Big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}})
    ``` |  |

    where
    $`C_{\alpha}=\frac{1+n_{G}(n_{Y}-1)/n_{Y}}{(n_{Y}-1)+n_{G}/n_{Y}}=\Theta(n_{Y})`$
    due to our choice of $`n_{G},n_{Y}`$ in
    [Assumption C.1](#A3.Thmassumption1 "Assumption C.1 (Assumption , restated). ‣ Roadmap. ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
4.  4.
    For any $`\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\dagger,3}`$, it holds that
    $`\overline{V}_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$.

The assertions of
[Theorem C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem C.1 (Learning Symmetric Group Actions). ‣ C.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
described the *feature shapes* of the neurons, which we formally defined
in the proof of learning simply transitive actions
([Definition B.1](#A2.Thmdefinition1 "Definition B.1 (Combinations ϕ,Φ,Φ^†). ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")).
The main difference between the results here and
[Theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
is that
[Theorem C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem C.1 (Learning Symmetric Group Actions). ‣ C.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")C
described a different symmetry of features without the factor
$`\Theta(n_{y})`$.

Let us follow the proof in
[Appendix B](#A2 "Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and the feature shape for symmetry group actions.

###### Definition C.6 (Feature shape).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Psi`$, and let
$`\delta=(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ be the error parameters. We say the
feature $`\psi`$ has shape $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ if the
followings are satisfied:

1.  1.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}(\delta_{1})`$:
    $`\underline{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq B-O(\delta_{1})`$ and
    $`\overline{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}\leq B+O(\delta_{1})`$;
2.  2.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}(\delta_{2})`$: For any
    $`\psi^{\prime}=(j,r,\varphi^{\prime})\in\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,1}`$,
    it holds that $`|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi^{\prime})|\leq O(\delta_{2})`$
    for all $`\phi^{\prime}\in\varphi^{\prime}`$;
3.  3.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,3}(\delta_{2})`$:
    $`|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-C_{\mathcal{Y}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)|\leq O(\delta_{2})`$
    for any $`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi`$;
4.  4.
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,4}`$: For any feature
    $`\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\psi}^{\dagger,3}`$, it holds that
    $`\overline{V}_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$.

Indeed, we aim to prove that $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ holds for
suitable parameter $`\delta`$ at the end of training, for every
$`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. And this is indeed what the theorem states.

###### Remark C.4.

A major difference between
[Definition C.6](#A3.Thmdefinition6 "Definition C.6 (Feature shape). ‣ C.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[Definition B.4](#A2.Thmdefinition4 "Definition B.4 (Feature Shape). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
is that now the conditions involve not just one combination of weights,
but all combinations $`\phi\in\varphi`$ given a combination
$`\varphi\in\Phi`$. This creates some significant difficulties for the
proof.

#### C.1.2 Facts and Basic Calculations

We now define the gradient notation and conditions.

###### Definition C.7 (gradient notation).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)`$, we write $`\Gamma^{+}`$ and $`\Gamma^{-}`$
to denote the different terms in the gradient computation. For any base
combination $`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi_{j,y}`$ where
$`\varphi_{j,y}\in\Phi`$, we write

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,\phi}:=\mathbb{E}\big[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\big],\quad\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,\phi}:=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\big].`$ |  |

When we consider the gradient of $`V_{j,r}(g)`$ or $`V_{j,r}(y)`$ for
individual tokens $`g\in\mathcal{G}`$ and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, we use
the following notations: let $`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi_{j,y}`$, then we
define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,g}:=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\big]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,g}:=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\varphi}(g)}\big]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,y}:=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\varphi}(y)}\big].`$ |  |

Finally, we sum up over $`\phi\in\varphi`$ for the gradient of
combination $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{\psi}:=\mathbb{E}\big[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}}\big],`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{\psi}:=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}^{\dagger}_{\varphi}}\big].`$ |  |

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)`$ where $`\varphi_{1}=\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$
One can check that the defined $`\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{\psi}`$ is connected to
the base pair $`\Gamma`$ by the following relation:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{\psi}=\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,g},\quad\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{\psi}=\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,y}+\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,g}`$ |  | (37) |

###### Definition C.8 (gradient condition).

At $`t\leq T_{1}`$ and $`\delta>0`$, write for $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\psi}(\delta):\ |\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{\psi}-\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{\psi}|\leq\delta,\qquad\mathcal{C}^{+}_{\psi}(\delta):\ |\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{\psi}|\leq\delta,\qquad\mathcal{C}^{-}_{\psi}(\delta):\ |\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{\psi}|\leq\delta.`$ |  |

These conditions control the magnitude of the gradient of the feature at
iteration $`t`$. Next we compute the expressions of the gradient using

###### Fact C.2 (gradient expressions).

Equipped with
[Definition C.8](#A3.Thmdefinition8 "Definition C.8 (gradient condition). ‣ C.1.2 Facts and Basic Calculations ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
the gradient with respect to $`\mathbf{W}_{i,j,r,p}`$ for $`i=5`$,
$`j\in[d]`$, $`r\in[m]`$, $`p\in[5]`$ and
$`v\in\mathcal{G}\cup\mathcal{Y}`$ can be computed by

1.  1.
    when $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y}),r\in[m],p=5`$, for $`g\in\mathcal{G}`$,
    let $`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi_{j,y}`$ be the combination with
    $`\phi_{1}=g`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,2}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{g}\rangle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,g}^{-,(t)})`$ |  | (38) |
2.  2.
    when $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y}),r\in[m],p=5`$, for $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$,
    let $`\varphi_{j,y}\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$ be the combination, we have

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,5}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{y}\rangle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,y}^{-,(t)})`$ |  | (39) |
3.  3.
    For any combination of $`p`$, $`v`$ not included above, we have due
    to our assumptions of the distribution and update rule:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{v}\rangle\equiv 0`$ |  |

#### C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases

We define the following intermediate time-steps:

###### Definition C.9 (Phase decomposition).

Define timestamps for $`\psi`$ using the feature:

1.  1.
    Phase I: $`t\in[0,T^{1}_{\psi}]`$, where
    $`T^{1}_{\psi}:=\min\{t\geq 0:\underline{V}^{(t)}_{\psi}\geq d^{c_{1}}\mu\}`$.
2.  2.
    Phase II.1: $`t\in(T^{1}_{\psi},T^{2,1}_{\psi}]`$, where
    $`T^{2,1}_{\psi}:=\min\{t\geq 0:V^{(t)}_{\psi}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\log d\}`$.
3.  3.
    Phase II.2: $`t\in(T^{2,1}_{\psi},T^{2,2}_{\psi}]`$, where
    $`T^{2,2}_{\psi}:=\min\{t\geq 0:\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}}]\leq d^{-1/2}\}`$.
4.  4.
    Phase II.3: $`t\in(T^{2,2}_{\psi},T^{2}_{\psi}]`$, where
    $`T^{2}_{\psi}=\min\{T^{2,3}_{\psi},T_{2,1}+O(\frac{1}{\eta(d^{c_{1}}\mu)^{q-2}})\}`$,
    and $`T^{2,3}_{\psi}`$ is defined by

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    T^{2,3}_{\psi}:=\min\{t\geq 0:\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\text{ holds, where }\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu,\delta_{2}=\widetilde{O}((d\varpi/\lambda)^{\frac{1}{q-1}})\}
    ``` |  |
5.  5.
    Phase III.1: $`t\in(T^{2}_{\psi},T_{1,1}]`$, where
    $`T_{1,1}=T_{\psi_{n_{y}^{2}}}^{2}`$ and $`\psi_{n_{y}^{2}}`$ is the
    last feature in $`\Sigma^{\star}`$.
6.  6.
    Phase III.2: $`t\in(T_{1,1},T_{1}]`$, the end of the training where
    the feature shape has stabilized

We give several induction hypotheses, each characterizing different
aspects of the process.

###### Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions).

Let $`t\leq T_{1}`$. The following holds:

1.  1.
    $`\underline{V}_{\psi}^{(t)}+b_{i,j,r}\geq\Omega(\mu)`$ for all
    $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$.
2.  2.
    $`T_{\psi}^{2}<T_{\psi^{\prime}}^{1}`$ if
    $`\psi\prec\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. Thus the intervals
    $`\{[T^{1}_{\psi},T^{2}_{\psi}]\}_{\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}}`$ are
    non-overlapping.
3.  3.
    Let $`\psi^{\prime}\prec\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, then the feature
    shape
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi^{\prime}}(d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0},\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$
    holds throughout $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{1}]`$;
4.  4.
    For any $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ There are at most
    $`\widetilde{O}(\sqrt{d}/\eta)`$ iterations where
    $`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}`$ conditioned on
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}`$ in $`t\in[0,T_{\psi}^{1}]`$.

##### Interpretations of [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").

We explain what the inductions hypothesis mean here:

1.  1.
    This property simply asserts that the *good* features never shrink
    below a certain level which is crucial for the optimization to work.
2.  2.
    This property describes the *curriculum* nature of
    $`\Sigma^{\star}`$: each feature $`V_{\psi}`$ grows in different
    time intervals within $`0\leq t\leq T_{1}`$.
3.  3.
    This property asserts that the feature $`\psi^{\prime}`$ previous to
    $`\psi`$ in the curriculum $`\Sigma^{\star}`$ has stable and good
    feature shapes, which helps with learning the current feature.
4.  4.
    This property means there are very few iteration where the gradient
    of $`V_{j,r}(\phi),\phi\in\varphi`$ is disturbed before
    $`T_{j,r,\varphi}^{1}`$ is reached.

### C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature

We first prove some properties at the beginning of phase I.

###### Lemma C.1 (Initialization range for features).

Under random initialization and
[Assumption C.1](#A3.Thmassumption1 "Assumption C.1 (Assumption , restated). ‣ Roadmap. ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
with probability at least $`1-o(1)`$, the following holds

1.  1.
    For any $`v\in\mathcal{V}`$, we have
    $`|\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p}^{(0)},e_{x}\rangle|\leq O(\mu/\sqrt{\log d})`$
    for any $`j,r,p`$.
2.  2.
    Let $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star},\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi`$ and
    $`\psi\prec\psi^{\prime}`$, then
    $`V_{\psi}^{(0)}\geq V_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(0)}+\Delta_{0}`$ for a gap
    $`\Delta_{0}=\Omega(\sigma_{0}/\mathrm{polylog}d)`$.
3.  3.
    We have $`\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(0)}(\mathbf{Z})=\Omega(\mu)`$ for all
    $`j\in[d],r\in[m]`$.

###### Proof.

The proof is basically the same as that of
[Fact B.3](#A2.Thmfact3 "Fact B.3 (initialization range). ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
The first one uses basic concentration properties of Gaussian
distribution and the second uses a union bound with the
anti-concentration property of the Gaussian distribution. (c) is based
on the fact that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(0)}(\mathbf{Z})=b_{5,j,r}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{1,0}}\sum_{p\in[5]}\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p}^{(0)},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k},p}\rangle=\mu\pm O(\mu/\sqrt{\log d})
``` |  |

which concludes the proof. ∎

###### Corollary C.1.

For any $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ and $`y\in\varphi^{2}`$,
we have with probability $`\geq 1-o(1)`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{|\varphi|}\sum_{g\in\varphi^{1}}\mathbb{E}_{x}[\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(0)})\mid\mathcal{H}_{g,y}]-\mathbb{E}_{x}[\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(V_{j,r}^{(0)}(y)+V_{j,r}(x)+b_{5,j,r})]\right|`$ |  |
````
