````math
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{G}/n_{Y}}})\mathbb{E}_{x}[\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(V_{j,r}^{(0)}(y)+V_{j,r}(x)+b_{5,j,r})]`$ |  |

###### Proof.

This is true because of
[lemma C.1](#A3.Thmlemma1 "Lemma C.1 (Initialization range for features). ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
and Hoeffding’s inequality. ∎

Now we present an induction for phase I.

###### Induction C.2.

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, for $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$,
the gradients satisfy
$`\Gamma_{j,r,y}^{+,(t)}\geq\Omega(\frac{n_{G}}{n_{Y}})\Gamma_{i,j,g}^{+,(t)}`$
for any $`(g,y)\in\varphi`$.

Using
[Fact B.1](#A2.Thmfact1 "Fact B.1 (gradient computation). ‣ B.1.3 Technical Calculations ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
with a representative $`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi_{j,y}`$ and the definition
$`V_{j,r}(\phi)=\tfrac{1}{2}(V_{j,r}(g)+V_{j,r}(y))`$, a single gradient
step with step size $`\eta`$ yields

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}^{(t+1)}(\phi)-V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{2}\Big(\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,5}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{y}\rangle+\langle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,2}}\mathsf{Loss},e_{g}\rangle\Big)`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{2}\Big(\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}+\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,\phi}-\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,g}-\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,y}\Big)`$ |  | (40) |

Similar to the proof of the simply transitive actions, we define a proxy
term and argue that the trajectory of $`V_{\psi}`$ is similar to the
proxy.

###### Lemma C.2 (Approximating the gradient with proxy).

Assuming
[Inductions C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [C.2](#A3.Thminduction2 "Induction C.2. ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at $`t`$. Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Psi`$, for every
$`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi`$, we define two proxy sequences
$`\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(v),v\in\{g,y\}`$ by the update rule:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{V}^{(t+1)}_{j,r}(y)=\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)+\tfrac{\eta}{2}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{+,(t)}_{\psi},\quad\widetilde{V}^{(t+1)}_{j,r}(g)=\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(g)+\tfrac{\eta}{2}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{+,(t)}_{j,r,g};
``` |  |

where both $`\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(g)`$ and $`\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(y)`$
start from
$`\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j,r}(y):=V^{(0)}_{j,r}(y),\ \widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j,r}(g):=V^{(0)}_{j,r}(g)`$.
And the $`\widetilde{\Gamma}`$ are defined by replacing the features of
$`V_{j,r}(v),v\in\varphi^{1}\cup\{y\}`$ with $`\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(v)`$.
Now for all $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(\phi)-V^{(t)}_{j,r}(\phi)|\leq\widetilde{O}(\mu/d^{\frac{1}{2}-3c_{1}}),\quad\text{ where }\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(\phi)=\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(g)+\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(y).
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Fix $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi_{j,y})`$.
([40](#A3.E40 "Equation 40 ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)-V^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)|=\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,y},\qquad|\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(g)-V^{(t)}_{j,r}(g)|=\tfrac{\eta}{2}\Gamma^{-,(t)}_{j,r,g}
``` |  |

Since
[Induction C.2](#A3.Thminduction2 "Induction C.2. ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at $`t`$, we have that
$`\widetilde{V}^{(t+1)}_{j,r}(y)-\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)\geq\Omega(\frac{n_{G}}{n_{Y}})(\widetilde{V}^{(t+1)}_{j,r}(g)-\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(g))`$.
Moreover, since
$`\sum_{g\in\varphi^{1}}\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,g}=\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,y}`$
by
[Definition C.7](#A3.Thmdefinition7 "Definition C.7 (gradient notation). ‣ C.1.2 Facts and Basic Calculations ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
This, combined with
[Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
implies that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{V}^{(t+1)}_{j,r}(y)-\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)\geq\sum_{g\in\varphi^{1}}\Gamma^{+,(t)}_{j,r,g}\geq\Omega(\frac{n_{G}}{n_{Y}})(\widetilde{V}^{(t+1)}_{j,r}(g)-\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(g)),\quad\forall g\in\varphi^{1}
``` |  |

The above update bound imply, for all $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$, the
following property hold:
$`\widetilde{V}_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-\widetilde{V}_{j,r}^{(0)}(g)\leq O(\frac{1}{n_{G}/n_{Y}})(\widetilde{V}_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-\widetilde{V}_{j,r}^{(0)}(y))`$
for all $`g\in\varphi^{1}`$. Now, by the same induction procedure in the
proof of
[Lemma B.2](#A2.Thmlemma2 "Lemma B.2 (Approximating gradient with proxy). ‣ B.2.1 Competition between feature combinations. ‣ B.2 Phase I: Feature Emergence and Competition ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we know that the sum

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left|V^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)+\sum_{g\in\varphi^{1}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)+\sum_{g\in\varphi^{1}}\widetilde{V}_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)\right|\leq O(\mu/d^{\frac{1}{2}-3c_{1}})`$ |  |

And then by the above relations we know

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)-V^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)|\leq O(\mu/d^{\frac{1}{2}-3c_{1}})
``` |  |

Thus the conclusion holds. ∎

###### Lemma C.3 (competition).

Let $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ Assuming
[Inductions C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [C.2](#A3.Thminduction2 "Induction C.2. ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds at $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$. Then at $`t=T_{\psi}^{1}`$, we have for
any feature $`\psi^{\prime}\in\Psi`$, $`\psi^{\prime}\succ\psi`$,
$`\overline{V}_{\psi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$.

###### Proof.

From
[lemma C.2](#A3.Thmlemma2 "Lemma C.2 (Approximating the gradient with proxy). ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we know that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{V}^{(t+1)}_{j,r}(y)=\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)\geq\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{g\in\varphi^{1}}\mathbb{E}_{x}[(\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(y)+\widetilde{V}^{(t)}_{j,r}(g)+b_{5,j,r}+V_{j,r}(x))^{q-1}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{g,y}}]
``` |  |

Suppose there is another
$`\psi^{\prime}=(\varphi^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ such
that $`\psi^{\prime}\succ\psi`$, then we can also write a comparison
based on
[lemma C.1](#A3.Thmlemma1 "Lemma C.1 (Initialization range for features). ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"):

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j,r}(y)\geq\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j,r}(y^{\prime})+\Delta_{0},`$ |  |

And therefore one can compute by absolutely bounding the average over
$`g\in\varphi`$ using
[corollary C.1](#A3.Thmcorollary1 "Corollary C.1. ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"):

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(0)}=\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{Y}|\varphi^{1}|}\sum_{g\in\varphi^{1}}\mathbb{E}_{x}[(\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j,r}(y)+\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j,r}(g)+b_{5,j,r}+V_{j,r}(x))^{q-1}]`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\geq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{Y}}(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{G}/n_{Y}}})\mathbb{E}_{x}[(\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j,r}(y)+b_{5,j,r}+V_{j,r}(x))^{q-1}]`$ |  |

Similarly, for
$`\psi^{\prime}=(j^{\prime},r^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})`$ and
$`\phi^{\prime}\in\varphi^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{\psi^{\prime}}^{+,(0)}=\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\varphi^{\prime,1}|}\sum_{g\in\varphi^{\prime,1}}\mathbb{E}_{x}[(\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j^{\prime},r^{\prime}}(y^{\prime})+\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j^{\prime},r^{\prime}}(g)+b_{5,j^{\prime},r^{\prime}}+V_{j^{\prime},r^{\prime}}(x))^{q-1}\mid\mathcal{H}_{g,y^{\prime}}]`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{G}/n_{Y}}})\mathbb{E}_{x}[(\widetilde{V}^{(0)}_{j^{\prime},r^{\prime}}(y^{\prime})+b_{5,j^{\prime},r^{\prime}}+V_{j^{\prime},r^{\prime}}(x))^{q-1}]`$ |  |

Now we can compare the proxies $`\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(y)+b_{5,j,r}`$ with
$`\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(y^{\prime})`$ using
[lemma B.12](#A2.Thmlemma12 "Lemma B.12 (TPM in expectation). ‣ B.5 Auxiliary Technical Tools ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
to get that when $`V_{j,r}(y)\geq d^{c_{1}}\mu`$ we have
$`V_{j^{\prime},r^{\prime}}(y^{\prime})\leq\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$. Now due
to
[lemma C.2](#A3.Thmlemma2 "Lemma C.2 (Approximating the gradient with proxy). ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[Induction C.2](#A3.Thminduction2 "Induction C.2. ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have the desired result. ∎

###### Proof of [Inductions C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization") and [C.2](#A3.Thminduction2 "Induction C.2. ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization") in Phase I.

Fix $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. We check items (a)-(d) in
[Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"):

- •
  [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a:
  This is simple by combining
  [lemmas C.2](#A3.Thmlemma2 "Lemma C.2 (Approximating the gradient with proxy). ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  and [C.1](#A3.Thmlemma1 "Lemma C.1 (Initialization range for features). ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
  where each $`V_{j,r}(\phi)`$ is well approximated by a monotonically
  growing proxy $`\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(\phi)`$.
- •
  [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b:
  This is proved later but here
  [lemma C.3](#A3.Thmlemma3 "Lemma C.3 (competition). ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  showed that for any pair $`\psi\prec\psi^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\star}`$,
  that $`T_{\psi}^{1}<T_{\psi^{\prime}}^{1}`$, which is a precondition
  for
  [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b.
- •
  [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c-d:
  These two are proved later in future phases and are not violated in
  phase I.
- •
  [Induction C.2](#A3.Thminduction2 "Induction C.2. ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"):
  We shall show that this is the case. Consider any feature
  $`g\in\varphi^{1}`$, its gradient is clearly bounded by
  $`O(n_{Y}/n_{G})\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}`$ initially before the feature
  $`V_{j,r}(g)\geq\Omega(\mu)`$. In this range the difference of its
  growth with the update of $`V_{j,r}(y)`$ averaged over all
  $`g\in\varphi^{1}`$ is bounded by
  $`O(n_{Y}/n_{G})(\widetilde{V}_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)+b_{5,j,r})^{q-1}`$
  because it grows slower than $`O(n_{Y}/n_{G})\widetilde{V}_{j,r}(y)`$
  and thus Hoeffding’s inequality with variance parameter $`\mu`$ would
  work to bound the difference between the averaged gradient and the
  gradient with $`V_{j,r}(g)`$ in expectation, and thus we obtain a
  bound
  $`\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}\geq\Omega(n_{G}/n_{Y})\Gamma_{j,r,g}^{+.(t)}`$.
  When $`V_{j,r}(g)\geq\Omega(\mu)`$, the feature $`V_{j,r}(y)`$ has
  grown more than $`\Omega(n_{G}/n_{Y})\mu`$ and the growth ratio still
  holds. This proved the induction for $`t\leq T_{\psi}^{1}`$.

∎

### C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations

Below we present an additional induction hypothesis for phase II.

###### Induction C.3 (Phase II).

For all features $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ and
$`t\in[T^{1}_{\psi},T^{2}_{\psi}]`$, then for any
$`g^{\prime}\notin\varphi^{1},y^{\prime}\notin\varphi^{2}`$, we have
$`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime}),V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y^{\prime})\in(-B-\widetilde{O}(\mu),\widetilde{O}(\mu))`$.

#### C.3.1 Technical Lemmas

###### Lemma C.4 (gradient estimation).

Assume
[Inductions C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [C.3](#A3.Thminduction3 "Induction C.3 (Phase II). ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi_{j,y})\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, let
$`\phi=(g,y)\in\varphi_{j,y}`$, then at
$`t\in[T^{1}_{\psi},T^{2}_{\psi}]`$, we have

1.  1.
    For $`t\in[T^{1}_{\psi},T^{2,1}_{\psi}]`$, we have
    $`\Gamma_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}))\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\underline{V}_{\psi}^{(t)})`$;
2.  2.
    If $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\in(\frac{1}{3}\log d,\frac{2}{3}\log d)`$,
    then
    $`\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{(t)}=\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{-,(t)}\geq\Omega(\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{H}_{\phi}))`$;
3.  3.
    For any $`g^{\prime}\notin\varphi^{1}`$ and any
    $`y^{\prime}\neq y`$,

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,g^{\prime}}^{(t)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}}\big]\pm O(\varpi n_{Y}/n_{G}),`$ |  |
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,y^{\prime}}^{(t)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\sum_{g\in\varphi^{1}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(t)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g,y^{\prime})}}\big]\pm O(\varpi).`$ |  |
4.  4.
    Any individual component
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(v),v\in\varphi^{1}\cup\varphi^{2}`$ cannot fall
    below $`O(\mu)`$, and $`\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}\geq 0`$.

###### Proof.

Write $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi_{j,y})`$.

1.  1.
    By the definition of $`T_{\psi}^{2,1}`$, we know there exist
    $`\phi\in\varphi`$ such that
    $`V_{j,r}(\phi)\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\log d`$. By the same arguments in
    [lemma C.2](#A3.Thmlemma2 "Lemma C.2 (Approximating the gradient with proxy). ‣ C.2 Phase I: Emergence of the Feature ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
    we can get that all $`\phi\in\varphi`$ satisfy
    $`\tfrac{1}{2}\log d+o(1)`$. Given that
    [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
    holds at $`t`$, conditioned on $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$, all other
    neuron $`(j^{\prime},r^{\prime})\neq(j,r)`$ has activation smaller
    than $`\varrho`$. Therefore $`F_{5,j^{\prime}}\leq o(1)`$, and
    $`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j^{\prime}}=o(1)`$ for any $`j^{\prime}\neq j`$
    conditioned on $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$. So conditioned on
    $`\mathbf{Z}\in\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$, when
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\leq\frac{2}{3}\log d`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}=\frac{e^{F_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z})}}{e^{F_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z})}+(1+o(1))(d-1)}\leq O(d^{-\Omega(1)})`$ |  |

    Now we can simply bound all $`\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{-,(t)}`$ for
    $`v\in\varphi^{1}\cup\varphi^{2}`$ to be smaller than
    $`\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}`$. Thus
    the proof is obtained by computing $`\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}`$ and
    sum to get $`\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}`$.
2.  2.
    Similar to (a), here we also notice that
    $`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}))=1`$ because
    $`\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z})\in[\varrho,B]`$ is in the linear
    regime. Now we can see that the terms
    $`\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}\geq\Omega(\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{H}_{\phi}))`$
    and $`\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{-,(t)}\leq O(d^{-\Omega(1)})`$ and thus we
    have the result.
3.  3.
    For $`g^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, let
    $`y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}`$ such that
    $`g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y^{\prime}}`$, then we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma^{(t)}_{j,r,g^{\prime}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j})\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})}}]-\sum_{y^{\prime}\neq y^{\prime},y}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y^{\prime})}}]`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}}]`$ |  |

    Since $`\mathcal{H}_{(g^{\prime},y)}`$ is confusing, the negative
    term dominates; other appearances contribute at most
    $`O(\varpi n_{Y}/n_{G})`$or $`O(\mu^{q-1})`$. Similar arguments hold
    for $`y^{\prime}`$.
4.  4.
    This one is proven similarly to (a) but can adapt to any level of
    $`V_{j,r}(\phi)`$ for the corresponding $`\phi\in\varphi`$ for
    $`v\in\varphi^{1}\cup\varphi^{2}`$. From
    [Induction C.3](#A3.Thminduction3 "Induction C.3 (Phase II). ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c
    we know that all other feature
    $`V_{j,r}(v^{\prime})\leq\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$ for
    $`v^{\prime}\notin\varphi^{1}\cup\varphi^{2}`$. So for any
    $`v\in\varphi^{1}\cup\varphi^{2}`$ such that
    $`V_{j,r}(v)\leq\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$, we have that the combination
    $`\phi=(v,v^{\prime})\in\Phi\setminus\varphi`$ has
    $`V_{j,r}(\phi)<O(\mu)`$ and thus have gradient

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{(t)}=\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}-\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{-,(t)}\geq\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}-O(\mu^{q-1})`$ |  |

    Note that $`\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}`$ is either $`\geq\lambda/d`$ or
    $`0`$ at this phase. So we can sum up the negative gradient
    $`-O(\mu^{q-1})`$ for all iterations
    $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{\psi}^{2}]`$ (which is fewer than
    $`\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta(d^{c_{1}}\mu)^{q-2}})`$) to get that
    the feature $`V_{j,r}(v)\gg\mu`$. This means
    $`\Lambda_{5,j,r}\geq 0`$ when $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$ happens and
    thus $`\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{+,(t)}\geq 0`$ for all
    $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{1},T_{\psi}^{2}]`$.

∎

###### Lemma C.5 (feature magnitude).

Assume
[Inductions C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [C.3](#A3.Thminduction3 "Induction C.3 (Phase II). ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds. For any feature $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, we have

1.  1.
    when $`\mathcal{C}^{+}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ holds for some
    $`\delta\in[n_{G}\varpi,O(\lambda/d^{c_{1}})]`$ at
    $`t\geq T^{2,1}_{\psi}`$, we have
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\geq B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu),\forall\phi\in\varphi`$.
2.  2.
    when $`\mathcal{C}^{-}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ holds for some
    $`\delta\in[n_{G}\varpi,O(\lambda/d^{1+c_{1}})]`$ at
    $`t\geq T^{2,1}_{\psi}`$, we have
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\in[-B-O(\mu),\widetilde{O}((\frac{d\delta}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})]`$
    for all $`\phi\in\Phi^{\dagger}_{\varphi}`$.

###### Proof.

1.  1.
    If $`\mathcal{C}^{+}_{\psi}(\delta)`$ holds with
    $`\delta\leq O(\lambda/d^{c_{1}})`$, then there are two probability:
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)`$ for all $`\phi\in\varphi`$ is as small as
    $`O(\delta^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$ which is not possible for $`\delta`$
    so small because of
    [lemma C.4](#A3.Thmlemma4 "Lemma C.4 (gradient estimation). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a;
    or that $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\geq B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$ such that
    $`F_{5,j}\geq B`$ and thus the gradient has vanished for some
    events.
2.  2.
    The logic from (a) can be applied here as well. Since the positive
    gradient is at most $`O(\varpi)`$ by
    [lemma C.4](#A3.Thmlemma4 "Lemma C.4 (gradient estimation). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b,
    thus whenever $`\delta\gg\varpi`$, the negative gradient conditioned
    on $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi},\phi\in\Phi^{\dagger}_{\varphi}`$ is
    bounded, which gives

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{\phi\in\varphi}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}]\leq O(\delta)`$ | $`\displaystyle\implies(\Lambda_{5,j,r})^{q-1}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\phi}}\leq\delta\lambda/(d\mathbf{Pr}(\mathcal{H}_{\phi}))`$ |  | (because [Fact B.2](#A2.Thmfact2 "Fact B.2 (logit lower bound). ‣ B.1.3 Technical Calculations ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")) |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\implies V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\lesssim(\frac{n_{G}n_{Y}\lambda\delta}{d})^{\frac{1}{q-1}},\forall\phi\in\varphi`$ |  |  |

    which proves the desired result.

∎

###### Lemma C.6 (gradient stationarity).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, then for any
$`\delta>\varpi^{q-2}`$, the followings hold:

- (a)
  Define
  $`\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\delta}:=\{t\in[T^{2,1}_{\psi},T^{2,3}_{\psi}]\mid\mathcal{C}^{+}_{\psi}(\delta)\text{ doesn't hold}\}`$
  then
  $`|\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\delta}|\leq O(\frac{\log^{2}d}{\eta\delta})`$;
- (b)
  Define
  $`\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\delta}:=\{t\in[T^{2,1}_{\psi},T^{2,3}_{\psi}]\mid\mathcal{C}^{-}_{\psi}(\delta)\text{ doesn't hold}\}`$
  then $`|B^{-}_{\delta}|\leq O(\frac{\log^{3}d}{\eta\delta})`$;
- (c)
  For some
  $`t=T_{\psi}^{2,3}\geq T^{2,1}_{\psi}+O(\frac{1}{\eta n_{G}\varpi})`$,
  we have $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\geq B-O(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$ for all
  $`\phi\in\varphi`$ and
  $`|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi^{\prime})|\leq\widetilde{O}((\frac{d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$
  for all $`\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\dagger}_{\varphi}`$, moreover, this
  will hold until $`t=T_{\psi}^{2}`$

###### Proof.

The proof is very similar in spirit to the proof of
[Lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
We first define the following quantity:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\psi}^{(t)}=V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-\sum_{g\notin\varphi^{1}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)`$ |  |

One can compute that the update of $`\Upsilon_{\psi}^{(t)}`$ is simply

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\eta\bigg(\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}-\sum_{\varphi^{\prime}\neq\varphi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}}\sum_{\phi\in\varphi^{\prime}}\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}+\sum_{\phi^{\prime}\notin\varphi\cup\Phi_{\varphi}^{\dagger}}\Gamma_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{-,(t)}\bigg)`$ |  |

The middle term is bounded by $`O(\varpi)`$ and the last term is bounded
below by $`-O(\varpi)`$, thus we have that the update satisfy

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\psi}^{(t+1)}-\Upsilon_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq\eta\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}-O(\eta\varpi)`$ |  |

Since $`\Gamma_{\psi}^{+,(t)}\geq 0`$ For any $`\delta\gg\varpi`$ by
[lemma C.4](#A3.Thmlemma4 "Lemma C.4 (gradient estimation). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c,
we know that the above bound accumulate at $`t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,1}`$ at

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\psi}^{(t)}-\Upsilon_{\psi}^{(T_{\psi}^{2,1})}\geq\sum_{s\in[T_{\psi}^{2,1},t]\cup B^{+}_{\delta}}\eta\delta-O(\eta\varpi)`$ |  |

So for no more than $`O(\frac{\log^{2}d}{\eta\delta})`$ iterations we
should have the that $`\Upsilon^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\log^{2}d)`$, which is
impossible in our setting. This contradiction proves the result for (a).
The proof of (b) is similar to (a) and the proof of
[lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b.
(c) is also similar to
[lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c
by using
[lemma C.5](#A3.Thmlemma5 "Lemma C.5 (feature magnitude). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-b
with parameter $`\lambda/d^{c_{1}}`$ and parameter $`n_{G}\varpi`$,
alonge with
[lemma C.7](#A3.Thmlemma7 "Lemma C.7 (Symmetry of features). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
We do not repeat the details here. ∎

###### Lemma C.7 (Symmetry of features).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ and
$`t\geq T_{\psi}^{2,3}`$, we have that if
$`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}(\delta)`$ hold for some $`\delta>0`$, then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-C_{\alpha}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)|\leq O(\delta)`$ |  |

For some
$`C_{\alpha}=\frac{1+n_{G}(n_{Y}-1)/n_{Y}}{(n_{Y}-1)+n_{G}/n_{Y}}`$.

###### Proof.

For a feature $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, their update can
be represented as:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-V_{j,r}^{(0)}(g)`$ | $`\displaystyle=U_{g,y}-\sum_{y^{\prime}\neq y}R_{g,y^{\prime}}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-V_{j,r}^{(0)}(y)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}U_{g,y}-\sum_{g^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}R_{g^{\prime},y}`$ |  |

where $`U_{g,y}=\sum_{s\leq t}\eta\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(s)}`$ and
$`R_{g,y^{\prime}}`$ is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle R_{g,y^{\prime}}=\sum_{s\leq t}\eta\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(s)}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(s)})\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{g^{\prime},y}}]`$ |  |

Therefore:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)=\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}U_{g,y}-\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}\sum_{y^{\prime}\neq y}R_{g,y^{\prime}}`$ |  |

Thus it is true for any $`\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$
that the following holds

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-V_{j,r}^{(0)}(y)+\sum_{g^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}R_{g^{\prime},y}=\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-V_{j,r}^{(0)}(g))+\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}\sum_{y^{\prime}\neq y}R_{g,y^{\prime}}`$ |  |

By summing both the left and right hand side with all the
$`(\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},y)`$ pairs, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-V_{j,r}^{(0)}(y))+\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\sum_{g^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}R_{g^{\prime},y}=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-V_{j,r}^{(0)}(g))+\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{y^{\prime}:\tau(g\cdot y^{\prime})\neq j}R_{g,y^{\prime}}`$ |  |

Since
$`\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\sum_{g^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}R_{g^{\prime},y}=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{y^{\prime}:\tau(g\cdot y^{\prime})\neq j}R_{g,y^{\prime}}`$,
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-V_{j,r}^{(0)}(y))=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}(V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)-V_{j,r}^{(0)}(g))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\implies\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)=\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)\pm O(\mu)`$ |  |

Moreover, we have assumed

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y^{\prime})|=O(\delta),\quad\forall y^{\prime}\neq y,g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)+V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g^{\prime})|=O(\delta),\quad\forall g^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ |  |

Thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle(1+\frac{|\mathcal{G}|}{|\mathcal{Y}|}(|\mathcal{Y}|-1))V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)=(\frac{|\mathcal{Y}|(|\mathcal{Y}|-1)}{|\mathcal{G}|}+1)\sum_{g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)\pm O(n_{G}\delta)`$ |  |

Therefore choosing whatever $`g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,t}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle(1+\frac{|\mathcal{G}|}{|\mathcal{Y}|}(|\mathcal{Y}|-1))V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)`$ | $`\displaystyle=(\frac{|\mathcal{Y}|^{2}}{|\mathcal{G}|}+1)\frac{|\mathcal{G}|}{|\mathcal{Y}|}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)\pm O(n_{G}\delta_{2})=(|\mathcal{Y}|-1+\frac{|\mathcal{G}|}{|\mathcal{Y}|})V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)\pm O(n_{G}\delta)`$ |  |

Now by dividing the right-hand side by the factor on
$`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)`$ on the LHS, we have the desired result. ∎

#### C.3.2 Proof of Induction

###### Lemma C.8 (Arrival times).

Assuming
[Inductions C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [C.3](#A3.Thminduction3 "Induction C.3 (Phase II). ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
for $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, we have

1.  1.
    $`T^{2,1}_{\psi}-T^{1}_{\psi}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta(d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0})^{q-2}})`$;
2.  2.
    $`T^{2,2}_{\psi}-T^{2,1}_{\psi}\leq O(\frac{d^{\frac{1}{2}+c_{1}}}{\eta})`$;
3.  3.
    $`T^{2,3}_{\psi}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta\varpi})`$;
4.  4.
    $`T^{2}_{\psi}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta(d^{2c_{1}}\mu)^{q-2}})`$.

###### Proof.

1.  1.
    By
    [lemma C.4](#A3.Thmlemma4 "Lemma C.4 (gradient estimation). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a,
    after phase I, within
    $`\widetilde{O}(1/(\eta(d^{c_{1}}\sigma_{0})^{q-2}))`$ we have
    $`V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)\geq\frac{1}{2}\log d`$ for all
    $`\phi\in\varphi`$.
2.  2.
    On $`[T^{1},T^{2,1}]`$,
    [lemma C.6](#A3.Thmlemma6 "Lemma C.6 (gradient stationarity). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
    gives the rate $`O(d^{\frac{1}{2}+c_{1}}/\eta)`$ time for the
    positive grad to arrive at
    $`1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}`$ conditioned on
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\varphi}`$.
3.  3.
    This is again provided by
    [lemma C.6](#A3.Thmlemma6 "Lemma C.6 (gradient stationarity). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c
    by using $`\delta=n_{G}\varpi`$, combined with
    [lemma C.5](#A3.Thmlemma5 "Lemma C.5 (feature magnitude). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b
    for the feature shape guarantees.
4.  4.
    This is simply by the definition of $`T_{\psi}^{2}`$ and that
    $`T_{\psi}^{2,3}\ll\frac{1}{\eta(d^{2c_{1}}\mu)^{q-2}}`$.

∎

###### Proof of [Inductions C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization") and [C.3](#A3.Thminduction3 "Induction C.3 (Phase II). ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").

Fix $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi_{j,y})`$ and consider
$`t\in[T^{1}_{\psi},T^{2}_{\psi}]`$, then

- •
  [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a:
  This is guaranteed by
  [lemma C.4](#A3.Thmlemma4 "Lemma C.4 (gradient estimation). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-b.
- •
  [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b:
  Because the time used is small between $`T_{\psi}^{1}`$ and
  $`T_{\psi}^{2}`$ by
  [lemma C.8](#A3.Thmlemma8 "Lemma C.8 (Arrival times). ‣ C.3.2 Proof of Induction ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
  we have that $`T_{\psi^{\prime}}^{1}`$ is still behind when this
  happens.
- •
  [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c:
  This will be proven in phase III, we do not use any properties that
  violate this hypothesis.
- •
  [Induction C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")d:
  This is due to
  [Induction C.3](#A3.Thminduction3 "Induction C.3 (Phase II). ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a-b
  and
  [lemma C.6](#A3.Thmlemma6 "Lemma C.6 (gradient stationarity). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
  with parameter $`\delta=1/\sqrt{d}`$.
- •
  [Induction C.3](#A3.Thminduction3 "Induction C.3 (Phase II). ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"):
  For any $`\phi\notin\varphi`$, we have that they are either desirable
  features $`\phi\in\varphi_{j,y^{\prime}}`$ for some different
  $`y^{\prime}\geq y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, or that they are the wrong
  features for the class $`j`$, i.e., $`\phi\in\varphi_{j^{\prime},y}`$
  for all $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$. The feature
  $`\psi^{\prime}=(j,r,\varphi_{j,y^{\prime}})`$ in the former case was
  out-competed by $`\psi=(j,r,\phi)`$ so their growth is bounded by
  $`\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$ in the first two stage and negative in the end
  due to the feature shape
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(d^{c_{1}}\mu,(\frac{d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$
  at the end as shown in
  [lemma C.6](#A3.Thmlemma6 "Lemma C.6 (gradient stationarity). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c.
  Combination $`\phi`$ in the latter case will be smaller as well due to
  [lemma C.6](#A3.Thmlemma6 "Lemma C.6 (gradient stationarity). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c.

The time bounds follow from
[lemma C.8](#A3.Thmlemma8 "Lemma C.8 (Arrival times). ‣ C.3.2 Proof of Induction ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
This proves the induction in phase II for any feature
$`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. ∎

### C.4 Phase III: Convergence

We present the induction hypothesis in this phase.

###### Induction C.4 (Phase III, final).

Let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, for
$`t\in[T_{\psi}^{2},T_{1}]`$, the following holds:

1.  1.
    At $`t\in[T_{\psi}^{2},T_{1,1}]`$, we have
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ holds with
    $`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu`$,
    $`\delta_{2}=\widetilde{O}((\frac{d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$;
2.  2.
    At $`t\in[T_{1,1},T_{1}]`$, we have
    $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ holds with
    $`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu`$,
    $`\delta_{2}=\widetilde{O}((\varpi)^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$.

We obtain the shape of logits at convergence.

###### Lemma C.9 (Logit shape at convergence).

Assuming
[Induction C.4](#A3.Thminduction4 "Induction C.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
Let $`\psi_{n_{y}^{2}}=\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ be the last of
$`\Sigma^{\star}`$, then at $`t\in[T_{\psi_{n_{y}^{2}}}^{2,3},T_{1}]`$,
the followings hold:

1.  1.
    For $`\phi\in\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$,
    $`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\geq 1-O(\lambda)`$ conditioned on
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$.
2.  2.
    For $`\phi\notin\Phi^{\star}_{j}`$,
    $`\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\leq O(\lambda/d)`$ conditioned on
    $`\mathcal{H}_{\phi}`$.

###### Proof.

The proof is very similar to that of
[lemma B.9](#A2.Thmlemma9 "Lemma B.9 (Logit shape at convergence). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we do not repeat here. ∎

###### Lemma C.10 (Gradient bounds, phase III).

Assuming
[Inductions C.1](#A3.Thminduction1 "Induction C.1 (learning symmetric group actions). ‣ C.1.3 Induction Hypothesis and Training Phases ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [C.4](#A3.Thminduction4 "Induction C.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds, and let $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, then at
$`t\in[T_{1,1},T_{1}]`$, the followings hold

1.  1.
    $`\Gamma_{\psi}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\lambda)`$ when
    $`V_{\psi}^{(t)}\leq B-d^{c_{1}}\mu`$;
2.  2.
    $`|\Gamma_{j,r,v}^{-,(t)}|\leq O(\frac{\lambda\varpi}{dn_{y}})`$ for
    any $`v\in\mathcal{G}\cup\mathcal{Y}`$;
3.  3.
    For $`\phi\in\cup_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\varphi_{j,y}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,r,\phi}^{+,(t)}=\begin{cases}-\Theta(\lambda\varpi/n_{y}^{2}),&\text{if }V_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\in[-B+2\mu,-\varpi]\\
    \in[-\Theta(\lambda\varpi/n_{y}^{2}),-\Theta(\lambda\varpi/dn_{y}^{2})]\cup\{0\},&\text{if }V_{j,r,\phi^{\prime}}^{(t)}\in[-B-2\mu,-B+2\mu]\end{cases}`$ |  |

###### Proof.

The proof of (a) is simple as $`\lambda\gg\varpi`$ which is the upper
bound of all other gradient terms. (b) is because of
[lemma C.9](#A3.Thmlemma9 "Lemma C.9 (Logit shape at convergence). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b
and that
[Induction C.4](#A3.Thminduction4 "Induction C.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b
holds which guaranteed suitable feature shape. (c) is simply a
combination of (b) and the
[lemma C.9](#A3.Thmlemma9 "Lemma C.9 (Logit shape at convergence). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a,
and
[Induction C.4](#A3.Thminduction4 "Induction C.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b.
∎

#### C.4.1 Proof of Induction in Phase III and [theorem C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem C.1 (Learning Symmetric Group Actions). ‣ C.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")

###### Proof of [Inductions C.4](#A3.Thminduction4 "Induction C.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization") and [C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem C.1 (Learning Symmetric Group Actions). ‣ C.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").

The proof is similar to the proof of
[Induction B.4](#A2.Thminduction4 "Induction B.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ B.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
thus we leave out some details here.

- •
  [Induction C.4](#A3.Thminduction4 "Induction C.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a:
  We know from
  [Inductions C.3](#A3.Thminduction3 "Induction C.3 (Phase II). ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
  [C.6](#A3.Thmlemma6 "Lemma C.6 (gradient stationarity). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  and [C.8](#A3.Thmlemma8 "Lemma C.8 (Arrival times). ‣ C.3.2 Proof of Induction ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  that
  [Induction C.4](#A3.Thminduction4 "Induction C.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
  holds at $`t=T_{\psi}^{2}`$. The feature shape
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(d^{c_{1}}\mu,\widetilde{O}((\frac{d\varpi}{\lambda})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}))`$
  guaranteed a starting point for reusing the argument in
  [lemma C.6](#A3.Thmlemma6 "Lemma C.6 (gradient stationarity). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c
  to guarantee that the feature stays at the same feature shape.
- •
  [Induction C.4](#A3.Thminduction4 "Induction C.4 (Phase III, final). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b:
  After $`t=T_{1,1}`$, which is $`t=T_{\psi_{n_{y}^{2}}}^{2}`$ for the
  last $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. We shall prove that the feature shape
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(d^{c_{1}}\mu,\widetilde{O}((\varpi)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}))`$
  holds for every $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. Since (a) holds for all
  $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$, we have that at
  $`T_{\psi_{n_{y}^{2}}}^{2,3}`$ that the feature shape in (a) holds for
  all $`\psi=(j,r,\varphi)\in\Sigma^{\star}`$. So by
  [lemma C.10](#A3.Thmlemma10 "Lemma C.10 (Gradient bounds, phase III). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")b
  and similar argument in
  [lemma B.6](#A2.Thmlemma6 "Lemma B.6 (Gradient Stationarity). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")c,
  we can prove that the feature
  $`|V_{j,r}^{(t)}(\phi)|\leq\widetilde{O}(\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$ for
  any $`\phi\in\Phi_{\varphi}^{\dagger}`$, which is
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}(\widetilde{O}(\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}}))`$.
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1}(d^{c_{1}}\mu)`$ is guaranteed by
  [lemma C.10](#A3.Thmlemma10 "Lemma C.10 (Gradient bounds, phase III). ‣ C.4 Phase III: Convergence ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")a
  and $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,3}(\widetilde{O}(\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}}))`$ is
  guaranteed by $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,1},\mathcal{F}_{\psi,2}`$ and
  [lemma C.7](#A3.Thmlemma7 "Lemma C.7 (Symmetry of features). ‣ C.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ C.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
  $`\mathcal{F}_{\psi,4}`$ is simple as any feature
  $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\dagger,3}_{\psi}`$ lost the competition and are
  bounded by $`\widetilde{O}(\mu)`$. Moreover, once each
  $`\varphi_{j,y},j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y}),y\in\mathcal{Y}`$ is learned.
  the feature in $`\Sigma^{\dagger,3}_{\psi}`$ has too small gradient
  bounded by $`O(\tfrac{\lambda}{d}\mu^{q-1})`$ which will not have
  sufficient growth before $`t=T_{1}`$.

Since all feature $`\psi\in\Sigma^{\star}`$ have shape
$`\mathcal{F}_{\psi}(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$ for
$`\delta_{1}=d^{c_{1}}\mu`$ and
$`\delta_{2}=\widetilde{O}(\varpi^{\frac{1}{q-1}})`$ at $`t=T_{1}`$, we
have proven
[theorem C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem C.1 (Learning Symmetric Group Actions). ‣ C.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
∎

## Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case

In this section, we consider the case where the group operations form a
simply transitive group. According to
[Assumption 4.1](#S4.Thmassumption1 "Assumption 4.1 (Simply transitive group action). ‣ 4 Learning CoT on Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we assume that for any $`y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}`$, there exists a
unique $`g\in\mathcal{G}`$ such that $`g\cdot y_{1}=y_{2}`$. Without
loss of generality, we let $`\mathcal{Y}=\{0,1,\dots,n_{y}-1\}`$, where
$`n_{y}\in[\Omega(\log\log d),\log d]`$.

We focus on updating only $`\mathbf{Q}`$, while keeping $`\mathbf{W}`$
fixed. Combined with the attention structure specified in
[Assumption 3.5](#S3.Thmassumption5 "Assumption 3.5 (Block-sparse attention matrix). ‣ Additional Technical Assumptions. ‣ 3.3 LEGO Task via CoT Reasoning and Training Objective ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
it suffices to consider the updates to the blocks $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$
and $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$ only. We consider the contribution to the
gradient from the position $`i=5`$ on task $`{\mathcal{T}}^{2}`$;
specifically, the relevant loss function is given by
$`\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,\ell}.`$ As $`\mathbf{W}`$
remains fixed in this section, we omit the superscript $`(t)`$ in
$`\mathbf{W}`$ and in all related notations that depend solely on
$`\mathbf{W}`$ (e.g., $`V_{j,r}`$) for notational simplicity.

### D.1 Gradient Computations

##### Notations for gradient expressions.

We firsr introduce some notations for the gradients of the attention
layer. For $`1\leq\ell\leq L`$, given $`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$ and
$`\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{L,\ell-1}`$, define

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Xi^{L}_{\ell,i,\mathbf{k}}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\triangleq\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{i,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{i,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\big)\langle\mathbf{W}_{i,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k}}\rangle,\quad{i\in[5].}`$ |  | (41) |

For simplicity of notation, we will henceforth omit the dependence on
$`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$ in the notation of
$`\Xi^{L}_{\ell,i,\mathbf{k}}`$ when it is clear from the context.

###### Fact D.1 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}`$).

For any $`p,q\in[5]`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{p,q}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{i\in[5]}-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{p,q}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L,\ell}_{i},\quad\text{ where }`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{p,q}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L,\ell}_{i}=`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{L,\ell-1}}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}}\cdot\left(\Xi^{L}_{\ell,i,\mathbf{k}}-\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}^{L,\ell-1}}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\Xi^{L}_{\ell,i,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\right)\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1,p}\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k},q}^{\top}\Bigg].`$ |  |

###### Lemma D.1 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$).

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,1}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,0})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},1}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg],`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},2}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,1}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,0})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},2}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{0}),s^{\prime}=\tau(x_{1})}\Bigg],`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},1}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1}),s^{\prime}=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

###### Proof.

For $`\ell=1`$, due to
[Fact D.1](#A4.Thmfact1 "Fact D.1 (Gradients of 𝐐). ‣ Notations for gradient expressions. ‣ D.1 Gradient Computations ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
the diagonal entry $`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ with
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, the expected gradient contribution from
$`-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,1}_{5}`$ takes the form

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\Big(\Xi^{2}_{\ell,5,\mathsf{pred},1}-\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}^{2,\ell-1}}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\,\Xi^{2}_{\ell,5,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\Big)\cdot\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{0})}\Big],
``` |  |

which is nonzero in expectation only when $`s=\tau(x_{0})`$. Therefore,
combined the definition of $`\Xi`$ in
([41](#A4.E41 "Equation 41 ‣ Notations for gradient expressions. ‣ D.1 Gradient Computations ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
we have:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,1}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\Big(\Xi^{2}_{\ell,5,\mathsf{pred},1}-\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}^{2,\ell-1}}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\Xi^{2}_{\ell,5,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\Big)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},1}\rangle-\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}^{2,\ell-1}}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\rangle\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},1}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Other quantities are computed similarly, and thus is omitted here. ∎

###### Lemma D.2 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$).

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,1}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,0})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},0}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg],`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},1}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have
$`[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,1}_{5}]_{s,s^{\prime}}=0`$,
and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},0}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1}),s^{\prime}=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

###### Proof.

The analysis is similar to that of
[Lemma D.1](#A4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma D.1 (Gradients of 𝐐_{4,3}). ‣ Notations for gradient expressions. ‣ D.1 Gradient Computations ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
and we omit the details here. ∎

### D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients

In this subsection, we establish several useful bounds on the gradients
of the attention layer, leveraging the feature structure of the MLP
layer learned during stage 1. These bounds will be instrumental for the
subsequent analysis.

As established in
[Lemma B.7](#A2.Thmlemma7 "Lemma B.7 (Symmetry of Features). ‣ B.3.1 Technical Lemmas ‣ B.3 Phase II: Feature Growth and Cancellations ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[Theorem B.1](#A2.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem B.1 (Learning Simply Transitive Actions). ‣ B.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ B.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix B Learning Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
at the end of stage 1, the network exhibits the following activation
properties:

- •
  Sparse activations: For each $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, and any
  feature $`(g,y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$, there exists a unique
  *activated* neuron $`r\in[m]`$ such that, under the event $`g_{1}=g`$
  and $`y_{0}=y`$, the following holds:

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(T_{1})}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq B-O(\delta),\quad|V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(g)-V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(y)|\leq O(\delta)`$ |  |
  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r^{\prime}}^{(T_{1})}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq O(\delta^{q-1})\quad\text{for all }r^{\prime}\neq r.`$ |  |
- •
  Cancellation of incorrect features: let $`r\in[m]`$ be the activated
  neuron associated with $`(g,y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$. Then for any
  $`g^{\prime}\neq g\in\mathcal{G}`$ and any
  $`y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}`$, we have:

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(y^{\prime})\right|`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq O(\delta),`$ |  |
  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(g^{\prime})+V_{j,r}^{(T_{1})}(y)\right|`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq O(\delta),`$ |  |

In the analysis of FFN layer, we have a pair
$`\delta=(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$; in the expressions above, some terms
should use $`\delta_{1}`$ and others $`\delta_{2}`$. For brevity, we
write $`\delta:=\max\{\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\}`$ and bound all such terms
by $`\delta`$. Since both $`\delta_{1}`$ and $`\delta_{2}`$ are small,
this simplification does not affect our analysis.

##### Notations for activated neurons.

Since in the simply transitive case, the feature sets are disjoint
across indices—i.e.,
$`\mathfrak{F}_{j}\cap\mathfrak{F}_{j^{\prime}}=\emptyset`$ for all
$`j\neq j^{\prime}`$—we denote the activated neuron corresponding to
$`(g,y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$ by $`r_{g\cdot y}`$. Moreover, let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\triangleq\cup_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathfrak{A}_{j},\text{ where }\mathfrak{A}_{j}\triangleq\{r\mid\exists(g,y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j},r=r_{g\cdot y}\}.`$ |  |

In other words, $`\mathfrak{A}`$ is the set of all activated neurons
across all feature sets $`\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$ for
$`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$. Given $`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$, letting
$`\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})=\cup\{g_{\ell^{\prime}}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{L}`$
be the collection of all the chosen group elements in the predicate
clauses. Similarly
$`\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}=\cup\{y_{\ell^{\prime}}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-1}`$.
Then define
$`\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})=\{r_{g\cdot y}\mid(g,y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j}\wedge\big(g\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\vee y\in\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}\big)\}`$.
For simplicity, we omit the dependence on $`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$ in
the notation of $`\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ when it is clear from the
context. Equipped with these notations, we can summarize the above
properties in the following lemmas.

###### Lemma D.3 (Properties of target feature magnitude).

Given $`(g,y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$ with $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, then,
the following properties hold.

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g)+V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y)\right)\geq B-O(\delta),\quad\left|V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g)-V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y)\right|\leq O(\delta),`$ |  | (42) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g)+V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y^{\prime})\right|\leq O(\delta),V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y^{\prime})<0\quad\text{for all }y^{\prime}\neq y,`$ |  | (43) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g^{\prime})+V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y)\right|\leq O(\delta),V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g^{\prime})<0\quad\text{for all }g^{\prime}\neq g.`$ |  | (44) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|V_{j,r}(g)\right|,\left|V_{j,r}(y)\right|\leq O(\delta)\quad\text{for all }r\notin\mathfrak{A}_{j}.`$ |  | (45) |

###### Lemma D.4 (Properties of irrelevant magnitude).

If $`(p,v)\notin\{2\}\times\mathcal{G}\cup\{5\}\times\mathcal{Y}`$, or
$`j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, then for any $`r\in[m]`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p},e_{v}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  | (46) |

The above lemmas give us some direct computations of the inner products
between the weight matrices and input embedding vectors.

###### Lemma D.5.

Let $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and $`\ell\in[2]`$. Then for any
$`r\in[m]`$, the following holds:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},\ell}\rangle`$ | $`\displaystyle=V_{j,r}(g_{\ell})\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}),`$ |  | (47) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rangle`$ | $`\displaystyle=V_{j,r}(y_{\ell-1})\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  | (48) |

Moreover, for $`j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and any
$`\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{2,1}`$ and $`r\in[m]`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k}}\rangle\big|=\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  | (49) |

###### Proof.

By direct computations and the definition of
$`\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},\ell}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},\ell}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,1},e_{x_{\ell}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,2},e_{g_{\ell}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,3},e_{x_{\ell-1}}\rangle`$ |  |

Plug in
[Lemma D.4](#A4.Thmlemma4 "Lemma D.4 (Properties of irrelevant magnitude). ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and the definition of $`V_{j,r}(g_{\ell})`$, we obtain
([47](#A4.E47 "Equation 47 ‣ Lemma D.5. ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")).
The proof of
([48](#A4.E48 "Equation 48 ‣ Lemma D.5. ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
and
([49](#A4.E49 "Equation 49 ‣ Lemma D.5. ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
is similar, and we omit the details here. ∎

Furthermore, we can establish some characterizations of the
$`\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1})`$ quantities, which are crucial
for the following analysis.

###### Lemma D.6 (Characterizations of Lambda).

Given $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1}`$ with $`\ell\in[2]`$, with an attention
structure
$`\{\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathbf{k}}\}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{2,\ell-1}}`$,

1.  1.
    for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for activated neuron
    $`r\in\mathfrak{A}_{j}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1})=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{2}\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathsf{pred},\ell^{\prime}}V_{j,r}(g_{\ell^{\prime}})+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathsf{ans},\ell^{\prime}-1}V_{j,r}(y_{\ell^{\prime}-1})\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for any non-activated neuron
    $`r\notin\mathfrak{A}_{j}`$ we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Big|\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1})\Big|\leq{O}(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for $`j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for any $`r\in[m]`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Big|\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1})\Big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |

###### Proof.

Recall the definition of $`\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1})`$ in
([23](#A1.E23 "Equation 23 ‣ Notations for gradient expressions ‣ A.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix A Learning In-Context Retrieval of Variables ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1})=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{2,\ell-1}}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}}\cdot\big\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k}}\big\rangle+b_{5,j,r}.`$ |  |

Thus, the first part follows directly from
([47](#A4.E47 "Equation 47 ‣ Lemma D.5. ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
and
([48](#A4.E48 "Equation 48 ‣ Lemma D.5. ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"));
similarly, the second part holds by plugging
([45](#A4.E45 "Equation 45 ‣ Lemma D.3 (Properties of target feature magnitude). ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
into
([47](#A4.E47 "Equation 47 ‣ Lemma D.5. ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
and
([48](#A4.E48 "Equation 48 ‣ Lemma D.5. ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"));
the last part is a direct consequence of
([49](#A4.E49 "Equation 49 ‣ Lemma D.5. ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
and the fact $`b_{i,j,r}=\sigma_{0}\log d`$.

∎

A direct consequence of the above lemma is the following finer
characterization of the activated neurons.

###### Lemma D.7.

Given $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for
$`r\in\mathfrak{A}_{j}\setminus\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$, we have
$`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)=0`$.

###### Proof.

For $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for $`r\in\mathfrak{A}_{j}`$, by
[Lemma D.6](#A4.Thmlemma6 "Lemma D.6 (Characterizations of Lambda). ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1})=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{2}\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathsf{pred},\ell^{\prime}}V_{j,r}(g_{\ell^{\prime}})+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathsf{ans},\ell^{\prime}-1}V_{j,r}(y_{\ell^{\prime}-1})\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |

By
([45](#A4.E45 "Equation 45 ‣ Lemma D.3 (Properties of target feature magnitude). ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ D.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")),
for for $`r\in\mathfrak{A}_{j}\setminus\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$, we
have
$`V_{j,r}(g_{\ell^{\prime}}),V_{j,r}(y_{\ell^{\prime}-1})\leq-B-O(\delta)`$.
Hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r}=-\Omega(B)\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\ll-\varrho,`$ |  |

which implies $`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)=0`$. ∎

Now we are ready to further derive the gradients of the attention layer
starting from
[Lemmas D.1](#A4.Thmlemma1 "Lemma D.1 (Gradients of 𝐐_{4,3}). ‣ Notations for gradient expressions. ‣ D.1 Gradient Computations ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [D.2](#A4.Thmlemma2 "Lemma D.2 (Gradients of 𝐐_{4,4}). ‣ Notations for gradient expressions. ‣ D.1 Gradient Computations ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and the properties established above.

###### Lemma D.8 (Refined expression for the gradient of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$).

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$, letting
$`j_{1}=\tau(g_{1}(y_{0}))`$ and $`j_{2}=\tau(g_{2}(y_{1}))`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,1}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{1}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{1}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{1},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{1},r}(g_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j_{1},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{1}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s}\Bigg];`$ |  |

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,1}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{1}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{1}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{1},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{1},r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j_{1},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{1}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s,\tau(x_{1})=s^{\prime}}\Bigg];`$ |  |

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(g_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
````
