````math
for $`\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}_{2}`$, we only need to focus on the
output $`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}`$ from the case
$`g_{1}=g_{2}\wedge y_{0}\neq y_{1}`$, and obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\eqref{eq-s23-cyc-gd3}+\eqref{eq-s23-cyc-gd4}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}\cdot\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}\big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\Big(\Big(V_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}(g_{2})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{1})=s\}\cap\{g_{1}=g_{2}\wedge y_{0}\neq y_{1}\}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}\cdot\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}\big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\Big(\Big(V_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}(y_{1})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{1})=s\}\cap\{g_{1}=g_{2}\wedge y_{0}\neq y_{1}\}}\Bigg].`$ |  |

By
[Lemma D.39](#A4.Thmlemma39 "Lemma D.39. ‣ D.5.1 Attention and Logit Preliminaries ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})=O\bigg(\frac{1}{d^{2\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)C_{B}}}\bigg).`$ |  |

Since $`\mathbb{E}[\Delta^{2,2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s]<\alpha`$, where
$`\alpha`$ is sufficiently small, then combining with
([79](#A4.E79 "Equation 79 ‣ Proof. ‣ D.5.3 Non-negative Gap ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})=O(1)\cdot\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,0}).
``` |  |

Therefore, since $`\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{c}`$ happens with
probability $`O(\frac{1}{\log d})`$, we obtain that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\eqref{eq-s23-cyc-gd3}+\eqref{eq-s23-cyc-gd4}\geq-O(\frac{1}{\log d})\Big(\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,3,1,ii}-\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,4,1,ii}\Big).
``` |  |

Putting it all together, we can conclude that when
$`\mathbb{E}[\Delta^{2,2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s]`$ reaches below $`\alpha`$,
the gap in non-decreasing direction is guaranteed, i.e.,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,\ell}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\Big[\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,\ell}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}>0,
``` |  |

which completes the proof. ∎

#### D.5.4 Upper Bound for $`\mathbf{Q}`$

###### Lemma D.43.

If
[Induction D.3](#A4.Thminduction3 "Induction D.3. ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`\in[T_{2,2,s},t)`$, given
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, then at time $`t`$, we have
$`[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,p}]_{s,s}\leq\widetilde{O}(1)`$ for
$`p\in\{3,4\}`$.

###### Proof.

Denote the first time that $`[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ reaches
$`\Omega(\log^{1+c}d)`$ for some small constant $`c>0`$ as
$`\widetilde{T}`$. Then by direct calculations, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},0\to\mathsf{pred},2}\leq O\bigg(\frac{1}{e^{\Omega(\log^{1+c}d)}}\bigg),`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\leq O\bigg(\frac{1}{e^{\Omega(\log^{1+c}d)}}\bigg).`$ |  |

Moreover, by
[Lemma D.37](#A4.Thmlemma37 "Lemma D.37. ‣ D.5.1 Attention and Logit Preliminaries ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we can simply bound the logits as follows:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{j_{1}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq O\Bigg(\frac{1}{d^{C_{B}\big(1-2\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},0\to\mathsf{pred},2}\big)-1}}\Bigg)\leq O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{C_{B}/2-1}}\Big)\text{ for }\mathbf{Z}^{2,0}\in\mathcal{E}_{1};`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle 1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{j_{2}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq O\Bigg(\frac{1}{d^{C_{B}\big(1-2\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-2\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)-1}}\Bigg)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~+O\Bigg(\frac{1}{d^{2\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)C_{B}}}\Bigg)\leq O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{C_{B}/2-1}}\Big)\text{ for }\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}.`$ |  |

Thus, by focusing on $`\mathcal{N}_{s,3,1,i}`$ and
$`\mathcal{N}_{s,4,1,i}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,\ell}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}\Bigg|\leq\frac{1}{d}\cdot O\Big(\frac{1}{d^{C_{B}/2-1}}\Big)\cdot O\bigg(\frac{1}{e^{\Omega(\log^{1+c}d)}}\bigg)\cdot B\leq O\bigg(\frac{\log d}{e^{\Omega(\log^{1+c}d)}d^{C_{B}/2}}\bigg),`$ |  |

which implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}^{(T)}_{4,3}\leq\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}+O\bigg(\frac{T\log d}{e^{\Omega(\log^{1+c}d)}d^{C_{B}/2}}\bigg)\leq\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}+O\bigg(\frac{\mathsf{poly}d\cdot\log d}{e^{\Omega(\log^{1+c}d)}d^{C_{B}/2}}\bigg)\leq O(\log^{1+c}d).`$ |  |

∎

#### D.5.5 Attention Upper Bound

###### Lemma D.44.

If
[Induction D.3](#A4.Thminduction3 "Induction D.3. ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`\in[T_{2,2,s},t)`$, given
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, then at time $`t`$, for any
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}`$, we have
$`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\leq 0.5+\widetilde{c}_{1}`$,
where $`\widetilde{c}_{1}>0`$ is some small constant.

###### Proof.

Let $`\widetilde{T}`$ denote the first time that
$`\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s]`$
reaches $`0.5+\widetilde{c}`$. where $`\widetilde{c}>0`$ is some small
constant s.t., $`2\widetilde{c}\cdot C_{B}>1`$. At $`\widetilde{T}`$, we
have
$`\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},0\to\mathsf{pred},1}\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s}\geq\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}`$
; moreover,
$`\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}`$
and
$`\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},0\to\mathsf{ans},0}`$ is
still at the constant level.

- •
  For $`\ell=1`$, by
  [Lemma D.35](#A4.Thmlemma35 "Lemma D.35. ‣ D.5.1 Attention and Logit Preliminaries ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
  and
  [Lemma D.38](#A4.Thmlemma38 "Lemma D.38. ‣ D.5.1 Attention and Logit Preliminaries ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
  for $`y\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{y_{0}\}`$, we have only
  $`r_{g_{1}\cdot y}`$ has been activted to the linear regime for the
  prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y))`$. Furthermore, we obtain

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{y_{0}\}}\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y))}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{\log d\cdot e^{2\widetilde{c}C_{B}\log d}}{\log d\cdot e^{2\widetilde{c}C_{B}\log d}+O(d)}\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{1}}\Big)`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle=(1-o(1))\cdot\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{1}}\Big).`$ |  |

  Thus,

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,1}\Big]_{s,s}`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\bigg(\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{1}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\Big(V_{j_{1},r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}(g_{1})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{1},r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~-\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{y_{0}\}}\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y))}\cdot\Big(\Big(V_{\tau(g_{1}(y)),r_{g_{1}\cdot y}}(g_{1})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y)),r_{g_{1}\cdot y}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{1}}\Big)\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}^{q})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s}\Bigg]`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\bigg(\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{1}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\Big(2\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot B\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~-\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{y_{0}\}}\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y))}\cdot\Big(\Big(2\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},0}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot B\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{1}}\Big)\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}^{q})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{0})=s}\Bigg]<0.`$ |  |
- •
  For $`\ell=2`$, for
  $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\cup\{g_{1}=g_{2},y_{0}\neq y_{1}x\}`$,
  we have

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{y_{0},y_{1}\}}\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y))}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{\log d\cdot e^{2\widetilde{c}C_{B}\log d}}{\log d\cdot e^{2\widetilde{c}C_{B}\log d}+O(d)}\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}^{\prime}}\Big)`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle=(1-o(1))\cdot\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}^{\prime}}\Big).`$ |  |

  Otherwise, we have

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{y_{1}\}}\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y))}`$ | $`\displaystyle=(1-o(1))\cdot\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}^{\prime}}\Big).`$ |  |

  Therefore, similar to $`\ell=1`$, we obtain
  $`\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\Big]_{s,s}<0`$.

Combing the above two cases, we have
$`\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,\ell}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}<0`$.
It is also clear from
[Lemma D.40](#A4.Thmlemma40 "Lemma D.40. ‣ D.5.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
that
$`\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,\ell}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}\geq 0`$.
Hence
$`\operatorname{\bf Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}`$
cannot further grow once it reaches $`0.5+\widetilde{c}`$. ∎

#### D.5.6 Decreasing Gap at the End of Convergence

Let $`\widetilde{T}`$ denote the first time that
$`\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{2,2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s]\leq 3\epsilon`$,
if
$`\mathbb{E}[\Delta^{2,2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s]\leq O\big(\frac{(d^{1.01}\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}}{\log d}\big)`$,
then we can let $`T^{\star}=\widetilde{T}`$ and stop the training.
Otherwise, we have
$`\mathbb{E}[\Delta^{2,2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s]\geq\Omega\big(\frac{(d^{1.01}\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}}{\log d}\big)`$.
Following the similar argument as in
[Lemma D.40](#A4.Thmlemma40 "Lemma D.40. ‣ D.5.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have the gradient contribution from $`\ell=1`$ is dominated by the
gradient contribution from $`\ell=2`$. Thus, we focus on $`\ell=2`$, and
obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\Big]_{s,s}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\bigg(\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\Big(V_{j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}(g_{2})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}^{q})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{0})=s\}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{y_{1}\}}\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y))}\cdot\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y)),r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}\big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~\cdot\Big(\Big(V_{\tau(g_{2}(y)),r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}(g_{2})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y)),r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{0})=s\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{2})-\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{1})=s\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}_{2}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot O(\epsilon\log d)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Turning to $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\Big]_{s,s}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\bigg(\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\Big(V_{j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}(y_{1})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}^{q})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{0})=s\}}\Bigg]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\{y_{1}\}}\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y))}\cdot\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y)),r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}\big)`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~\cdot\Big(\Big(V_{\tau(g_{2}(y)),r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}(y_{1})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y)),r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{0})=s\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}\Bigg]`$ |  | (85) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\cdot`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(y_{1})-\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{1})=s\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}_{2}}\Bigg].`$ |  | (86) |

For
([85](#A4.E85 "Equation 85 ‣ D.5.6 Decreasing Gap at the End of Convergence ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | ([85](#A4.E85 "Equation 85 ‣ D.5.6 Decreasing Gap at the End of Convergence ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")) | $`\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\bigg(-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2}}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~\min\bigg\{\Omega(\log d),\Omega\Big(\big((\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1})\log d\big)^{q-1}\log d\Big)\bigg\}\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{0})=s\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot\Omega\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~\min\bigg\{\Omega(\log d),\Omega\Big(\big((\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1})\log d\big)^{q-1}\log d\Big)\bigg\}\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{0})=s\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for
([86](#A4.E86 "Equation 86 ‣ D.5.6 Decreasing Gap at the End of Convergence ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | ([86](#A4.E86 "Equation 86 ‣ D.5.6 Decreasing Gap at the End of Convergence ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")) | $`\displaystyle\geq-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))}\cdot\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}\big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\Big(\Big(V_{\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}(y_{1})-\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{1})=s\}\cap\{g_{1}\neq g_{2}\wedge y_{0}=y_{1}\}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\frac{1}{\log d}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\big(\epsilon\log d\big)^{q-1}\Big)\mathds{1}_{\{\tau(x_{0})=s\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}\Bigg],`$ |  |

where the last inequality holds since
$`\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}\leq\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)B\leq O(\epsilon\log d)`$.

Since
$`\mathbb{E}\Big[\Delta^{2,2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s\Big]\geq\Omega\big(\frac{(d^{1.01}\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}}{\log d}\big)`$
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\eqref{eq-s23-de-gap-1}\geq d^{0.01}\cdot\bigg|\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\Big]_{s,s}\bigg|,
``` |  |

and thus $`\eqref{eq-s23-de-gap-1}\gg\eqref{eq-s23-de-gap-2}`$. Thus,
for $`\epsilon\ll\frac{1}{d}`$, if the attention gap does not decrease
to the level of
$`O\bigg(\frac{(d^{1.01}\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}}{\log d}\bigg)`$,
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$ will start to dominantly increase while
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ will not change too much. On the other
hand, if the gap of attention holds, then
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}\geq[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{2,2}=1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{O(1)}{O(1)+e^{[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,4}]_{s,s}}+e^{[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}]_{s,s}}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{O(1)}{O(1)+2e^{[\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}]_{s,s}}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot 3\epsilon>\epsilon.`$ |  |

This implies that, we can find some time between $`\widetilde{T}`$ and
$`T_{2,3,s}`$, s.t., the gap will decrease to
$`O\bigg(\frac{(d^{1.01}\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}}{\log d}\bigg)`$. We
denote this time as $`T^{\star}`$ and stop the training.

#### D.5.7 At the End of the Training

Putting everything together, we have that at the end of the training, we
have

###### Lemma D.45.

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, at
$`T^{\star}=\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{d^{(1-2\epsilon)C_{B}}}{\eta\epsilon}\Big)`$,
if
$`\widetilde{\Omega}(\sigma_{0})\ll\epsilon\leq O(\frac{1}{d^{1.01}})`$,
we have

1.  1.
    Attention convergence:
    $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{2,\ell}\leq O(\epsilon)`$ for
    $`\ell\in[2]`$, and
    $`\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta^{2,2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s\big]\leq O(\frac{(d^{1.01}\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{q-1}}}{\log d})`$;
2.  2.
    $`\Big[\mathbf{Q}^{(T_{2,3,s})}_{4,p}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}\geq\Omega(\log d)`$
    for $`p\in\{3,4\}`$ if $`s=s^{\prime}\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$,
    otherwise,
    $`\Big[\mathbf{Q}^{(T_{2,3,s})}_{4,p}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})`$;
3.  3.
    Loss convergence:
    $`\sum_{\ell=1}^{2}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,\ell}_{5}\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}`$.

### D.6 Proof of Main Theorem

###### Theorem D.1 (Restatement of [Theorem 4.1](#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 4.1. ‣ 4 Learning CoT on Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")).

Under
Assumptions [3.1](#S3.Thmassumption1 "Assumption 3.1 (Asymptotic regime). ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[3.2](#S3.Thmassumption2 "Assumption 3.2 (LEGO distribution 𝒟^𝐿,𝒟^{𝐿,𝐿^′}). ‣ 3.1 Data Distribution ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[3.3](#S3.Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3.3 (Initialization). ‣ 3.3 LEGO Task via CoT Reasoning and Training Objective ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[3.4](#S3.Thmassumption4 "Assumption 3.4 (Logit clipping). ‣ Additional Technical Assumptions. ‣ 3.3 LEGO Task via CoT Reasoning and Training Objective ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[3.5](#S3.Thmassumption5 "Assumption 3.5 (Block-sparse attention matrix). ‣ Additional Technical Assumptions. ‣ 3.3 LEGO Task via CoT Reasoning and Training Objective ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[4.1](#S4.Thmassumption1 "Assumption 4.1 (Simply transitive group action). ‣ 4 Learning CoT on Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
for some constant $`0<c^{*}<1`$, $`n_{y}<m\ll\log^{2}d`$, the
transformer model $`F^{(T_{1}+T_{2})}`$ obtained by
Algorithm [1](#algorithm1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 4 Learning CoT on Simply Transitive Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
with learning rate $`\eta=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}`$, and stage 1 and
2 iteration
$`T_{1}=\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{d}{\eta(\sigma_{0})^{q-2}}\Big)`$,
$`T_{2}=\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{\mathsf{poly}(d)}{\eta\sigma_{0}}\Big)`$
satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{Acc}_{L}\!\Bigl(F^{(T_{1}+T_{2})}\Bigr)\;\geq\;1-\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)},\text{ for every $L\leq O(d^{c^{*}})$, }`$ |  | (87) |

i.e., $`F^{(T_{1}+T_{2})}`$, which is trained for task
$`{\mathcal{T}}^{1}`$ and $`{\mathcal{T}}^{2}`$, generalizes to solve
the tasks $`{\mathcal{T}}^{\ell},\ell\leq L`$.

###### Proof.

By
[Lemma D.45](#A4.Thmlemma45 "Lemma D.45. ‣ D.5.7 At the End of the Training ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
at the end of Stage 2 training, we have
$`\Big[\mathbf{Q}^{(T_{2,3,s})}_{4,p}\Big]_{s,s}\geq\Omega(\log d)`$ for
all $`p\in\{3,4\}`$ and $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$.

Therefore, for task $`{\mathcal{T}}^{L}`$ with $`L\leq O(d^{c^{*}})`$,
where $`0<c^{*}<1`$ is a constant depending on the value of $`\epsilon`$
in
[Lemma D.45](#A4.Thmlemma45 "Lemma D.45. ‣ D.5.7 At the End of the Training ‣ D.5 Stage 2.3: Decrease of Gap and Convergence ‣ Appendix D Learning the Attention Layer: Simply Transitive Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{L,\ell}\leq\frac{O(1)\cdot L}{O(1)\cdot L+e^{\Big[\mathbf{Q}^{(T_{2,3,s})}_{4,3}\Big]_{s,s}}+e^{\Big[\mathbf{Q}^{(T_{2,3,s})}_{4,4}\Big]_{s,s}}}=o(1).
``` |  |

Moreover, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta^{L,\ell}\leq\Delta^{2,1}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(T_{2,3,s})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(T_{2,3,s})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\leq o(1).
``` |  |

These together guarantee that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,\tau(g_{\ell+1}(y_{\ell}))}(F^{(T^{\star})},\mathbf{Z}^{(L,\ell)})\leq\frac{O(1)\cdot d+e^{o(1)}}{O(1)\cdot d+e^{o(1)}+e^{\Omega(\log d)}}\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)},
``` |  |

which implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathrm{Acc}_{L}\big(F^{(T^{\star})}\big)\geq 1-\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}.
``` |  |

∎

## Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length

In this part, we consider the scenario where the group operations form a
symmetry group. Specifically, following
[Assumption 5.1](#S5.Thmassumption1 "Assumption 5.1 (Symmetry group actions). ‣ 5 Learning CoT on Symmetry Group Actions on ℤ_𝑛 ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we assume that $`\mathcal{Y}=\{0,1,\dots,n_{y}-1\}`$, and let
$`\mathcal{G}`$ be the symmetry group for $`\mathcal{Y}`$, with order
$`|\mathcal{G}|=n_{y}!=\Theta(\mathrm{polylog}d)\gg\frac{1}{\varrho}`$,
where $`n_{y}=\Theta\left(\frac{\log\log d}{\log\log\log d}\right)`$.
Similar to the simply transitive case, we restrict our analysis to
updating only $`\mathbf{Q}`$, specifically the blocks
$`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$ and $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$.

Throughout this section, we focus on the simple task
$`{\mathcal{T}}^{2}`$ and analyze gradient descent updates with respect
to the per-token loss $`\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}`$. Given
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, let
$`\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5,s}=-\mathbb{E}\Big[\log p_{F}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}\mid\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\,\big|\,\tau(x_{1})=s\Big]`$.
Due to the symmetry of $`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ and
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$ across $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, we may,
without loss of generality, focus on a particular
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$ and analyze the corresponding loss
$`\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5,s}`$ in what follows.

### E.1 Gradient Computations

We start with the gradient computations for the attention layer.

##### Notations for gradient expressions.

We first introduce some notations for the gradients of the attention
layer. For $`1\leq\ell\leq L`$, given $`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$ and
$`\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{L,\ell-1}`$, define

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Xi^{L}_{\ell,i,\mathbf{k}}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\triangleq\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{i,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{i,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\big)\langle\mathbf{W}_{i,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k}}\rangle,\quad{i\in[5].}`$ |  | (88) |

For simplicity of notation, we will henceforth omit the dependence on
$`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$ in the notation of
$`\Xi^{L}_{\ell,i,\mathbf{k}}`$ when it is clear from the context.

###### Fact E.1 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}`$).

For any $`p,q\in[5]`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{p,q}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\sum_{i\in[5]}-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{p,q}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L,\ell}_{i},\quad\text{ where }`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{p,q}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L,\ell}_{i}=`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{L,\ell-1}}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}}\cdot\left(\Xi^{L}_{\ell,i,\mathbf{k}}-\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}^{L,\ell-1}}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rightarrow\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\Xi^{L}_{\ell,i,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\right)\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1,p}\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k},q}^{\top}\Bigg].`$ |  |

###### Lemma E.1 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$).

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},2}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},1}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1}),s^{\prime}=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

###### Lemma E.2 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$).

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},1}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},0}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1}),s^{\prime}=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

### E.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients

In this subsection, we establish several useful bounds on the gradients
of the attention layer, leveraging the feature structure of the MLP
layer learned during stage 1.1. These bounds will be instrumental for
the subsequent analysis.

Recall that for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, the
fiber $`\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, and the set of feature combinations for
predicting $`y=\tau^{-1}(j)`$, are defined as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}=\{g\in\mathcal{G}\mid\tau\big(g(y)\big)=j\},\quad\mathfrak{F}_{j}=\Big\{(\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},y)\in 2^{\mathcal{G}}\times\mathcal{Y}\Big\}.`$ |  |

As established in
[Theorem C.1](#A3.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem C.1 (Learning Symmetric Group Actions). ‣ C.1.1 Theorem Statement ‣ C.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix C Learning Symmetry Group Actions ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
at the end of stage 1.1, we have

- •
  Sparse activations: For $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, let
  $`\phi=(\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$, then there
  exists exactly one *activated* neuron $`r\in[m]`$ such that when
  $`g_{1}=g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},y_{0}=y`$ happens:

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r}^{(T_{1.1})}\geq B-O(\delta),\qquad\Big|C_{\alpha}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)\Big|\leq O(\delta)`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r^{\prime}}^{(T_{1.1})}\leq O(d^{-\Omega(1)})\quad\forall r^{\prime}\neq r.`$ |  |

  for some $`C_{\alpha}=\Theta(n_{y})`$;
- •
  Cancellation of incorrect features: For $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, let
  $`\phi=(\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$, and let the
  $`r\in[m]`$ be the activated neuron, then for any
  $`g^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, and any
  $`y^{\prime}\neq y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, we have

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big|V_{j,r}^{(T_{1.1})}(g)+V_{j,r}^{(T_{1.1})}(y^{\prime})\Big|\leq O(\delta)\quad\text{and}\quad\Big|V_{j,r}^{(T_{1.1})}(g^{\prime})+V_{j,r}^{(T_{1.1})}(y)\Big|\leq O(\delta).`$ |  |

In the analysis of FFN layer, we have a pair
$`\delta=(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})`$; in the expressions above, some terms
should use $`\delta_{1}`$ and others $`\delta_{2}`$.

##### Notations for activated neurons.

We denote by $`r_{j,y}`$ the unique activated neuron corresponding to
$`\phi=(\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},y)\in\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$. For any
$`g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, we also write $`r_{g\cdot y}`$ for the
same neuron $`r_{j,y}`$. Note that $`r_{g_{1}\cdot y}=r_{g_{2}\cdot y}`$
for distinct $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$. Moreover, define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\triangleq\cup_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathfrak{A}_{j},\text{ where }\mathfrak{A}_{j}\triangleq\{r_{j,y}\mid y\in\mathcal{Y}\}.`$ |  |

In other words, $`\mathfrak{A}`$ is the set of all activated neurons
across all feature sets $`\mathfrak{F}_{j}`$ for
$`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$. Given $`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$, letting
$`\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})=\cup\{g_{\ell^{\prime}}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{L}`$
be the collection of all the chosen group elements in the predicate
clauses. Similarly
$`\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}=\cup\{y_{\ell^{\prime}}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-1}`$.
Then define
$`\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})=\bigg\{r_{g\cdot y}\mid g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}\wedge\Big(g\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\vee y\in\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}\Big)\bigg\}`$.
For simplicity, we omit the dependence on $`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$ in
the notation of $`\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ when it is clear from the
context. Equipped with these notations, we can summarize the above
properties in the following lemmas.

###### Lemma E.3 (Properties of target feature magnitude).

Given $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$ then for
$`g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, the following properties hold.

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g)+V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y)\geq 2B-O(\delta),\quad\Big|C_{\alpha}V_{j,r}^{(t)}(y)-V_{j,r}^{(t)}(g)\Big|\leq O(\delta),`$ |  | (89) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g)+V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y^{\prime})\right|\leq O(\delta),V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y^{\prime})<0\quad\text{for all }y^{\prime}\neq y,`$ |  | (90) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g^{\prime})+V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(y)\right|\leq O(\delta),V_{j,r_{g\cdot y}}(g^{\prime})<0\quad\text{for all }g^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}.`$ |  | (91) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|V_{j,r}(g)\right|,\left|V_{j,r}(y)\right|\leq O(\delta)\quad\text{for all }r\notin\mathfrak{A}_{j}.`$ |  | (92) |

###### Lemma E.4 (Properties of irrelevant magnitude).

If $`(p,v)\notin\{2\}\times\mathcal{G}\cup\{5\}\times\mathcal{Y}`$, or
$`j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, then for any $`r\in[m]`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r,p},e_{v}\rangle\big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  | (93) |

The above lemmas give us some direct computations of the inner products
between the weight matrices and input embedding vectors.

###### Lemma E.5.

Let $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and $`\ell\in[2]`$. Then for any
$`r\in[m]`$, the following holds:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},\ell}\rangle`$ | $`\displaystyle=V_{j,r}(g_{\ell})\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}),`$ |  | (94) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\rangle`$ | $`\displaystyle=V_{j,r}(y_{\ell-1})\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  | (95) |

Moreover, for $`j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$ and any
$`\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{2,1}`$ and $`r\in[m]`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big|\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbf{k}}\rangle\big|=\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  | (96) |

Furthermore, we can establish some characterizations of the
$`\Lambda_{5,j,r}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,\ell-1})`$ quantities, which are crucial
for the following analysis.

###### Lemma E.6 (Characterizations of Lambda).

Given $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}`$ with
$`\{\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathbf{k}}\}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{2,1}}`$,
then

1.  1.
    for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for activated neuron
    $`r\in\mathfrak{A}_{j}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{2}\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},\ell^{\prime}}V_{j,r}(g_{\ell^{\prime}})+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{2}\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},\ell^{\prime}-1}V_{j,r}(y_{\ell^{\prime}-1})\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for any non-activated neuron
    $`r\notin\mathfrak{A}_{j}`$ we have

    |     |                                                            |     |
    |-----|------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
    |     | $`\displaystyle\Big|\Lambda_{5,j,r}\Big|\leq{O}(\delta).`$ |     |
3.  3.
    for $`j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for any $`r\in[m]`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Big|\Lambda_{5,j,r}\Big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |

A direct consequence of the above lemma is the following finer
characterization of the activated neurons.

###### Lemma E.7.

Given $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for
$`r\in\mathfrak{A}_{j}\setminus\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$, we have
$`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)=0`$.

Now we are ready to further derive the gradients of the attention layer
starting from
[Lemmas E.1](#A5.Thmlemma1 "Lemma E.1 (Gradients of 𝐐_{4,3}). ‣ Notations for gradient expressions. ‣ E.1 Gradient Computations ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [E.2](#A5.Thmlemma2 "Lemma E.2 (Gradients of 𝐐_{4,4}). ‣ Notations for gradient expressions. ‣ E.1 Gradient Computations ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and the properties established above.

###### Lemma E.8 (Refined expression for the gradient of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$).

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$, letting
$`j_{2}=\tau(g_{2}(y_{1}))`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(g_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s,\tau(x_{0})=s^{\prime}}\Bigg].`$ |  |

###### Lemma E.9 (Refined expression for the gradient of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$).

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$, letting
$`j_{2}=\tau(g_{2}(y_{1}))`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(y_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(y_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(y_{0})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(y_{0})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s,\tau(x_{0})=s^{\prime}}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Following the above calculations, we can further obtain the gradient
summation of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$ and $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$ as follows:

###### Lemma E.10 (Gradient sum of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$ and $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$).

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, letting $`j_{2}=\tau(g_{2}(y_{1}))`$,
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\Big]_{s,s}+\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\Big]_{s,s}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j_{2},r}(y_{0})-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j_{2},r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~+\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\big(\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}+\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Bigg].`$ |  |

##### Notations for gradient decompositions.

We shall define some useful notations to further simplify the
expressions of gradient.

###### Lemma E.11.

For any $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, we define the following notations for
the gradient decompositions:

1.  1.
    for $`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ we have
    $`\big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{N}_{s,3,2,i}+\mathcal{N}_{s,3,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}_{s,3,2,iii}\big]`$,
    where

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,3,2,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot`$ |  | (97) |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s},`$ |  |  |

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,3,2,ii}`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot`$ |  | (98) |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s},`$ |  |  |

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,3,2,iii}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\pm\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}.~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~`$ |  | (99) |
2.  2.
    for $`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$, we have
    $`\big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{N}_{s,4,2,i}+\mathcal{N}_{s,4,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}_{s,4,2,iii}\big]`$,
    where

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,4,2,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot`$ |  | (100) |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(y_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s},`$ |  |  |

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,4,2,ii}`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot`$ |  | (101) |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(y_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s},`$ |  |  |

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,4,2,iii}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\pm\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}.~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~`$ |  | (102) |
3.  3.
    for the summation of $`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ and
    $`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$, we have
    $`\big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\big]_{s,s}+\big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{N}_{s,2,i}+\mathcal{N}_{s,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}_{s,2,iii}\big]`$,
    where

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,2,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle=(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j_{2},r}(y_{0})-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j_{2},r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~+\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}`$ |  |
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,2,ii}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}`$ |  |
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{s,2,iii}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\big(\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}+\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}.`$ |  |

##### Probabilistic events.

We conclude this subsection by introducing several probabilistic events
that will be used to simplify the characterization of activated neurons
in the subsequent analysis. We first define some events that may
contribute non-trivially to the gradient of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$ and
$`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big\{y_{0}\neq y_{1},g_{1}(y_{\ell-1})\not=g_{2}(y_{\ell-1}),\text{ for all }\ell\in[2]\Big\},`$ |  | (103) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big\{g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),y_{0}},g_{1}(y_{1})\neq g_{2}(y_{1})\Big\},`$ |  | (104) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big\{y_{0}=y_{1},g_{1}(y_{1})\neq g_{2}(y_{1})\Big\},`$ |  | (105) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big\{y_{0}\neq y_{1},g_{1}(y_{\ell-1})=g_{2}(y_{\ell-1}),\text{ for all }\ell\in[2]\Big\}.`$ |  | (106) |

We first observe that event $`\mathcal{E}_{1}`$ occurs with high
probability $`1-O\big(\frac{1}{n_{c}}\big)`$, and serves as the primary
regime of interest. In contrast, events $`\mathcal{E}_{2}`$ and
$`\mathcal{E}_{3}`$ each occur with probability $`\Theta(1/n_{y})`$ and
correspond to instances of initial prediction ambiguity, where certain
incorrect classes may exhibit disproportionately large logits.
Similarly, $`\mathcal{E}_{4}`$ corresponds to instances of initial
prediction ambiguity, while occurs with probability
$`\Theta(1/n^{2}_{y})`$. Furthermore, we define the following events,
which yield negligible gradient contributions to $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$
and $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$, as they do not lead to significant confusion
among the incorrect predictions:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{5}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big\{y_{0}\neq y_{1},g_{1}(y_{1})=g_{2}(y_{1}),g_{1}(y_{0})\neq g_{2}(y_{0})\Big\},`$ |  | (107) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{6}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big\{y_{0}=y_{1},g_{1}(y_{1})=g_{2}(y_{1})\Big\}.`$ |  | (108) |

Here, $`\mathcal{E}_{5}`$ occurs with probability $`\Theta(1/{n_{y}})`$,
and $`\mathcal{E}_{6}`$ occurs with probability
$`\Theta(1/{n^{2}_{c}})`$. Together, the events
$`\cup_{i\in[6]}\mathcal{E}_{i}`$ form a partition of the entire sample
space.

### E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of $`\mathbf{Q}`$

We define the following notations:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\epsilon^{L,\ell}_{\mathsf{attn}}\big(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}\big)`$ | $`\displaystyle=1-\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathsf{pred},\ell}\big(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}\big)-\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\big(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}\big),`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\Delta^{L,\ell}\big(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}\big)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathsf{pred},\ell}\big(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}\big)-\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},\ell-1\to\mathsf{ans},\ell-1}\big(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}\big)\Big|.`$ |  |

We abbreviate
$`\epsilon^{L,\ell}_{\mathsf{attn}}\big(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}\big)`$ and
$`\Delta^{L,\ell}\big(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}\big)`$ as
$`\epsilon^{L,\ell}_{\mathsf{attn}}`$ and $`\Delta^{L,\ell}`$ for
simplicity. Since we only focus on the input $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}`$ in the
following analysis, we will omit the notation related to the length,
i.e., we use $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}`$ and $`\Delta`$ when the
context is clear.

###### Induction E.1.

Given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, let $`T_{1,2,1,s}`$ denote the first
time that
$`\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s]\leq 0.4`$ For
all iterations $`t\leq T_{1,2,1,s}`$, we have the following holds

1.  1.
    $`\big[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}\big]_{s,s}+\big[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,4}\big]_{s,s}\leq O(1)`$
    monotonically increases;
2.  2.
    for $`p\in\{3,4\}`$, for $`s^{\prime}\in\tau(\mathcal{X})\not=s`$,
    $`\Big|\big[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,p}\big]_{s,s^{\prime}}\Big|\leq O\bigg(\frac{\big[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,p}\big]_{s,s^{\prime}}}{d}\bigg)`$
    ; otherwise $`\big[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,p}\big]_{s,s^{\prime}}=0`$;
3.  3.
    for any sample $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}`$, we have
    $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}\geq-O\big(\frac{\log\log d}{\log d}\big)`$;
4.  4.
    for any sample $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}`$, we have
    $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\leq c_{1}`$
    for some small constant $`c_{1}>0`$.

#### E.3.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries

###### Lemma E.12.

If
[Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$,then we have

1.  1.
    $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\in[0.4\pm\widetilde{O}\big(\frac{1}{d}\big),0.5]`$;
2.  2.
    $`\big|\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big|\leq\widetilde{O}\big(\frac{1}{d}\big)`$.

###### Lemma E.13.

If
[Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}`$,

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\Big)\cdot 2B+\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`j^{\prime}_{2}=\tau\big(g_{2}(y_{0})\big)`$, we
    have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{0}))`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{0})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}=`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+O(\delta).`$ |  |

    furthermore, we have
    $`\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{0})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}\leq\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{1})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}`$.
4.  4.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d}\Big)+O(\delta).`$ |  |
5.  5.
    for other $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, if there are $`j`$ and $`y`$,
    s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ (notice that
    $`y\neq y_{0},y_{1}`$ for $`\mathcal{E}_{1}`$), then for such a
    $`j`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    else, none of $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ can be activated.

Notice that for $`\mathcal{E}_{1}`$, the neurons mentioned in 1-4 should
be different neurons, while the predictions in 1 and 3, or 2 and 4 can
be the same in some cases, e.g., $`g_{1}(y_{1})=g_{2}(y_{0})`$. In all
cases, except for the neurons mentioned above, i.e.,
$`\cup_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[2]}\{r_{g_{\ell}\cdot y_{\ell^{\prime}-1}}\}`$
(which may not be activated), all other neurons
$`r\in\cup_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[2]}\Big(\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{\ell}\cdot y_{\ell^{\prime}-1})}\setminus\{r_{g_{\ell}\cdot y_{\ell^{\prime}-1}}\}\Big)`$
cannot be activated.

###### Lemma E.14.

If
[Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\Big)\cdot 2B`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~+\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`j^{\prime}_{2}=\tau\big(g_{2}(y_{0})\big)`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j^{\prime}_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}\}`$
    (notice that in this case
    $`r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}=r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}`$) cannot be activated,
    moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+O(\delta).`$ |  |
4.  4.
    for other $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, if there are $`j`$ and $`y`$,
    s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ (notice that
    $`y\neq y_{0},y_{1}`$ for $`\mathcal{E}_{2}`$), then for such a
    $`j`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    else, none of $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ can be activated.

###### Lemma E.15.

If
[Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot 2B\pm O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot 2B\pm O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for other $`j=g(y_{1})`$, where
    $`g_{1},g_{2}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y_{1}}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    moreover, if there exists $`y`$, s.t.,
    $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ (notice that $`y\neq y_{1}`$
    for $`\mathcal{E}_{3}`$), we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    besides, other $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ cannot be
    activated.

###### Lemma E.16.

If
[Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{4}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot 2B+O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for other $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, if there exist $`j`$ and $`y`$,
    s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, then for such a $`j`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    else, none of $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ can be activated.

###### Lemma E.17.

If
[Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{5}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot 2B\pm O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{0}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{0})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle+\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+O(\delta).`$ |  |
4.  4.
    for other $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, if there are $`j`$ and $`y`$,
    s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, then for such a $`j`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    else, none of $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ can be activated.

###### Lemma E.18.

If
[Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{6}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}\Big)\cdot 2B\pm O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for other $`j=g(y_{1})`$, where
    $`g_{2}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y_{1}}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    moreover, if there exists $`y\neq y_{1}`$, s.t.,
    $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    besides, other $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ cannot be
    activated.

#### E.3.2 Gradient Lemma

###### Lemma E.19.

If
[Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`<t`$, given $`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, we
have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}}{\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}}\Big]_{s,s}+\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,4}}{\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}}\Big]_{s,s}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\geq\min\bigg\{\Omega\Big(\frac{1}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big),\Omega(\frac{B}{d})\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigg]\bigg\}>0.`$ |  |

###### Proof.

By
[Lemma E.10](#A5.Thmlemma10 "Lemma E.10 (Gradient sum of 𝐐_{4,3} and 𝐐_{4,4}). ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ E.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[Lemma E.11](#A5.Thmlemma11 "Lemma E.11. ‣ Notations for gradient decompositions. ‣ E.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we have
$`\big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\big]_{s,s}+\big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{N}_{s,2,i}+\mathcal{N}_{s,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}_{s,2,iii}\big]`$.
Based
on[Lemma E.13](#A5.Thmlemma13 "Lemma E.13. ‣ E.3.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")-[Lemma E.18](#A5.Thmlemma18 "Lemma E.18. ‣ E.3.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we can first directly bound the term $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,iii}`$ as
follows:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,iii}\bigg]\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}^{q})=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}.`$ |  |

In the following discussion, we focus on $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}`$
and $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}`$, and consider two regimes for the
gradient lower bound: (i)
$`\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s\big]\leq\frac{\varrho}{B}`$,
and (ii)
$`\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s\big]\geq\frac{\varrho}{B}`$.
The proof strategy is to analyze $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}`$ and
$`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}`$ under different event conditions in two
regimes.

1.  1.
    For regime (i),

    1.  (a)
        For $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}`$, by
        [Induction E.1](#A5.Thminduction1 "Induction E.1. ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
        and
        [Lemma E.13](#A5.Thmlemma13 "Lemma E.13. ‣ E.3.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
        we have an immediate logit upper bound for
        $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$:
        $`\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\leq O\big(\frac{1}{d}\big)`$. Then
        by
        [Lemma E.10](#A5.Thmlemma10 "Lemma E.10 (Gradient sum of 𝐐_{4,3} and 𝐐_{4,4}). ‣ Notations for activated neurons. ‣ E.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
        we have

        |  |  |  |  |
        |----|----|----|----|
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]`$ |  |
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot`$ |  |
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\Big(-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j_{2},r}(y_{0})-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j_{2},r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~+\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg].`$ |  |

        By
        [Lemma E.13](#A5.Thmlemma13 "Lemma E.13. ‣ E.3.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
        we can obtain

        |  |  |  |  |  |
        |----|----|----|----|----|
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}})\cdot`$ |  |  |
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\Big(-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}(y_{0})-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}(g_{1})`$ |  |  |
````
