````math
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta(1)\cdot\max\Bigg\{e^{\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\big)2B-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B},`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\qquad e^{\log d-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}\Bigg\}`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta\!\left(e^{2c_{1}B-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}\right)`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta\!\left(e^{-2\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-2c-c_{1}\right)B}\right),`$ |  |

    where the last inequality follows from the fact that
    $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(1-2c)=\tfrac{1}{2}-c`$.
  - –
    If $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\log p_{F}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}|\mathbf{Z}^{(2,1)})\leq\Theta(1)`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\cdot\max\Bigg\{e^{\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\big)B-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B},`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\qquad e^{\log d-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}\Bigg\}`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta\!\left(e^{2(c+c_{1})B-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}\right)`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta\!\left(e^{-2\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-3c-c_{1}\right)B}\right).`$ |  |
  - –
    If $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\log p_{F}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}|\mathbf{Z}^{(2,1)})`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta(1)\cdot\max\Bigg\{e^{\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\big)2B},e^{\log d-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}2B}\Bigg\}`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta(1)\cdot\max\Big\{e^{-2(\tfrac{1}{2}-c_{1}-c)B},\;e^{-2(\tfrac{1}{2}-2c)B}\Big\}.`$ |  |
- •
  If
  $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\leq\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}`$,
  then

  - –
    If $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\log p_{F}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}|\mathbf{Z}^{(2,1)})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta(1)\cdot e^{\log d-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta\!\left(e^{2c_{1}B-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}\right)`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta\!\left(e^{-2\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{5}{2}c-\tfrac{c_{2}}{2}-c_{1}\right)B}\right),`$ |  |

    where the last inequality uses the fact that
    $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\leq c-c_{2}`$,
    implying
    $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{3}{2}c+\tfrac{c_{2}}{2}`$.
  - –
    If $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\log p_{F}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}|\mathbf{Z}^{(2,1)})\leq\Theta(1)\cdot`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\max\Bigg\{e^{\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\big)2B-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B},`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\qquad e^{\log d-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}\Bigg\}`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta\!\left(e^{\max\{c,c_{1}\}2B-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}\right)`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta\!\left(e^{-2\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-2c-\max\{c,c_{1}\}\right)B}\right).`$ |  |
  - –
    If $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\log p_{F}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}|\mathbf{Z}^{(2,1)})`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta(1)\cdot\max\Bigg\{e^{\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\big)2B},e^{\log d-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}2B}\Bigg\}`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Theta(1)\cdot\max\Big\{e^{-2(\tfrac{1}{2}-c_{1}-c)B},\;e^{-2(\tfrac{1}{2}-2c)B}\Big\}.`$ |  |

Putting all cases together, if $`c\leq\tfrac{2}{3}c_{1}`$, we must have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathsf{Loss}_{5,s}^{2,2}\;\leq\;\Theta\!\left(e^{-2(\tfrac{1}{2}+3\cdot\tfrac{2c_{1}}{3}+c_{1})B}\right)=\Theta\!\left(e^{(-\tfrac{1}{2}+3c_{1})2B}\right),
``` |  |

which contradicts the definition of $`T_{1,2,2,s}`$. Therefore, it must
hold that $`c\geq\tfrac{2}{3}c_{1}`$ for all $`t\leq T_{1,2,2,s}`$.

∎

###### Lemma E.25.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`[T_{1,2,1,s},t)`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}`$,

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\Big)\cdot 2B+O\Big(\frac{B}{n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`j^{\prime}_{2}=\tau\big(g_{2}(y_{0})\big)`$, we
    have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{0}))`$, $`r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}`$
    cannot be activated.
4.  4.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d}\Big)+O(\delta).`$ |  |
5.  5.
    for other $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, if there are $`j`$ and $`y`$,
    s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ (notice that
    $`y\neq y_{0},y_{1}`$ for $`\mathcal{E}_{1}`$), then for such a
    $`j`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    else, none of $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ can be activated.

Notice that for $`\mathcal{E}_{1}`$, the neurons mentioned in 1-4 should
be different neurons, while the predictions in 1 and 3, or 2 and 4 can
be the same in some cases, e.g., $`g_{1}(y_{1})=g_{2}(y_{0})`$. In all
cases, except for the neurons mentioned above, i.e.,
$`\cup_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[2]}\{r_{g_{\ell}\cdot y_{\ell^{\prime}-1}}\}`$
(which may not be activated), all other neurons
$`r\in\cup_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[2]}\Big(\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{\ell}\cdot y_{\ell^{\prime}-1})}\setminus\{r_{g_{\ell}\cdot y_{\ell^{\prime}-1}}\}\Big)`$
cannot be activated.

###### Lemma E.26.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`j^{\prime}_{2}=\tau\big(g_{2}(y_{0})\big)`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j^{\prime}_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}\}`$
    (notice that in this case
    $`r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}=r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}`$) cannot be activated,
    moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+O(\delta).`$ |  |
4.  4.
    for other $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, if there are $`j`$ and $`y`$,
    s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ (notice that
    $`y\neq y_{0},y_{1}`$ for $`\mathcal{E}_{2}`$), then for such a
    $`j`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    else, none of $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ can be activated.

###### Lemma E.27.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot 2B\pm O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot 2B\pm O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for other $`j=g(y_{1})`$, where
    $`g_{1},g_{2}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y_{1}}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    moreover, if there exists $`y`$, s.t.,
    $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$ (notice that $`y\neq y_{1}`$
    for $`\mathcal{E}_{3}`$), we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    besides, other $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ cannot be
    activated.

###### Lemma E.28.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{4}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot 2B+O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for other $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, if there are $`j`$ and $`y`$,
    s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, then for such a $`j`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    else, none of $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ can be activated.

###### Lemma E.29.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{5}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot 2B\pm O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta).`$ |  |
3.  3.
    for the prediction $`\tau(g_{1}(y_{0}))`$, none of
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))}`$ can be
    activated.
4.  4.
    for other $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, if there are $`j`$ and $`y`$,
    s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, then for such a $`j`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    else, none of $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ can be activated.

###### Lemma E.30.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, then given
$`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{6}`$, we have

1.  1.
    for the prediction $`j_{2}`$,
    $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}\setminus\{r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}\}`$
    cannot be activated, moreover, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}\Big)\cdot 2B\pm O(\delta).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for other $`j=g(y_{1})`$, where
    $`g_{2}\notin\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y_{1}}`$, we have
    $`r_{g\cdot y_{1}}`$ cannot be activated, moreover, if there exists
    $`y\neq y_{1}`$, s.t., $`g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y}`$, we
    have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\cdot 2B+\widetilde{O}\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)+O(\delta);`$ |  |

    besides, other $`r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ cannot be
    activated.

#### E.4.2 Gradient Lemma

###### Lemma E.31.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, given
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, if
$`\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s\big]\leq\frac{1}{2}`$
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}}{\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}}\Big]_{s,s}+\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,4}}{\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}}\Big]_{s,s}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\Omega\Big(\frac{B}{d}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigg]+\Omega\Big(\frac{B}{n_{y}d}\Big)\cdot\sum_{m=2}^{3}\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{m}\bigg].`$ |  |

###### Proof.

The analysis follows the similar idea as
[Lemma E.19](#A5.Thmlemma19 "Lemma E.19. ‣ E.3.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
while
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
ensures that
$`\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s\big]\geq\Omega(1)>\frac{\varrho}{B}`$,
which falls into the regime (ii). Thus for the main event
$`\mathcal{E}_{1}`$, the term
$`\Lambda^{(t)}{5,j_{2},r{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}`$ is guaranteed to remain
within the linear regime. We can first directly upper bound the term
$`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,iii}`$ by $`\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}^{q})`$.

1.  1.
    For $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}`$, by
    [Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
    and
    [Lemma E.25](#A5.Thmlemma25 "Lemma E.25. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
    we can obtain

    |  |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]`$ |  |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~=\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\Big(-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}(y_{0})-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}(g_{1})`$ |  |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~+\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]`$ |  | (114) |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\Omega\Big(\frac{B}{d}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigg].`$ |  |  |

    Moving to $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}`$, by
    [Lemma E.25](#A5.Thmlemma25 "Lemma E.25. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
    we have

    - •
      for $`j=\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))`$, $`r=r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}`$, due to
      the cancellation of
      $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r}(g_{1})`$,
      we have

      |  |  |  |  |
      |----|----|----|----|
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}.`$ |  |
    - •
      for $`j=\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))`$, $`r=r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}`$

      |  |  |  |  |
      |----|----|----|----|
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}.`$ |  |
    - •
      for $`j=g_{1}(y)`$, where $`\exists y\neq y_{0},y_{1}`$, s.t.,
      $`g_{2}(y)=g_{1}(y)`$, we have

      |  |  |  |  |
      |----|----|----|----|
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}.`$ |  |

    Putting them together, and upper bound
    $`\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}`$
    by $`1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\cdot\max_{y\neq y_{0},y_{1}}\Big\{\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}},\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}\Big\}\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg],`$ |  |

    where the last inequality is due to the fact that
    $`\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y)),r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}})`$
    for $`y\neq y_{0},y_{1}`$ s.t., $`g_{1}(y)=g_{2}(y)`$. Notice that

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\max_{y\neq y_{0},y_{1}}\Big\{\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}},\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y_{0})),r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}\Big\}\leq`$ |  |
    |  | $`\displaystyle\max\bigg\{\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1},\Theta(\frac{1}{n_{y}})\bigg\}2B,`$ |  |

    while

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  | ([114](#A5.E114 "Equation 114 ‣ Item 1 ‣ Proof. ‣ E.4.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")) | $`\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot 2B\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}}\Big)\cdot\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)B\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg].`$ |  |

    Since by
    [Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
    $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\leq c_{1}\ll\frac{1}{2}`$,
    thus we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]\geq\Omega\Big(\frac{B}{d}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigg].`$ |  |
2.  2.
    For $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}`$,
    $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}`$ can be bounded analogously to
    ([114](#A5.E114 "Equation 114 ‣ Item 1 ‣ Proof. ‣ E.4.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")),
    yielding

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\bigg]\geq\Omega\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{2}\bigg]\geq 0.`$ |  |

    Moving to $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}`$,

    - •
      For $`j=\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))`$, we obtain

      |  |  |  |  |  |
      |----|----|----|----|----|
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\Big(\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}})\cdot`$ |  |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}(g_{1})`$ |  | (115) |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\Bigg]`$ |  |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq-O\!\left(\frac{B}{n^{2}_{y}\cdot d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\,\big|\,\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{2}\Big],`$ |  |  |

      where the inequality follows from the cancellation of the term

      |  |  |  |
      |----|----|----|
      |  |
      ``` math
      \mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}(g_{1}),
      ``` |  |

      together with the fact that

      |  |  |  |
      |----|----|----|
      |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}=\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\Big)\cdot\frac{2B}{n_{y}}+O(\delta)\;\leq\;O\!\left(\frac{B}{n_{y}}\right).`$ |  |
    - •
      For $`j^{\prime}_{2}=\tau(g_{2}(y_{0}))=\tau(g_{1}(y_{0}))`$, it
      is clear that

      |  |  |  |  |
      |----|----|----|----|
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2}}\Big(\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}})\cdot`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}(g_{1})`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq 0,`$ |  |

      where the last inequality is due to the fact that

      |  |  |  |  |
      |----|----|----|----|
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}(g_{1})-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{0}}}`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\bigg(1-2\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\Big)\bigg)2B`$ |  |
      |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\bigg(1-2\Big(c_{1}+0.2\Big)\bigg)2B\geq 0.`$ |  |
    - •
      For $`j=\tau(g_{2}(y))`$ with some $`y\not=y_{0},y_{1}`$ such that
      $`g_{1}(y)=g_{2}(y)`$, we distinguish two cases:

      - –
        If

        |  |  |  |
        |----|----|----|
        |  |
        ``` math
        \mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s\big]\leq\frac{c_{1}}{4},
        ``` |  |

        then by
        [Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
        and
        [Lemma E.26](#A5.Thmlemma26 "Lemma E.26. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
        we have we have
        $`\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}-\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{2}\cdot y}}\geq\big(-\frac{c_{1}}{6}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\big)2B\geq(-\frac{c_{1}}{4}+\frac{3}{4}c_{1})2B=c_{1}B`$,
        which implies

        |  |  |  |
        |----|----|----|
        |  |
        ``` math
        \sum_{y\neq y_{0},y_{1}}\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{2}(y))}\mathds{1}_{g_{1}(y)=g_{2}(y)}\ll\widetilde{O}\!\left(\tfrac{1}{d^{1/2}}\right)\cdot\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}^{\prime}}.
        ``` |  |

        Consequently,

        |  |  |  |  |
        |----|----|----|----|
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{y\neq y_{0},y_{1}}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\Big(\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y}})\cdot`$ |  |
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y}}(y_{0})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y}}(g_{1})`$ |  |
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
        |  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\mathds{1}_{g_{1}(y)=g_{2}(y)}\Bigg]\Bigg|\leq O\!\left(\tfrac{1}{d^{1/2}}\right)\cdot\eqref{eq-s22-gd1-non-3}.`$ |  |
      - –
        If

        |  |  |  |
        |----|----|----|
        |  |
        ``` math
        \mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s\big]\geq\frac{c_{1}}{4},
        ``` |  |

        then by
        [Lemma E.26](#A5.Thmlemma26 "Lemma E.26. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
        the activation $`\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j,r_{g_{1}\cdot y}}`$ cannot
        be triggered.

    Putting the above discussion together, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\bigg]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\bigg]\geq\Omega\Big(\frac{B}{d\cdot n_{y}}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{2}\bigg].`$ |  |
3.  3.
    For $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}`$, the gradient admits an
    analysis analogous to that in
    [Lemma E.19](#A5.Thmlemma19 "Lemma E.19. ‣ E.3.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
    In particular, by
    [Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
    and
    [Lemma E.27](#A5.Thmlemma27 "Lemma E.27. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
    we first obtain the following logit bound:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}^{(t)}\;\leq\;\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}\cdot\Big(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}}\Big),\qquad j\neq j_{2},\,\tau\!\big(g_{1}(y_{1})\big).`$ |  |

    Therefore,

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{3}}\Big]`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}(y_{1})-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}(g_{1})`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{3}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\;\Omega\!\left(\frac{B}{n_{y}\cdot d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{3}\Big].`$ |  |

    Moving to $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{3}}\Big]`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1}))}\cdot\Big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}(y_{1})+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot V_{\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r}(g_{1})`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle-\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda^{(t)}_{5,\tau(g_{1}(y_{1})),r_{g_{1}\cdot y_{1}}}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)\,\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{3}}\Bigg]`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad-O\!\left(\frac{B}{d\cdot\mathsf{poly}d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{3}\Big].`$ |  |

    Combining the two bounds, we conclude

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{3}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{3}}\Big]\;\;\geq\;\;\Omega\!\left(\frac{B}{n_{y}\cdot d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{3}\Big].`$ |  |
4.  4.
    For $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{4}`$, in analogy with
    [Lemma E.19](#A5.Thmlemma19 "Lemma E.19. ‣ E.3.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
    we obtain the following crude bound:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{4}}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{4}}\Big]\;\;\geq\;0.`$ |  |
5.  5.
    For $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{5}\cup\mathcal{E}_{6}`$, by
    [Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
    [Lemma E.29](#A5.Thmlemma29 "Lemma E.29. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
    and
    [Lemma E.30](#A5.Thmlemma30 "Lemma E.30. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
    we obtain the following logit condition:

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}},\;\;\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j}\;\;\leq\;\;\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big],\qquad j\neq j_{2}.
    ``` |  |

    Hence, $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}`$ and
    $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}`$ can be bounded as

    |  |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|----|
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\big]\Big|,\quad\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,ii}\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\big]\Big|`$ |  |
    |  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq O\!\left(\frac{B}{dn_{y}}\cdot\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big],\qquad m\in\{5,6\}.`$ |  |

    Moreover, for $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{6}`$, if

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  |
    ``` math
    \mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}>\tfrac{1}{2},
    ``` |  |

    then $`\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,i}=0`$. Nevertheless, due to the above
    order-wise bound, this observation does not affect the overall
    analysis.

Putting everything together, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}}{\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}}\Big]_{s,s}+\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,4}}{\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}}\Big]_{s,s}=\sum_{\kappa\in\{i,ii,iii\}}\sum_{m\in[6]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{N}^{(t)}_{s,2,\kappa}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\bigg]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\Omega\Big(\frac{B}{d}\Big)\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{1}\bigg]+\Omega\Big(\frac{B}{n_{y}d}\Big)\cdot\sum_{m=2}^{3}\mathbb{E}\bigg[(1-\mathbf{logit}^{(t)}_{5,j_{2}})\big|\tau(x_{1})=s,\mathcal{E}_{m}\bigg].`$ |  |

∎

###### Lemma E.32.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, given
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for $`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,p}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$,
$`p\in\{3,4\}`$, $`s^{\prime}\not=s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\bigg|\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,p}}{\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}\bigg|\leq O\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big)\bigg|\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,p}}{\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5}}\Big]_{s,s}\bigg|.`$ |  |

#### E.4.3 Attention gap is small

###### Lemma E.33.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, then for any
sample $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\leq c_{1},`$ |  |

where $`c_{1}>0`$ is a small constant.

The proof follows along the same lines as
[Lemma E.22](#A5.Thmlemma22 "Lemma E.22. ‣ E.3.4 Attention gap is small ‣ E.3 Stage 1.2.1: Initial Growth of 𝐐 ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
and hence the details are omitted for brevity.

###### Lemma E.34.

If
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},T_{1,2,2,s})`$, then for any
sample $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\leq\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}-c_{2},`$ |  |

where $`c_{2}=\frac{\log d}{4B}>0`$ is a small constant.

###### Proof.

Let $`\widetilde{T}`$ denote the first time such that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\Big[\,\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s\Big]\;\leq\;\frac{\log d}{4.02B}.
``` |  |

Since
$`\big|[\mathbf{Q}_{4,p}^{(t)}]_{s,s^{\prime}}\big|\leq O\!\left(\tfrac{[\mathbf{Q}_{4,p}^{(t)}]_{s,s}}{d}\right)`$
for $`p\in\{3,4\}`$, it follows that for any sample $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}`$
with $`\tau(x_{1})=s`$, at time $`\widetilde{T}`$ we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\;\geq\;\frac{\log d}{4.02B}\;\pm\;\widetilde{O}(1/d).`$ |  |

By
[Lemma E.24](#A5.Thmlemma24 "Lemma E.24. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we also have
$`\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\geq\Omega(1)\gg\varrho`$.
We then consider the following cases:

- •
  If $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}`$, then
  $`\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{1}}}`$ is
  already activated into the linear regime. Moreover, since
  $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\leq\frac{\log d}{4.02B}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\to\mathsf{pred},1}<0`$,
  it follows that
  $`\Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}`$
  cannot be activated. Thus, by
  [Lemma E.11](#A5.Thmlemma11 "Lemma E.11. ‣ Notations for gradient decompositions. ‣ E.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,i}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,iii}\big)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\Big]\;\geq\;\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{B}{d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{T})}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{1}\big],
  ``` |  |

  while

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,i}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,iii}\big)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\Big]\;\leq\;-\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{B}{dn_{y}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{T})}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{1}\big].
  ``` |  |
- •
  If $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}`$, then

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \Lambda^{(\widetilde{T})}_{5,j^{\prime}_{2},r_{g_{2}\cdot y_{0}}}=(\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0})2B\;\leq\;\frac{\log d}{2.01}.
  ``` |  |

  Hence
  $`\mathbf{logit}^{(1)}_{5,j_{2}}\leq O(d^{-1.01/2.01})(1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(t)})`$.
  By
  [Lemma E.11](#A5.Thmlemma11 "Lemma E.11. ‣ Notations for gradient decompositions. ‣ E.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,i}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,iii}\big)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\Big]\;\geq\;\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{B}{dn_{y}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{T})}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{2}\big],
  ``` |  |

  and

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,i}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,iii}\big)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}\Big]\;\leq\;-\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{B}{dn_{y}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{T})}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{2}\big].
  ``` |  |
- •
  If $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}`$, we can apply the crude
  bounds

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,i}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,3,2,iii}\big)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{3}}\Big]\;\geq\;0,
  ``` |  |

  and

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,i}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,ii}+\mathcal{N}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{s,4,2,iii}\big)\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{3}}\Big]\;\leq\;0.
  ``` |  |

Combining the above, and noting that the gradient contribution from
$`\mathcal{E}_{4}\cup\mathcal{E}_{5}\cup\mathcal{E}_{6}`$ is negligible,
we conclude

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle\;\geq\;\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{B}{d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{T})}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{1}\big]`$ |  | (116) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{B}{dn_{y}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{T})}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{2}\big],`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}^{(\widetilde{T})}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}_{5}^{2,2}\big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle\;\leq\;-\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{B}{d}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{T})}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{1}\big]`$ |  | (117) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad-\Omega\!\left(\tfrac{B}{dn_{y}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}}^{(\widetilde{T})}\;\big|\;\tau(x_{1})=s,\,\mathcal{E}_{2}\big].`$ |  |  |

Finally, observe that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\;=\;\frac{e^{[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}]_{s,s}}-e^{[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,4}]_{s,s}}-e^{\widetilde{O}(1/d)}}{e^{[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}]_{s,s}}+e^{[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,4}]_{s,s}}+e^{\widetilde{O}(1/d)}}.
``` |  |

Hence,
([116](#A5.E116 "Equation 116 ‣ Proof. ‣ E.4.3 Attention gap is small ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
and
([117](#A5.E117 "Equation 117 ‣ Proof. ‣ E.4.3 Attention gap is small ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"))
together imply that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}+\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}
``` |  |

must decrease at time $`\widetilde{T}+1`$. ∎

#### E.4.4 At the End of Training

###### Lemma E.35 (At the end of stage 1.2.2).

[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all iterations
$`T_{1,2,1,s}<t\leq T_{1,2,2,s}={O}\Big(\frac{\mathsf{poly}(d)}{\eta B}\Big)`$.
At the end of training, we have

1.  1.
    Attention concentration:
    $`\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}\mid\tau(x_{1})=s]\leq 2.52c_{1}`$
    for some small constant $`0<c_{1}=\frac{1.005\log d}{2B}`$;
2.  2.
    Loss convergence:
    $`\mathsf{Loss}^{2,2}_{5,s}\leq e^{(-\frac{1}{2}+{3.01c_{1}})\cdot 2B}=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}d}`$.

###### Proof.

[Lemma E.24](#A5.Thmlemma24 "Lemma E.24. ‣ E.4.1 Attention and Lambda Preliminaries ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and
[Lemma E.31](#A5.Thmlemma31 "Lemma E.31. ‣ E.4.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
guarantee the continual growth of $`[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,3}]`$ and
$`[\mathbf{Q}^{(t)}_{4,4}]`$ until the attention weight
$`\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}`$ reaches $`\tfrac{4c_{1}}{3}`$. Combining
[Lemma E.31](#A5.Thmlemma31 "Lemma E.31. ‣ E.4.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[Lemma E.32](#A5.Thmlemma32 "Lemma E.32. ‣ E.4.2 Gradient Lemma ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
[Lemma E.33](#A5.Thmlemma33 "Lemma E.33. ‣ E.4.3 Attention gap is small ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
and
[Lemma E.34](#A5.Thmlemma34 "Lemma E.34. ‣ E.4.3 Attention gap is small ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
we conclude that there exists a stopping time

|     |                                                              |     |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       T_{1,2,2,s}=O\!\left(\frac{\mathsf{poly}(d)}{\eta B}\right),
       ```                                                           |     |

and that
[Induction E.2](#A5.Thminduction2 "Induction E.2. ‣ E.4 Stage 1.2.2: Convergence with Small Wrong Attention ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
holds for all $`t\in[T_{1,2,1,s},\,T_{1,2,2,s})`$.

Next, we establish an upper bound for $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}`$ at
the end of training. In this stage, it suffices to focus on the main
event $`\mathcal{E}_{1}`$. We denote

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},1},\quad\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\;=\;c+\widetilde{O}\!\left(\tfrac{1}{d}\right).
``` |  |

- •
  If
  $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\geq\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}`$,
  then for $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}`$ we have

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\log p_{F}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}|\mathbf{Z}^{(2,1)})`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\Theta(1)\cdot e^{\log d-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\Theta\!\left(e^{-\left(\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{c_{1}}{2}-2c-\tfrac{\log d}{B}\right)2B}\right),`$ |  |

  where the last inequality follows from

  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|
  |  |
  ``` math
  \mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}(1-2c+c_{1})=\tfrac{1+c_{1}}{2}-c.
  ``` |  |
- •
  If
  $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\leq\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}`$,
  then for $`\mathbf{Z}^{2,1}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}`$ we have

  |  |  |  |  |
  |----|----|----|----|
  |  | $`\displaystyle\log p_{F}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}|\mathbf{Z}^{(2,1)})`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\Theta(1)\cdot e^{\log d-\big(\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},0}\big)2B}`$ |  |
  |  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\Theta\!\left(e^{-\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-2c-\tfrac{\log d}{B}\right)2B}\right),`$ |  |

  where the last inequality follows from the fact that
  $`\mathbf{Attn}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}-c`$.

Putting the above cases together, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\tfrac{1}{2}+\frac{c_{1}}{2}-2c-\tfrac{\log d}{B}\;\geq\;\tfrac{1}{2}-3.01c_{1},
``` |  |

which implies

|     |                                                                |     |
|-----|----------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       c\;\leq\;\tfrac{1}{2}(3.02c_{1}+0.5c_{1}-c_{1})\;=\;1.26c_{1}.
       ```                                                             |     |

∎

## Appendix F Recursive Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case

In this section, we analyze the recursive learning dynamics of the
attention layer in the symmetry case. To this end, we recall the
definitions of the greedy data annotator, the bootstrapped LEGO data
distribution, and the associated self-training loss.

###### Definition F.1 (Greedy data annotator).

The greedy language model $`\widehat{p}_{F}`$ induced by a network $`F`$
is defined as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{p}_{F}(Z_{\mathsf{ans},L^{\prime}+1}\mid Z^{L,L^{\prime}})=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }Z_{\mathsf{ans},L^{\prime}+1}=\operatornamewithlimits{argmax}_{Z}p_{F}(Z\mid Z^{L,L^{\prime}}),\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}
``` |  | (118) |

###### Definition F.2 (Bootstrapped LEGO distribution).

We define $`\mathcal{D}_{F}^{L,L^{\prime}}`$ as the LEGO distribution in
[Assumption 3.2](#S3.Thmassumption2 "Assumption 3.2 (LEGO distribution 𝒟^𝐿,𝒟^{𝐿,𝐿^′}). ‣ 3.1 Data Distribution ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"),
except that the answers
$`Z_{\mathsf{ans},\ell},1\leq\ell\leq L^{\prime}`$ are generated
recursively by sampling
$`Z_{\mathsf{ans},\ell}\sim\widehat{p}_{F}(\cdot\mid Z^{L,\ell-1})`$
from the greedy language model $`\widehat{p}_{F}`$.

###### Definition F.3 (Self-training loss).

Given a (fixed) model $`\widetilde{F}`$ and length $`L`$, the
self-training next-clause-prediction loss is defined by replacing
$`\mathcal{D}^{L,L^{\prime}}`$ in
[Definition 3.7](#S3.Thmdefinition7 "Definition 3.7 (Next clause loss). ‣ 3.3 LEGO Task via CoT Reasoning and Training Objective ‣ 3 Problem Formulation ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
with the bootstrapped distribution $`\mathcal{D}_{F}^{L,L^{\prime}}`$
from
[Definition F.2](#A6.Thmdefinition2 "Definition F.2 (Bootstrapped LEGO distribution). ‣ Appendix F Recursive Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization"):

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathsf{Loss}^{L,L^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{F}}(F)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{Z^{L,L^{\prime}}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{F}}^{L,L^{\prime}}}\Big[-\log p_{F}(Z_{\mathsf{ans},L^{\prime},i}\mid Z^{L,L^{\prime}-1})\Big].`$ |  | (119) |

Similarly, the per-token loss is defined by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathsf{Loss}^{L,L^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{F},i}=\mathbb{E}_{Z^{L,L^{\prime}}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\widetilde{F}}^{L,L^{\prime}}}\big[-\log p_{F_{i}}(Z_{\mathsf{ans},L^{\prime},i}\mid Z^{L,L^{\prime}-1})\big],\quad i\in[5].
``` |  |

At stage $`k\geq 2`$, let $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ denote the model obtained
from the previous stage, trained on the task $`{\mathcal{T}}^{L_{k-1}}`$
with $`L_{k-1}=2^{k-1}`$. Using the greedy annotator
$`\widehat{p}_{F^{(T_{k-1})}}`$, we construct the bootstrapped LEGO
distribution $`\mathcal{D}_{F^{(T_{k-1})}}^{L_{k},L_{k}}`$ with total
length $`L_{k}=2^{k}`$. The corresponding self-training loss
$`\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},L^{\prime}}_{F^{(T_{k-1})}}`$ is then defined as
in
[Definition F.3](#A6.Thmdefinition3 "Definition F.3 (Self-training loss). ‣ Appendix F Recursive Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
In this stage, we focus on training via gradient descent on
$`\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},L^{\prime}}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},i}`$ with sequence
length $`L^{\prime}=2`$ and target token $`i=5`$, initialized from the
previous-stage model $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$.

For notational simplicity, throughout the discussion we drop the
subscript of the expectation operator $`\mathbb{E}`$ when $`\mathbf{Z}`$
is sampled from the ground-truth LEGO distribution; the subscript will
be explicitly included only when the expectation is taken over the
bootstrapped distribution. Moreover, we abbreviate the wrong attention
error $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{L_{k},2}`$ as
$`\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{L_{k}}`$ and the attention gap
$`\Delta^{L_{k},2}`$ as $`\Delta^{L_{k}}`$ when the context is clear.

### F.1 Preliminaries

We first present preliminaries on the recursive learning attention
layer, including its gradient computations and some useful probability
lemmas.

#### F.1.1 Gradient Computations

###### Fact F.1 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}`$).

Given $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ from the previous stage
$`{\mathcal{T}}^{L_{k-1}}`$ with $`L_{k-1}=2^{k-1}`$ for $`k\geq 2`$,
for $`(p,q)\in\Big\{(4,3),(4,4)\Big\}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{p,q}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}=-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{p,q}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{5}.`$ |  |

###### Remark F.1.

This fact is straightforward to verify. Since the attention error
$`\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{L_{k-1},2}`$ has already been controlled at
a small constant level in the previous stage for the model
$`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$, when transitioning from length $`2^{k-1}`$ to
$`2^{k}`$, the incorrect attention produced by $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ cannot
increase significantly. In particular, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{L_{k}}\;\leq\;2\epsilon_{\mathsf{attn}}^{L_{k-1}},
``` |  |

which remains small. Moreover, the attention gap
$`\Delta^{L_{k}}=\lvert\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},2}-\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},1}\rvert`$
is non-increasing due to the doubling of irrelevant clauses. As a
result, the probability assigned by $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ to the correct
prediction of $`Z_{\mathsf{ans},2,5}`$ given $`Z^{L_{k},1}`$ remains
significantly larger than that of any incorrect prediction.
Consequently, under the greedy data annotator, the self-training loss
$`\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}`$ coincides with the
original loss $`\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{5}`$ on the ground-truth LEGO
distribution.

###### Lemma F.1 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$).

Given $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ from the previous stage and
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},2}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{pred},1}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1}),s^{\prime}=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

###### Lemma F.2 (Gradients of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$).

Given $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ from the previous stage and
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},1}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot\bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathcal{E}_{5,j}(\mathbf{Z}^{2,1})\sum_{r\in[m]}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~\Big(\langle\mathbf{W}_{5,j,r},\mathbf{Z}_{\mathsf{ans},0}\rangle-\Lambda_{5,j,r}+b_{5,j,r}\Big)\bigg)\mathds{1}_{s=\tau(x_{1}),s^{\prime}=\tau(x_{0})}\Bigg].`$ |  |

##### Notations for activated neurons.

Recall that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}=\bigcup_{j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathfrak{A}_{j},\quad\text{where }\mathfrak{A}_{j}=\{\,r_{j,y}\mid y\in\mathcal{Y}\,\}.`$ |  |

Given $`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$, let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})=\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{L}\{g_{\ell^{\prime}}\}
``` |  |

be the collection of all group elements chosen in the predicate clauses,
and similarly define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}=\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-1}\{y_{\ell^{\prime}}\}.
``` |  |

We then introduce

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})=\Big\{\,r_{g\cdot y}\;\Big|\;g\in\mathrm{Fiber}_{j,y},\;g\in\widehat{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1})\ \vee\ y\in\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}\Big\}.
``` |  |

For simplicity, we omit the dependence on $`\mathbf{Z}^{L,\ell-1}`$ in
the notation of $`\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$ when it is clear from
context.

The above recalls the relevant notations from
[Section E.2](#A5.SS2 "E.2 Some Useful Bounds for Gradients ‣ Appendix E Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case for Short-Length ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization").
With these preliminaries in place, we now state the following lemma,
which also relies on the feature-magnitude bounds established therein.

###### Lemma F.3 (Characterizations of Lambda).

Given $`\mathbf{Z}^{L_{k},1}`$ with
$`\{\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathbf{k}}\}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathcal{I}^{L_{k},1}}`$,
then

1.  1.
    for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for activated neuron
    $`r\in\mathfrak{A}_{j}`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Lambda_{5,j,r}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{2}\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{pred},\ell^{\prime}}V_{j,r}(g_{\ell^{\prime}})+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{L_{k}}\mathbf{Attn}_{\mathsf{ans},1\to\mathsf{ans},\ell^{\prime}-1}V_{j,r}(y_{\ell^{\prime}-1})\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |
2.  2.
    for $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for any non-activated neuron
    $`r\notin\mathfrak{A}_{j}`$ we have

    |     |                                                            |     |
    |-----|------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
    |     | $`\displaystyle\Big|\Lambda_{5,j,r}\Big|\leq{O}(\delta).`$ |     |
3.  3.
    for $`j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for any $`r\in[m]`$, we have

    |  |  |  |
    |----|----|----|
    |  | $`\displaystyle\Big|\Lambda_{5,j,r}\Big|\leq\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0}).`$ |  |

###### Lemma F.4.

Given $`j\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})`$, for
$`r\in\mathfrak{A}_{j}\setminus\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}`$, we have
$`\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}\big(\Lambda_{5,j,r}\big)=0`$.

We are now ready to derive the gradients of the attention layer,
building on
[Lemmas F.1](#A6.Thmlemma1 "Lemma F.1 (Gradients of 𝐐_{4,3}). ‣ F.1.1 Gradient Computations ‣ F.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix F Recursive Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and [F.2](#A6.Thmlemma2 "Lemma F.2 (Gradients of 𝐐_{4,4}). ‣ F.1.1 Gradient Computations ‣ F.1 Preliminaries ‣ Appendix F Recursive Learning the Attention Layer: Symmetry Case ‣ Transformers Provably Learn Chain-of-Thought Reasoning with Length Generalization")
and the properties established above.

###### Lemma F.5 (Refined expression for the gradient of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$).

Given $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ from the previous stage and
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$, letting
$`j_{2}=\tau(g_{2}(y_{1}))`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{2})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},1}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(g_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(g_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s,\tau(x_{0})=s^{\prime}}\Bigg].`$ |  |

###### Lemma F.6 (Refined expression for the gradient of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$).

Given $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ from the previous stage and
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, for the diagonal entry
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}]_{s,s}`$ of the block $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$, letting
$`j_{2}=\tau(g_{2}(y_{1}))`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(y_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(y_{1})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Moreover, for the off-diagonal entries
$`[\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}]_{s,s^{\prime}}`$ with $`s\neq s^{\prime}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot\Big(V_{j_{2},r}(y_{0})-\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j\neq j_{2}\in\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j,r})\cdot\Big(V_{j,r}(y_{0})-\Lambda_{5,j,r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\pm\sum_{j\notin\tau(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbf{logit}_{5,j}\widetilde{O}(\sigma^{q}_{0})\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\tau(x_{1})=s,\tau(x_{0})=s^{\prime}}\Bigg].`$ |  |

Following the above calculations, we can further obtain the gradient
summation of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$ and $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$ as follows:

###### Lemma F.7 (Gradient sum of $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}`$ and $`\mathbf{Q}_{4,4}`$).

Given $`F^{(T_{k-1})}`$ from the previous stage and
$`s\in\tau(\mathcal{X})`$, letting $`j_{2}=\tau(g_{2}(y_{1}))`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s}+\Big[-\nabla_{\mathbf{Q}_{4,3}}\mathsf{Loss}^{L_{k},2}_{F^{(T_{k-1})},5}\Big]_{s,s}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg((1-\mathbf{logit}_{5,j_{2}})\cdot\Big(\sum_{r\in\widehat{\mathfrak{A}}_{j_{2}}}\mathbf{sReLU}^{\prime}(\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r})\cdot`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\Big(-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},0}\cdot V_{j_{2},r}(y_{0})-\sum_{\ell\neq 2}\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},\ell}\cdot V_{j_{2},r}(g_{\ell})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\big(1-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{ans},1}-\mathbf{Attn}_{{\mathsf{ans},1}\rightarrow\mathsf{pred},2}\big)\Lambda_{5,j_{2},r}\pm\widetilde{O}(\sigma_{0})\Big)\pm\widetilde{O}(\delta^{q})\Big)`$ |  |
````
